## Compiled-backend codegen bugs in HTTP handler paths (2026-06-29)

- ✅ **Publish-path Perceus UAF — FIXED (2026-06-29): it was a monomorphization gap, not a Perceus/borrow bug.** `Forgepm.Api.Upload.get_file` crashed at `"--" ++ boundary` (UBSan: `march_string_concat`). The real cause: the request header is read via a generic helper `get_req_header(conn, name) = lookup(conn.hdrs, name)` whose row-polymorphic `conn` gives the projection `conn.hdrs` a fresh type var; monomorphising `conn → Conn` left `lookup`'s value type abstract, so `Option('V)` was emitted **Boxed** while the concrete caller read it as a **Niche** — the Some-box pointer was dereferenced as the payload String (SIGSEGV). The "boundary string freed" framing was wrong; `boundary`'s argument was a heap `Some` box read as a string. Fix in `lib/tir/mono.ml`: `refine_field_types` (resolve record-field projections against the now-concrete record before call specialisation) + a `TRecord` case in `match_ty`. The three committed repros (`specs/repros/perceus_tuple_proj_rc_{min,full,control_ok}.march`) pass compiled, stable across 100 runs; regression test in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` ("record-field poly projection: Option niche/box repr consistent"). See `specs/2026-06-29-perceus-tuple-projection-rc-bug.md`. **789 stdlib / 372 compiler / 224 eval / 322 codegen pass.**
- [ ] **Home-route empty render** — compiled `GET /` returns a 200 with an EMPTY body (`octet-stream`); isolated to `list_popular(8)` / `home_page(...)`. `/packages` (also DB+HTML) and `registry_stats()` (in `forge test`) are fine. Likely the same monomorphization class as the publish-path fix above (a generic projection feeding a generic callee) — re-test against a fresh compiled forgepm binary before assuming it still reproduces.

## P0 — Blocking / Active

- [ ] **Interpreter/compiled divergence: `ERecordUpdate` on a missing field (eval.ml vs llvm_emit.ml)** — found during Wave 1's B5 fix (`0d1a829e`, `specs/analysis/2026-07-01-pipeline-deep-review.md`). Compiled mode now panics via `march_record_update_dyn` when an `{ base with field: v }` update names a field absent from the base record's actual shape (the typechecker can't catch this against an erased/generic base). The tree-walking interpreter's `ERecordUpdate` (`lib/eval/eval.ml`) still silently *appends* the unknown field instead of erroring — so the same source program panics compiled and succeeds (with a fabricated field) interpreted. Reconcile by making `eval.ml`'s `ERecordUpdate` fail loudly on an unknown field name, matching the compiled contract (the safer of the two).
- [ ] **Monomorphization limit reached compiling a self-recursive nested closure (2026-07-02)** — found during Wave 2 Task 2 (test-harness loud-skip hardening, `specs/plans/2026-07-02-wave2-testing-infra.md`) when a previously-vacuous `if compile_rc <> 0 then ()` skip in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`'s `test_compiled_recursive_closure_capture` was converted to a loud failure and immediately caught a real, independently-reproducible compiler crash: `march --compile` on a trivial nested closure that recurses via a captured outer-fn parameter —
  ```march
  mod RcClosure do
    fn f(m) do
      fn go(mi, acc) do
        if mi >= m do mi else go(mi + 1, acc + 1) end
      end
      go(1, 0)
    end
    fn main() do println(int_to_string(f(11))) end
  end
  ```
  — dies with `Fatal error: exception Failure("Monomorphization limit reached: function 'List.fold_left' has more than 512 specializations. This usually indicates polymorphic recursion...")`, exit code 2 (uncaught OCaml exception). Reproduces even with the module's only diagnostic-triggering call (`process_exit`, which additionally needs `needs IO.Process` — a separate, harmless, non-blocking hint/warning) replaced by `println`, so the crash is intrinsic to the nested-closure-capture shape itself, not to the capability check. `go` is not polymorphic-recursive in the source (`Int -> Int -> Int` throughout) and never mentions `List.fold_left`, so `lib/tir/mono.ml`'s specialization-count blowup for `List.fold_left` must come from how this shape lowers/defuns before monomorphization — needs investigation starting from `lib/tir/mono.ml`'s worklist/`done_set` dedup for closure-captured self-recursive local fns (compare against the already-fixed `ECallPtr` self-recursive-return-type case this same test targets, `specs/2026-0?-...` — the test's *original* regression, which the interpreter/compiled parity check below the crash was written to guard). Test converted to a documented `Alcotest.skip` (matching on the exact "Monomorphization limit reached" message so any OTHER compile failure at that site still fails loudly) rather than a silent no-op, pending a fix.

### Recently fixed
- ✅ **Final-review fixes: `in_tail` blind spot in mutual-TCO group formation + REPL desugar-diagnostic rendering (2026-07-02)** — Three items from a final whole-branch review pass over Wave 1. **(1)** `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` `has_non_tail_group_call`'s dec-chain-wrapper arm (`ELet (tmp, EApp (f, _), dec-chain-returning-tmp)`, the Perceus post-call-DecRC wrapper recognizer shared with `tail_calls_in`) matched at ANY position without checking `in_tail`, always treating the wrapped call as tail regardless of where it actually sat. A group member that tail-calls its partner (forming a legitimate SCC edge) but ALSO non-tail-calls the same partner elsewhere (Perceus-wrapped, result bound and fed into further computation) would pass `find_mutual_tco_groups`'s "no non-tail call" gate — the mutual-TCO back-edge emitted for that pair then strands the continuation in a dead block. Fixed by checking `List.mem f.Tir.v_name group && not in_tail` in that arm too, mirroring the bare-`EApp` arm above. New regression test `test/test_codegen.ml` "final-review: non-tail dec-chain-wrapped group call rejected" (`mutual_tco_codegen` suite): `build_loop`/`consume_loop` where one branch is a genuine tail call (forms the SCC edge) and a sibling branch dec-chain-wraps the same call but binds the result to `r` and returns `r + 1` (non-tail) — confirmed RED pre-fix (`mutual_loop` wrongly formed) / GREEN post-fix (rejected), by temporarily reverting the one-line guard. **(2)** `lib/repl/repl.ml`: `run_simple`'s `ReplExpr`/`ReplLetQ` branches call `March_desugar.Desugar.desugar_expr` outside any local `try/with`, so a desugar-time diagnostic (e.g. the B6 pipe-into-match check, which raises `March_errors.Errors.ParseError`) escaped to the loop's outermost catch-all and printed a bare `internal error: March_errors.Errors.ParseError(...)` instead of the span-rendered diagnostic the batch driver (`bin/main.ml`) shows for the same error. Added a `last_src` ref (set alongside the existing per-form `src` binding) and a `ParseError` case in the outer catch-all that calls `March_errors.Errors.render_parse_error` the same way `bin/main.ml`'s `fmt_file`/compile paths do. New subprocess-based test `test/test_eval.ml` "final-review: renders desugar ParseError, not internal error" (`repl integration` suite) pipes the repro through the real `march repl` binary (the lightweight `repl_eval_exprs` helper used by the other REPL tests calls `desugar_expr` directly and would not reproduce the escape) — confirmed RED/GREEN the same way. **(3)** Stale comment in `test/imports/record_native/rn_entry.march:9` said the erased-shape `EUpdate` fallback "routes through march_record_put"; corrected to `march_record_update_dyn` (the actual B5 fix). **383 compiler / 227 eval (+1) / 335 codegen (+1) / 788 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march pass. RC benchmarks (tree_transform=104857600, list_ops=333333666666, binary_trees standard output, mutual_recursion even/odd N=10M + state machine + collatz) all compiled+run correctly; `bench/mutual_recursion.march`'s legitimate mutual groups confirmed still forming (`mutual_loop` present in `--emit-llvm` output) — the `in_tail` fix does not reject genuine mutual tail recursion.**
- ✅ **Three P0 items from the 2026-07-01 pipeline deep review (2026-07-01).** **(1) Differential oracle skipped compiled crashes** (`test/test_properties.ml` `oracle_check`): a signal-killed compiled binary (exit ≥ 128 via `sh`: SIGABRT=134, SIGSEGV=139) when the interpreter succeeded is now a FAILURE with the signal number in the error output, not a skip; clean nonzero error exits (and `timeout`'s 124) still skip. **(2) Perceus dual-position owned+borrowed double-consumption** (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`, EApp + ECallPtr-extern `post_dec_vars` sites): a var passed at both an owned and a borrowed position of the same call, dead afterwards, was consumed twice (owned transfer + borrowed post-call dec) against the caller's single reference — `both(s, s, 1)` with `both(a:own, b:borrow, n)` aborted exit 134 ("RC underflow") compiled while the interpreter was fine. Normal calls now add one balancing `EIncRC` and keep the post-dec; self calls drop the post-dec (TCO makes the trailing dec dead code — an inc would leak per iteration). **(3) FBIP `same_arity` conflated type-parameter count with constructor field count** (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`): the dead-binding FBIP path fed a binding's raw declared type, so a dead 1-field cell of a multi-param type was reused for a wider constructor — heap overflow past the cell (observed as the compiled binary printing the wrong match arm). `add_scrutinee_free_for`'s arity encoding now carries an unforgeable `$fbip$` marker and `same_arity` accepts only marked encodings; `Borrow.has_matching_alloc` audited (name-prefix ownership gate only — no arity use, no change needed). Compiled Slow regression tests (parity + exit 0) in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`; `fbip_p8` unit tests updated + `same_arity_raw_type_refused` added in `test/test_codegen.ml`. **372 compiler / 224 eval / 323 codegen / 790 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 36 properties pass; tree_transform/list_ops/binary_trees outputs and timings unchanged.**
- ✅ **MARCH_SANITIZE binaries aborted at process exit on macOS arm64 (2026-07-01)** — ASAN-linked binaries printed correct output then died with SIGTRAP/exit 133: `AddressSanitizer failed to deallocate 0x10000 bytes ... (error code: 22)` + `CHECK failed: sanitizer_common.cpp:81 "unable to unmmap"`. Root cause (lldb backtrace: `_pthread_exit → _pthread_tsd_cleanup → AsanThread::Destroy → UnsetAlternateSignalStack → UnmapOrDie`): the scheduler's `setup_alt_stack()` (`runtime/march_scheduler.c`) installed a **malloc'd** 64 KiB per-thread `sigaltstack`, replacing the mmap'd altstack ASAN installs on every thread; at thread exit ASAN blindly `munmap()`s whatever altstack is current — our non-page-aligned malloc'd buffer → EINVAL → CHECK-abort. Fix: under ASAN (`__has_feature(address_sanitizer)` / `__SANITIZE_ADDRESS__`) `setup_alt_stack()` is a no-op — ASAN's own altstack is SA_ONSTACK-capable and serves the lazy stack-growth SIGSEGV handler (verified: 2000-deep non-tail recursion grows the 4 KiB initial stack and exits 0 under ASAN). Non-sanitized builds unchanged (preprocessed out). Regression test (Slow, compiled): "MARCH_SANITIZE binary exits 0" in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` — red without the fix (exit 133), green with it. Also fixed a fresh-checkout build failure: `lib/ed25519/dune` foreign stubs needed `(extra_deps %{project_root}/runtime/tweetnacl.h)` (the header was never copied into `_build`; main repo built only via a stale `_build`). **789 stdlib / 372 compiler / 224 eval / 322 codegen / 53 stdlib_march pass.**
- ✅ **Compiled-backend RC/codegen bugs corrupting `(String, TomlValue)` pair lists — Toml `get_str` returned wrong values (2026-06-28)** — `Toml.parse` of a `[section]` with 4+ keys then `get_str` returned a sibling key's NAME (or crashed with OOM / RC underflow) **only when compiled**; the interpreter was always correct, so this blocked compiled `forge.toml` parsing. Three independent compiler bugs combined to corrupt the pair list, all fixed: **(1) TCO mis-applied to non-tail self-calls** (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`) — the loop back-edge fired for EVERY self `EApp` in a TCO'd function, including a non-tail one inside `Cons(x, f(t))` (an ELet rhs in ANF), silently dropping the surrounding construction. Added `ctx.tco_in_tail` (set at TCO body entry, cleared around ELet rhs / ESeq prefix) and gated the back-edge on it. **(2) Niche/newtype scrutinee double-free** (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`) — for niche- (`Some(x)≡x`) / newtype- (`S(x)≡x`) represented scrutinees the payload branch var IS the scrutinee object, so the separate `add_scrutinee_free_for` dec double-freed it (toml `get_str`'s `Some(TStr(s))`). Skip the scrutinee free when `Repr.repr_of_ty` is `Newtype`/`Niche`. **(3) Borrow inference over-owned linear-search helpers** (`lib/tir/borrow.ml`) — `owned_in` treated a `let p = name` alias (Lower's destructured-field binding, often behind sibling/join-point bindings) as an *owning* use, so `table_get`/`tget` were inferred `pairs:own` and consumed/freed the list spine + elements that `set_nested`/`table_has` then reused. Fixed `owned_in`'s `ELet` to flow through an alias (`owned_in v body`) instead of marking the source owned. Exposed a 4th issue (escaping field of a borrowed-derived tuple not inc'd; dead tuple-field dec corrupting a borrowed list) fixed in `perceus.ml` by treating `TTuple`/`TRecord` scrutinees as borrowed (they are `needs_rc=false`, so fields belong to the never-freed aggregate). Regression tests: compiled guard `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` "Toml [section] with 4 keys…" (interpreter tests can't catch a compiled-only bug); eval-level "Section with many keys (RC regression)" in `test/stdlib/test_toml.march`. Also fixed a stale `test/stdlib/test_dataframe.march` (commit e1e5b589's `IntCol`→`NativeIntArr` migration missed it — `typed_array_from_list`→`native_int_arr_from_list`, `typed_array_get`→`native_int_arr_get`) that blocked `forge test`. **320 codegen / 224 eval / 372 compiler / 787 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march pass; benchmarks leak-neutral.**

- ✅ **Refinement types: `@invariant` syntax + schema persist — Gap #2 (2026-06-25)** — `lib/lexer/lexer.mll`: `"invariant" -> INVARIANT` keyword. `lib/parser/parser.mly`: `%token INVARIANT`, two new `decl` productions (with/without additional `fn_attrs`) that extract the invariant expression and attach it to `DActor`'s `actor_def`; `%start <March_ast.Ast.expr> expr_eof` and `expr_eof: e = expr; EOF { e }` for round-trip re-parsing. `lib/ast/ast.ml`: `actor_invariant : expr option` field added to `actor_def` (sibling to `actor_compat`; default `None` at all existing construction sites). `bin/main.ml`: `pred_to_string` serializer (operators via `EApp` pattern matching, field projections, measure calls); `collect_actor_invariant` walker; schema emit writes `"invariant": %S,` between `"compat"` and `"state_fields"` when attribute is present; `normalize_inv_receiver` rewrites `EField` receiver vars to the canonical binder before `TyRefine` injection. `forge/lib/schema_diff.ml`: `invariant : string option` field on `actor_schema`; robust scanner uses first/last-quote index extraction (not `split_on_char '"'`) so `%S`-escaped chars in predicates don't break parsing. **321 compiler tests pass.** Spec: `specs/2026-06-25-refinement-verified-state-migration-design.md §3`.

- ✅ **`lib/refinecheck/return_infer.ml` — Z3 return-refinement inference (2026-06-25)** — New `March_refinecheck.Return_infer` module: given a function with Int-refined parameters, probes 6 sign candidates (`r > 0`, `r >= 0`, `r >= 1`, `r != 0`, `r < 0`, `r <= -1`) for the return value via Z3. Single-pass: (1) collects refined params → `(decls, assumptions)`; (2) walks block body for let-binding equalities; (3) reflects return expression as SMT term; (4) calls `Refine.discharge` for each candidate. Result type `inferred_return = { fn_name; verified_preds }`. Public API: `infer_module ?root m`. If Z3 is absent, returns `[]` (no false positives). 6 new tests in `return_refine_infer` suite. **359 compiler tests pass.**
- ✅ **`lib/refinecheck/cap_infer.ml` — standalone capability-inference hint pass (2026-06-25)** — New `March_refinecheck.Cap_infer` module walks function bodies and `DLet` bindings after typecheck, maps direct calls to capability-requiring builtins (`cap_table`, ~90 entries mirroring `builtin_cap_table` in typecheck.ml) against the module's declared `needs`, and emits `Err.hint` (not warning/error) at the exact call site. Deduplicates per-capability-per-module to suppress repeated hints. Respects the IO hierarchy: `needs IO` covers all `IO.*` children (`cap_subsumes`). Recurses into `DMod` children with their own `DNeeds` scope. Wired into `bin/main.ml` at both `--check` and `--compile` call sites (immediately after `Refine_check.check_module`). `march_refinecheck` added to `march_test_compiler` deps in `test/dune`. `has_hints`/`has_hint_with` helpers added to `test/test_helpers.ml`. 7 new `cap_infer` tests in `test/test_compiler.ml`; all 328 compiler tests pass.
- ✅ **Perceus RC use-after-free on cross-module opaque types** — a value whose concrete type isn't propagated across a module boundary (e.g. `let gate = Gate.cast(...)` where `Gate` is an opaque type from another module) keeps an unresolved type-var `'_NNNN` in monomorphic TIR. `needs_rc (TVar _)` returned `false` ("skip RC"), so Perceus never emitted an `EIncRC` for such a heap value; when the binding was consumed twice (e.g. two `Gate.get_change(gate, …)` calls) the first consume freed the box and the second double-freed it (`RC underflow in march_decrc_freed`, bastion `Gate.cast` test). Fix: `needs_rc (TVar _) = true` (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`) — `llvm_ty (TVar _) = "ptr"`, so the value is a heap pointer and must be RC-managed; safe for genuine scalars because RC ops are guarded by `if ty = "ptr"` in llvm_emit and `IS_HEAP_PTR` in the runtime. Mirrors the earlier `TFn` fix. Regression test: `perceus` group "incrc for unresolved-tvar value used twice (cross-module opaque)" in `test/test_march.ml`.

## P1 — High Impact / Near-Term

### Runtime / stdlib

- [ ] **Signal-handler API (`Signal.watch`) — spec'd 2026-07-02, not implemented.** User-facing
  SIGTERM/SIGINT/HUP/USR1/USR2 watchers with deferred green-thread dispatch (never in signal
  context), composing with the existing internal handlers (`g_http_shutdown` in march_http.c,
  `shutdown_requested` in eval.ml) via suppress-default + second-delivery escape hatch. Blocks
  graceful drain in Bastion apps (forgepm's operations spec parks it on exactly this). Full
  design incl. per-layer implementation plan and test plan: `specs/2026-07-02-signal-handler-api.md`.

- ✅ **Bastion scoped island CSS and static file serving** — `Static` module (bastion): ETag (mtime+size), smart cache-control (immutable for 8-char-hash fingerprinted files, 1h otherwise), `Static.path/1` (manifest lookup with `/static/` fallback), `Static.css_tag/1`, `Static.js_tag/1`, `Static.mime_type/1` (20 MIME types), `Static.looks_fingerprinted/1`. New `IslandCss` module (bastion): `scope_css/2` (CSS selector prefixing with island data-attribute), `attribute_for/1`, `attr_name/1`, `to_kebab/1` (PascalCase→kebab-case). `Islands.wrap_with_css/5`, `Islands.wrap_eager_with_css/4` in stdlib (embeds scoped CSS in `data-march-island-css` attribute). `IslandAssets` enhanced: manifest.json endpoint, content-hash WASM cache (immutable/60s), `wasm_url/2`. `march-islands.js` updated: `_injectScopedCss` injects per-island `<style>` tags on hydration (deduplicated by module name). 48 tests in `bastion/test/bastion_test.march`.
- ✅ **WASM Tier 4 (browser target) — pipeline validated** — `wasm32-unknown-unknown` codegen produces working WASM binaries. Validated end-to-end: `march_island_render`, `march_island_update`, `march_island_init`, `march_alloc_export`, `march_dealloc`, `march_island_render_html`, `march_island_string_length`, `march_island_string_data` all export correctly and pass JS `WebAssembly.instantiate` tests. Fixed 4 compiler bugs: (1) `find_fn` island export matching was catching `Vault.update` instead of user's `update` — narrowed to user module prefix; (2) vault builtins (`vault_new/get/set/update/etc.`) missing from `is_builtin_fn`/`mangle_extern`/preamble — causing broken `%vault_update.addr` IR; (3) `ECallPtr` to known builtins wasn't short-circuited to direct calls; (4) `build_wasm.sh` pointed to native `march_runtime.c` instead of `march_runtime_wasm.c`. Added `memset/memcpy/memmove` stubs and vault WASM stubs (panic) to `march_runtime_wasm.c`. **Remaining**: Integrate into `Islands.loadWasmModule` stub in `march_islands.js`; `march_island_render_html` only works for IOList-returning renders (String-returning renders need `march_island_render` + `march_island_string_*`).
- ✅ **Bastion.Cache + Bastion.Depot** — HTTP middleware layer in `stdlib/bastion.march`. `Bastion.Cache`: `etag/1` (FNV-1a ETag + 304 Not Modified short-circuit), `cached/4` (full-response cache via Vault with TTL), `fragment/3` (IOList fragment cache with TTL), `invalidate/2`, `invalidate_prefix/2` (O(n) prefix scan via `Vault.keys`), `cache_control/2`, `no_cache/1`, `public_cache/2`. `Bastion.Depot`: `with_pool/2` (attaches DB pool to conn assigns under `"db"`). Also added `Vault.keys/1` and `HttpServer.get_resp_header/2`. 16 tests.
- ✅ **Bastion web framework layer** — Full framework layer on top of `HttpServer`. Router: `Router.new`, `Router.get/post/put/delete/patch`, `Router.action` (ActionResult-based), `Router.scope`, `Router.fallback`, `Router.dispatch`; pattern params via `:id`. Controller: `ActionResult = Ok(Conn) | Error(Int, String)`, `Controller.action`, `Controller.render`, `Controller.render_iolist`, `Controller.json`, `Controller.text`, `Controller.redirect`. FallbackController: `call`, `call_json`, `new`. Middleware: `compose`, `pipeline`, `logger`, `request_id`, `cors`, `content_type`, `body_parser`. TypedMiddleware: session (cookie-backed, signed HMAC-SHA256), JWT auth, typed assigns, `require_auth`. Request/Response/ConnStates helpers. ErrorView. Static file server with ETag, fingerprint caching, manifest. IslandCss: selector scoping, kebab-case transform. IslandAssets: island runtime JS/WASM serving, manifest, cache control. Islands stdlib additions: `wrap_with_css`, `wrap_eager_with_css`. 143 tests in `bastion/test/bastion_test.march`.
- ✅ **Bastion configuration system** — `Env` stdlib module (`Env.get`, `Env.require`, `Env.get_int`, `Env.get_bool`, `Env.is_set`, `Env.require_int`). `Config` stdlib module backed by a global Vault table: 2-level `Config.get`/`Config.put`, 3-level `Config.get_in`/`Config.put_in`, `Config.from_env`/`Config.from_env_int`/`Config.from_env_bool` for env-var loading, `Config.validate`/`Config.validate_in` with predicate, `Config.env`/`Config.is_dev`/`Config.is_test`/`Config.is_prod` (reads `MARCH_ENV`), `Config.put_endpoint`/`Config.endpoint_port`/`Config.endpoint_host`/`Config.secret_key_base` for web server config, `Config.new_store` for isolated named stores. - ✅ **`BastionDev` stdlib module** — Live-reload SSE injection, request timing middleware, Phoenix LiveDashboard-inspired dev dashboard at `/_bastion`, and rich error overlay. `BastionDev.request_timer/finish_timer/server_timing/conn_inspector` middleware plugs. `BastionDev.inject_live_reload` injects SSE script before `</body>`. `BastionDev.live_reload_handler` serves `/_bastion/live_reload`. `BastionDev.dashboard_html/dashboard_handler` renders full metrics dashboard (total requests, error count, avg duration, WS connections, vault table sizes, color-coded request log, error log with expandable traces, route registry). `BastionDev.error_overlay_html/error_overlay` renders Phoenix-style rich 500 pages with stack trace and conn state. Stat types: `RequestEntry`, `ErrorEntry`, `RouteEntry`, `VaultStat`, `DevStats`. 66 tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_dev.march`.
- ✅ **Signed/encrypted cookies with key rotation** — `BastionCookies.put_signed/get_signed` and `put_encrypted/get_encrypted`. Keys derived via PBKDF2-SHA256 from `secret_key_base` with cookie-name context. HMAC-SHA256 signing; authenticated encryption with integrity verification. `delete/2` sets Max-Age=0. Full opts support (max_age, secure, http_only, same_site, path). `stdlib/bastion_cookies.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_cookies.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/cookies.md`.
- ✅ **Reversible routing / compile-time route verification** — `BastionRoutes` registry backed by Vault. `register/4` (method, pattern, helper_name, arity), `all/0`, `find_by_name/1`, `path/3` (fills `:param` slots, appends `?k=v` query strings with percent-encoding), `static_path/1`, `path1/2`, `path2/3`. `stdlib/bastion_routes.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_routes.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/route-verification.md`.

Wildcard topic matching (`*`). Vault-backed subscription and broadcast log. `stdlib/bastion_pubsub.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_pubsub.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/pubsub.md`.

Isolated Vault namespace per sandbox (`vault_put/3`, `vault_get/2`, `vault_delete/2`). `build_conn/3`, `build_conn_full/5`. Global registry in Vault tracks active sandboxes. `stdlib/bastion_test_sandbox.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_test_sandbox.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/test-sandbox.md`.
- ✅ **Idempotency keys as middleware** — `BastionIdempotency`: `IdempotencyState = InProgress(Int) | Completed(Int, List, String, Int)`. `get_key/1` (reads `Idempotency-Key` header), `lookup/3`, `mark_in_progress/3`, `complete_entry/6`, `replay_response/2`, `invalidate/3`, `protect/3` (handler fn wrapper). Cache hit replays stored response; InProgress returns 409 with `Retry-After: 5`; 5xx not cached. `X-Idempotent-Replayed` header. `stdlib/bastion_idempotency.march`, tests in `test/stdlib/test_bastion_idempotency.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/idempotency.md`.
- ✅ **Structured logging with request correlation IDs** — `CorrelationId.assign/1` middleware reads or generates a UUID v4 as the request correlation ID, stores it in `conn.assigns["request_id"]`, echoes it in the `X-Request-ID` response header, and injects it into the Logger context via `Logger.with_context` so all subsequent log calls in the request process include `request_id` automatically — no manual threading. `CorrelationId.request_id/1` accessor, `CorrelationId.clear/0` to reset Logger context. `uuid_v4` builtin added to eval (RFC 4122 v4, version nibble=4, variant=10xx). 15 tests in `test/stdlib/test_correlation.march`. Spec: `specs/bastion/logging.md`.
- ✅ **`Html` stdlib module** — `Html.escape`, `Html.escape_attr`, `Html.Safe` type, `Html.raw`, `Html.safe_to_string`, `Html.tag`, `Html.render_partial`, `Html.render_collection`, `Html.layout`, `Html.content_hash`, `Html.list`, `Html.join`. Auto-escaping `~H` sigil via `html_auto_escape` builtin (handles `Html.Safe` and IOList without double-escaping). `IOList.hash` (FNV-1a 64-bit, for ETag generation). `IOList` is the canonical rendered HTML fragment type: cacheable in Vault, re-embeddable in `~H` without escaping. 52 tests in `test/stdlib/test_html.march`, 19 tests in `test/stdlib/test_sigil.march`. Example: `examples/templates.march`.
- ✅ **`CSRF` stdlib middleware module** — `stdlib/csrf.march`. `CSRF.generate_token()` (CSPRNG, 32 bytes, base64); `CSRF.ensure_token(conn)` idempotent per-session token assignment; `CSRF.token(conn)` accessor; `CSRF.tag(conn)` IOList hidden input; `CSRF.tag_string(conn)` String variant; `CSRF.validate(conn)` form-body token comparison; `CSRF.protect(conn)` middleware (GET/HEAD/OPTIONS + JSON pass, POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE validated, 403 on mismatch); `CSRF.skip(conn)` webhook bypass. `~H` desugar: `inject_csrf_tokens` in `lib/desugar/desugar.ml` splits `<form method="post/put/patch/delete">` string literals at the form's `>` and injects `CSRF.tag_string(conn)` — automatic hidden-field injection in all `~H` templates. 32 tests in `test/stdlib/test_csrf.march`.
- ✅ **`Session` stdlib middleware module** — `stdlib/session.march`: cookie-backed (`:cookie`) and Vault-backed (`:vault`) session middleware. `:cookie` stores full session data in a signed browser cookie; `:vault` stores only session ID in cookie with data in `Vault`. Signing: HMAC-SHA256 via `pbkdf2_sha256` key derivation from `Config.secret_key_base` + per-cookie-name salt; cookie format `base64(payload).base64(hmac)`; tampered cookies silently start fresh sessions. Public API: `Session.load(conn, backend)` middleware plug; `Session.save(conn)` / `Session.save_with_opts(conn, opts)` (supports `max_age`, `secure`, `same_site`, `path`, `HttpOnly`); `Session.get/put/delete/clear/all`; `Session.put_flash/get_flash` one-request flash messages (sweep on `save`, unread flash survives). Session data serialised as `key\x1Fval\x1Ekey\x1Fval` strings for storage in Conn string assigns. 49 tests in `test/stdlib/test_session.march`.

### Tooling: Forge Build Tool

- ✅ **Shell completions + external subcommands** — `forge completions bash|zsh|fish` prints a ready-to-source completion script; `forge-<cmd>` binaries on PATH are exec'd when `<cmd>` is not a built-in (built-ins always win). `forge/lib/cli_ext.ml` (pure `completion_script`/`external_subcommand`, 6 unit tests). Item #5 of `specs/forge_tooling_roadmap.md`.
- ✅ **`forge watch`** — reruns build/test/run on source changes (polls `lib/ src/ test/ config/` + `forge.toml`); never exits on failure. `forge/lib/watcher.ml` (pure `changed`, 5 unit tests) + `cmd_watch.ml`. Item #1 of `specs/forge_tooling_roadmap.md`.
- ✅ **`forge build --target js` / `forge run --compiled --target js` (2026-06-22)** — `forge build --target js` compiles the project entry to an ES module (`.mjs`) under `.march/build/{debug,release}/<name>.mjs`; the compiler auto-copies `march_runtime.mjs` + `march_dom.mjs` alongside it. `forge run --compiled --target js` builds the `.mjs` and runs it with `node`. All other targets (`native`, `wasm*`) unchanged — `?target` defaults to `None` so existing call-sites (`forge test`, `forge release`, `forge watch`) are unaffected. `forge/bin/main.ml` + `forge/lib/cmd_build.ml` + `forge/lib/cmd_run.ml`.
- ✅ **`forge bench`** — compiles + runs `bench/*.march` at `--opt 2`, reports wall-clock times (table or `--json`), name-substring filter. `forge/lib/cmd_bench.ml` (pure `bench_name`/`matches`/`format_results`, 4 unit tests). Item #2 of the tooling roadmap.
- ✅ **`forge version` / `forge release`** — bump `[package].version` (patch/minor/major or explicit), `--tag` commits + tags `vX.Y.Z`; `release` gates clean→build→test→bump+tag. `forge/lib/versioning.ml` (pure `bump`/`rewrite_version_line`, 5 unit tests) + `cmd_version.ml`/`cmd_release.ml`. Item #3 of the tooling roadmap (fmt --check + lint CI already existed).
- ✅ **`forge licenses` + `--frozen`** — `forge licenses [--json] [--strict]` lists deps + declared license; `forge build --frozen/--locked` errors on lockfile drift instead of re-resolving. `forge/lib/cmd_licenses.ml` (pure `format`/`any_missing`/`frozen_error`, 3 unit tests). Item #4 of the tooling roadmap.

- ✅ **Implement `forge`** — Implemented as `forge/` package. Commands: `forge new`, `forge build`, `forge run`, `forge test`, `forge format`, `forge interactive`/`i`, `forge deps`, `forge clean`, `forge search`. Template scaffolding generates valid March code (PascalCase module names, `do/end` fn bodies, `println` builtin). 15 tests in `forge/test/test_forge.ml`.
- ✅ **`forge search` — Hoogle-style search** — `lib/search/search.ml` (new `march_search` library): Levenshtein fuzzy name search, type-signature component matching, doc-keyword search, combined search (AND-semantics). JSON index cache at `.march/search-index.json`. `forge/lib/cmd_search.ml` wires up cmdliner with `--type`, `--doc`, `--limit`, `--json`, `--rebuild` flags. 25 tests in `test/test_search.ml` (levenshtein, name search, type search, doc search, combined, JSON roundtrip, stdlib integration).
- ✅ **`forge bastion` command group + scaffold fixes** — *(Moved to bastion repo)* `forge bastion new/server/routes/gen` commands (`cmd_bastion_new.ml`, `cmd_bastion_server.ml`, `cmd_bastion_routes.ml`, `cmd_bastion_gen.ml`, `gen_auth.ml`, `scaffold_bastion.ml`, `cmd_assets.ml`) removed from `forge/` and ported to the bastion repo. `forge bastion` subcommands now ship alongside the framework they scaffold.
- ✅ **Bastion adversarial test suite** — 346 adversarial tests across 7 files in `examples/bastion_tests/`: routing (42 tests — path params, method dispatch, scope prefixes, fallbacks, edge cases), caching (37 — ETags, Cache-Control, Vault fragment cache, CDN headers), sessions (35 — cookie/vault backends, flash, TTL, concurrent), CSRF (44 — token generation, form injection, bypass, replay), dev-dashboard (64 — BastionDev middleware, SSE live-reload, System stats, error overlay), HTML (54 — escaping, sigil injection, IOList, partial rendering), islands (70 — WASM loading, hydration strategies, CSS scoping, cross-island messaging). Fixed `stdlib/islands.march` bug: `_island_runtime_script` embedded the full JS blob instead of serving from `/_bastion/islands.js`. `RESULTS.md` documents all 346 findings.
- ✅ **Dotted import paths** — `import Mod.Sub`, `import Mod.Sub.{foo, bar}`, `import Mod.Sub, only: [foo]`, `import Mod.Sub, except: [foo]` all supported. `import_decl` in `lib/parser/parser.mly` refactored from 4 flat rules using `upper_name` to a single rule with a new right-recursive `import_path_tail` nonterminal (modeled after `use_path_tail`). Conflict-free LALR(1): DOT+LBRACE→brace selector, DOT+UPPER_IDENT→recurse, COMMA→only/except filter, empty→UseAll. 3 new parse tests.
- ✅ **Dotted names in `impl` and `when` constraints** — `impl Conduit.Storage(T)` and `when Conduit.Storage(s)` now parse: both `impl_decl` and `constraint_expr` in the parser accept `upper_dot_path`, joining segments with "." into the `impl_iface.txt`. Typecheck: `prebind_mod_members` now also pre-registers nested module interfaces as `"Mod.Iface"` qualified names; `DUse UseAll` handler now imports interface names (not just vars) from the module prefix into scope, enabling `import Conduit` + `impl Storage(T)` to resolve correctly. 1 new parse test (1311 total).
- ✅ **Multi-file / project-aware compilation** — Scaffolded Bastion apps now compile end-to-end with `forge build`. Three fixes in `bin/main.ml`: (1) `module_name_to_filename` extended to handle dotted names — `MyApp.Router` → `my_app/router.march`, `MyApp.Templates.Layout` → `my_app/templates/layout.march`; (2) `resolve_imports` now tracks loaded file paths (`loaded_paths` set) to prevent duplicate loads when explicit `use` and auto-discovery overlap; (3) auto-discovery: after resolving explicit imports, all `.march` files in `MARCH_LIB_PATH` dirs are loaded — sorted by module-name dot-depth (more dots = deeper namespace = fewer dependents = load first) so the evaluator sees dependencies before their users (`Templates.Page.Index` → `Templates.Layout` → `PageController` → `Router` → app root). Validated: full `Router.dispatch → PageController.index → Layout.wrap → Page.Index.render` chain works from a scaffolded `test_app_01` with `MARCH_LIB_PATH` set.
- ✅ **Forge dependency resolution and versioning system** — Full plan at `specs/plans/forge-dependency-resolution-plan.md`. Six implementation phases (Phases 1–6 complete): (1) Semver `~>` / `>=` / `<=` / `>` / `<` / exact / `AND` combinations (`resolver_version.ml`, `resolver_constraint.ml`); five dep source types (`RegistryDep`, `GitTagDep`, `GitBranchDep`, `GitRevDep`, `PathDep`); `forge.lock` TOML with manifest hash drift detection (`resolver_lockfile.ml`). (2) In-memory registry index; PubGrub greedy solver with FIFO work queue, conflict-driven incompatibility tracking, human-readable error messages (`resolver_pubgrub.ml`, `resolver_registry.ml`). (3) SHA-256 content-addressed package store — sorted canonical archive (`.git`/`.march` excluded, no timestamps), store/lookup/verify with tampering detection (`resolver_cas_package.ml`). (4) `[patch]` fork overrides — `GitBranchDep`/`PathDep` substitution before solver; extra patches for transitive deps; `forge deps` and `forge deps update`. (5) Text-based `.march` source API surface extraction (`pub fn`/`pub type`), diff (Added/Removed/Changed), MAJOR/MINOR/PATCH classification, semver enforcement in `forge publish --old-source` (`resolver_api_surface.ml`, `cmd_publish.ml`). (6) Property-based tests (QCheck, 200 cases × 3 properties), regression tests for Cargo/Hex/npm known bugs, solver performance benchmarks (chain-100: <100ms, chain-500: <1s, diamond-20×20: <500ms). Total: 132 forge tests across 6 suites.
- ✅ **Forge toolchain version manager (first slice)** — `forge toolchain install [<version>|nightly]` / `use` / `list` / `uninstall` over the shared `~/.march/{versions,current,bin}` layout (`forge/lib/toolchain.ml`), plus the `install.sh` curl installer. See `specs/forge_version_manager.md § Implementation Status`.

#### Forge version & package management — remaining gaps

_Full prioritised list and rationale in `specs/forge_version_manager.md § Gaps & Roadmap (2026-06-13)`._

- ✅ **Per-project toolchain pinning** — `.march-version` (walking up parents) + Version Resolution Order (`Toolchain.find_pin`/`resolve_version`/`march_command`/`path_prefix`, 12 unit tests), wired into `forge build`/`run`/`check`/`test` via the shared `lib_path_env` PATH prefix; `build`/`run` hard-error on a missing pin. New `forge toolchain pin`/`which` commands. See `forge_version_manager.md § Implementation Status`.
- ✅ **Toolchain version in `forge.lock`** — Option B (record + drift-warn, never override): `forge deps` writes a `[toolchain]` section; `forge build` warns on drift (`Resolver_lockfile.read_toolchain`/`?toolchain`, `Toolchain.toolchain_drift`, 5 unit tests). `.march-version` stays authoritative. `--frozen` enforce mode is future work.
- ✅ **`forge.toml` `march` constraint enforcement** — `forge build` blocks when the resolved toolchain doesn't satisfy an optional `march = "~> X.Y"` under `[package]` (`Toolchain.check_constraint`, 4 unit tests; non-semver tags allowed through). `project.ml` gains a `march_req` field.
- ✅ **Auto-install a missing pinned toolchain** — `forge build`/`run` download the resolved pin/global toolchain on demand (`Toolchain.ensure_installed`, rustup-style) instead of erroring; constraint checked before download. 2 unit tests + live e2e.
- ✅ **`forge upgrade`** — installs the latest March (newest stable, else newest nightly) and makes it active; no-op if already latest (`Toolchain.upgrade`, verified e2e).
- ✅ **`forge toolchain list --remote`** — lists installable versions from the GitHub releases API, grouped stable/nightly, marking installed ones (`Toolchain.classify_remote`/`list_remote`, 1 unit test).
- ✅ **`forge tree` / `forge why <pkg>`** — dependency-graph introspection (`deptree.ml` pure tree/path logic, 4 unit tests; `cmd_tree.ml` rebuilds edges from each installed dep's forge.toml since the lockfile has none). Diamonds and cycles handled.
- [ ] **`forge outdated`** — show deps with newer compatible versions (needs the registry to know what's newer).
- ✅ **`license`/`repository`/`homepage` in `forge.toml`** — parsed into the project record (`project.ml`); `forge new` scaffolds a `license` placeholder; `forge publish` nudges when unset (2 unit tests). `keywords` (array) now feasible — TOML parser gained `Array` support.
- [ ] **Registry server + network publish/fetch + `forge login`/`forge yank`** — `forge publish` validates locally only today (no server, no auth, no network fetch); distribution is git/path-only. Largest package-side gap ("Phase 6").
- ✅ **Workspaces / monorepo — Phase 1** — `[workspace].members` in `forge.toml`; `forge build`/`test`/`lint` iterate all members at the workspace root (dep order TBD — currently declaration order); `-p <name>` selector for a single member; `Workspace.find_root` walks up parents; `Workspace.parse_members` (TOML array) pure + 5 unit tests. TOML parser now supports arrays (`Array` variant + `get_string_list`). Phase 2 (unified `forge.lock`) and Phase 3 (topological order, `--changed`) are future work.
- [ ] **Vendoring / explicit offline mode** (`forge vendor`, `--offline`) — partly mitigated by the CAS cache; no explicit story.
- [ ] **Optional dependencies / feature flags** — conditional deps and compile-time features (a language-design question).
- [ ] **Full AST-based semver-compat checking on publish** — `Resolver_api_surface` is text-heuristic; linearity/generic diffing needs compiler integration.

## P2 — Important / Near-Term

- [ ] **mono: `build_subst`/`match_ty` can bind a type param to a dangling non-`"_"` TVar — `mangle_ty` silently produces a `...$V_<n>` "specialized" name** — latent gap in the shared specialization machinery, found in review of the Wave 2 Task 1 fix but **pre-existing**: `match_ty` will bind e.g. `a → TVar "_1456"` when the call-site argument type is itself still abstract (a real dangling var, not the `TVar "_"` lowering placeholder, which IS handled by the wildcard-preference logic), and `mangle_ty` renders it as `V__1456` — yielding a specialized name (e.g. `Show$List.show$List_V__1456`, or any ordinary generic fn's `foo$V__1456`) whose body still carries the unresolved element type. This affects the **pre-existing generic-fn EApp specialization branch** just as much as the interface-impl `enqueue_specialized_impl` path added in Wave 2 Task 1 — both feed `build_subst` output straight into `mangle_name`. Downstream consequences today: the nested method call inside such a body stays unresolvable, and (post-Wave-2) trips the loud `fail_if_unresolved_iface_method` failwith in llvm_emit rather than miscompiling; the fresh-name-derived `V_<n>` suffix is also compile-order-dependent, which could make emitted symbol names nondeterministic across compiles. **No concrete trigger program was found (reviewer tried)** — `match_ty`'s concrete-over-TVar binding preference covers the observed cases — so this is hardening, not a live bug: consider having `build_subst` (or its two consumers) drop/reject bindings whose RHS still `has_tvar`, or mangle them to a canonical erased marker instead of `V_<n>`.
- [ ] **lower: guarded match arms don't share the fallback join point — 7 duplicate closure types / 9 functions for a 3-arm guarded match** — found during Wave 2 Task 5 bookkeeping, reading `test/snapshots/perceus/guard_match.expected` (the golden snapshot for `classify(x)`'s 3 guarded arms, `n > 0` / `n < 0` / fallback `"zero"`). The dump shows **7 separate join-point closure types (`$Clo_$jp11$0`, `$Clo_$jp6$1`, `$Clo_$jp1$2` through `$Clo_$jp1$6`) and 9 functions**, with `$jp1$apply$2`, `$jp1$apply$3`, `$jp1$apply$5`, `$jp1$apply$6` all being byte-identical `panic("non-exhaustive pattern match")` bodies and `$jp6$apply$1`/`$jp6$apply$4` both being byte-identical `"zero"` bodies — despite `lower.ml`'s own doc comment on the join-point mechanism claiming it exists specifically to share the fallback body "once... instead of being duplicated 4× per nesting level." The guard-desugaring path appears to re-lower a fresh join point per guard check in the chain rather than threading one shared join point through all of them. This is IR bloat, not a correctness issue (all three guard values 5/-3/0 produce correct compiled output, per Wave 2 Task 4's audit) — relates to the Wave-1 analysis §4 Medium finding "guarded matches duplicate the rest-tree per arm" (`specs/analysis/2026-07-01-pipeline-deep-review.md`), which this snapshot now gives a concrete, reproducible, human-auditable artifact for. Worth a look if TIR size becomes relevant for guard-heavy code; not fixed here (test-infra/bookkeeping task, `lib/tir/lower.ml` out of scope).
- [ ] **`MARCH_LIB_PATH` compiles every `.march` file in dependency directories regardless of reachability — one broken unrelated module blocks the whole build** — March's multi-file compilation model (see `CLAUDE.md` "Multi-file compilation") walks ALL `.march` files under every `MARCH_LIB_PATH` directory recursively and loads their modules automatically; there is no reachability analysis from the entry file, so a compile error in a module the entry never imports or calls still fails the whole build. Demonstrated harmful in practice on 2026-07-02 (the "march_bin B9-regression incident," see `specs/todos.md`'s B9-regression hotfix entry and deciduous nodes 1556–1558): an unrelated bastion module's compile error under `MARCH_LIB_PATH` blocked all `forgepm` builds even though nothing in the actual build target depended on that module. Wants either (a) reachability-based module pruning — only typecheck/lower modules transitively imported from the entry file — or (b) per-module compile isolation so one module's failure doesn't abort sibling modules' compilation/reporting. Either direction is a `bin/main.ml`/resolver-level change (`resolve_imports`, auto-discovery phase) outside this bookkeeping task's scope.

### Stdlib hardening: Phase 5 — missing high-demand APIs

Spec: `specs/2026-04-14-stdlib-phase5-plan.md`.  These are *new
functionality* gaps left after Phase 0–4 closed every blocker, security
finding, and convention drift.  Listed in the recommended order.

- ✅ **Logger v2 — structured fields, tracing, appenders** — Replaced
  the `println`-with-severity v1 logger with: rich `LogValue` field
  type (Str/Int/Float/Bool/Atom/Null), pluggable
  appenders+formatters (`format_text` / `format_logfmt` /
  `format_json`), per-module level overrides, scoped context
  (`with_scope` with auto-pop on panic via `try_finally`), tracing
  helpers (`with_span`, `with_trace_context`,
  `current_trace_id`/`current_span_id`).  JSON formatter is
  OpenTelemetry-friendly — `trace_id`/`span_id`/`parent_span_id`
  appear at the top level; everything else under `attributes`.
  Field deduplication ensures nested scopes don't emit duplicate
  keys.  Backward-compatible with v1: existing `Logger.info`,
  `with_context`, `set_level` keep working unchanged.  Runtime adds
  11 new builtins.  4 new tests; `test_march` 1311 → 1315.  Spec:
  `specs/2026-04-14-logger-v2-design.md`.
- ✅ **Structured concurrency** — `Task.race`, `Task.any`,
  `Task.all_settled`, `Task.scope`, cancellation tokens, and
  `task_cancel_by_id`.  Runtime: signal-based preemption daemon
  (`SIGUSR1` via `pthread_kill`, 1 ms quantum), `march_cancel_token`
  ref-counted struct with `march_sched_spawn_with_cancel`.  Eval:
  `VCancelToken` value variant, `te_cancelled` flag on `task_entry`,
  five new builtins (`task_cancel_token_new`, `task_cancel`,
  `task_is_cancelled`, `task_spawn_with_cancel`, `task_cancel_by_id`).
  Typecheck: `CancelToken` type, `task_cancel_by_id` poly1 signature.
  Stdlib: `Task.race` (first wins, losers cancelled), `Task.any`
  (first Ok wins, all-Err → `Err(list)`), `Task.all_settled`
  (no short-circuit), `Task.scope` (Phase 1 pass-through; Phase 2+
  injects cancel token).  Tests: 24 in `test/stdlib/test_task.march`
  (100 % pass), 10 new alcotest cases in `test_march`
  (`test_march` 1320 → 1330).
- ✅ **`Test.assert_eq` / `assert_ne` (generic)** — `stdlib/test.march`:
  single generic `assert_eq(expected, actual, msg)` using structural `==`
  (which falls through to OCaml equality when no `Eq` impl is present).
  Works on ints, strings, bools, floats, tuples, records, ADT variants,
  lists, and arbitrary nestings.  Failure messages render both sides
  via `to_string`.  Counterpart `assert_ne`.  9 new tests in
  `test/stdlib/test_test.march` (the first test for the `Test` module
  itself).
- ✅ **`UUID.v7` / `v7_at` / `timestamp_ms`** — RFC 9562 time-ordered
  UUIDs for better B-tree locality than v4.  Runtime adds
  `uuid_v7` (CSPRNG + current unix_time_ms), `uuid_v7_at(unix_ms)`
  (pinned timestamp — useful for backfilling and deterministic
  tests), and `unix_time_ms()`.  Stdlib exposes `UUID.v7`, `UUID.v7_at`,
  and `UUID.timestamp_ms` (extracts the embedded millisecond stamp
  from v7 UUIDs, returns `None` for other versions).  Version = 7
  and variant = `10xx` bits set correctly.  String-sort order of v7
  UUIDs matches timestamp order — that's the whole point.  5 new
  tests in `test_march` `uuid stdlib` group.  1315 → 1320.
- ✅ **DateTime timezones — Phase A: fixed offsets** —
  `stdlib/datetime.march`: new `Tz(name, offset_seconds)` and
  `LocalDateTime(civil, tz)` ADTs.  Constructors:
  `utc_zone`, `fixed_zone(name, secs)`, `fixed_zone_hours(h)`,
  `fixed_zone_hm(h, m)` (auto-derives ISO label).  Accessors:
  `zone_name`, `zone_offset_seconds`, `format_offset` (`"Z"`,
  `"+HH:MM"`, `"-HH:MM"`).  Construction:
  `local_of`, `local_now`, `local_from_timestamp`,
  `local_from_utc`.  Conversion: `local_to_utc`,
  `local_to_timestamp`, `local_with_zone` (same instant, different
  wall clock).  Format extension: `local_format` adds `%z` (compact
  `+HHMM`), `%:z` (ISO `+HH:MM`/`Z`), `%Z` (zone name).  Parse:
  `parse_offset` accepts `2024-01-15T10:30:45Z`,
  `2024-01-15 10:30:45+05:30`, and the compact `±HHMM` form,
  rejecting anything without an offset suffix.  Phase B (DST-aware
  IANA zones) deferred — needs tzdata loading or hard-coded
  transition rules; the fixed-offset primitive shipping now is
  the substrate that approach will sit on top of.  11 new tests in
  `test_march` `stdlib_datetime` group; `test_march` 1330 → 1341.
- ✅ **Streams (`Seq.from_*` source constructors) — Phase A** —
  `stdlib/seq.march` gains the source-end primitives the existing
  transform/consume API was missing, all yielding the same
  church-encoded `Seq(a)` so they compose with `map`/`filter`/`take`/
  `batch`/`to_list`/`fold` exactly like `from_list`.  Adds:
  `from_string_lines(s)` (split by `"\n"`, trailing-newline-tolerant),
  `from_file_lines(path)` (lazy pull, auto-close on EOF; returns
  `Result(Seq, FileError)`), `from_file_chunks(path, size)` (lazy
  fixed-size byte chunks), `repeat(x, n)` (n copies), `iterate(seed,
  step, n)` (apply `step` n times, yielding seed first), `cycle(seq, n)`
  (concatenate seq with itself n times — re-folds source per cycle, so
  not safe for one-shot file/http sources), `batched(seq, n)` (alias for
  the existing `batch` to match the API name in the spec), and
  `unfold_until(seed, next, max)` (bounded `unfold` that stops at the
  smaller of `next=None` or `max` elements).  Documented limitation:
  because Seq is church-encoded, `take(seq, k)` reads the entire source
  to EOF (the consumer-side fold doesn't truly short-circuit); a real
  pull-stream representation (`Step(a) = Done | Yield(a, () -> Stream)`)
  is the planned Phase B follow-up, which would also enable
  `from_http` and `from_channel` streaming with proper back-pressure.
  TDD: 21 new tests in `test/stdlib/test_seq.march` exercising
  every new constructor + the lazy-take and EOF-close paths,
  added BEFORE the implementation as red-then-green.  test_seq
  101 → 122; test_stdlib_march 18 → 19 (now wires `test_seq.march`
  into the runner).
- ✅ **`Stats.quantile` / `quantiles` / `iqr` / `five_number_summary`**
  — `stdlib/stats.march`: `quantile(xs, q, method)` implements all 9
  Hyndman & Fan (1996) interpolation methods matching R's
  `quantile(type=k)` and numpy's `method=` argument
  (`InvertedCdf`, `AveragedInvertedCdf`, `ClosestObservation`,
  `InterpolatedInvertedCdf`, `Hazen`, `Weibull`, `Linear` [default],
  `MedianUnbiased`, `NormalUnbiased`).  Batched `quantiles(xs, qs,
  method)` sorts once.  `iqr(xs, method)` / `iqr_default(xs)`.
  `five_number_summary(xs, method)` returns `(min, Q1, median,
  Q3, max)`.  `quantile_default` is a `Linear` alias consistent with
  the existing `percentile`.  18 new tests in
  `test/stdlib/test_stats.march`, cross-checked against R reference
  values for each method.
- ✅ **`Random.normal` / `exponential` / `bernoulli` /
  `choice_weighted` / `choice`** — `stdlib/random.march`:
  `standard_normal(rng)` via Box-Muller; `normal(rng, mu, sigma)`;
  `exponential(rng, lambda)` via inverse-CDF; `bernoulli(rng, p)`;
  `choice(rng, xs)` uniform; `choice_weighted(rng,
  List((a, Float)))` with cumulative scan.  All samplers validate
  params (panic on `sigma<0`, `lambda<=0`, `p` out of
  `[0,1]`, negative or all-zero weights).  All return `(value,
  new_rng)` following Random's existing functional style.  13 new
  tests in `test/stdlib/test_random.march` including
  law-of-large-numbers check for normal mean, deterministic
  same-seed-same-result, and weight-collapse behaviour.
- ✅ **Distributed algorithms — `Deque`, `VectorClock`, `Merkle`, `CRDT`, `ConsistentHash`** — Five new stdlib modules. `stdlib/deque.march`: functional double-ended queue with O(1) size tracking; banker's deque (two-list representation); `push_front/back`, `pop_front/back`, `peek_front/back`, `from_list`, `to_list`, `map`, `filter`, `fold_left`, `fold_right`, `concat`, `any`, `all`. `stdlib/vector_clock.march`: causal ordering for distributed actors via `Map(String, Int)`; `increment`, `merge` (component-wise max), `compare` → `ClockOrder = Before | After | Concurrent | Equal`, `happens_before`, `concurrent`, `advance`, `entries`, `sum`. `stdlib/merkle.march`: SHA-256 content-addressed tree; `MLeaf`/`MBranch` constructors; `leaf` (caller-supplied serializer), `branch`, `root_hash`, `build` (balanced from list), `diff` (tail-recursive work-list), `leaves`, `leaf_hashes`, `contains_hash`, `size`. `stdlib/crdt.march`: four CRDTs nested under `CRDT.*` — `GCounter` (grow-only counter, per-actor map), `PNCounter` (increment/decrement via two maps), `LWWRegister` (last-write-wins with VectorClock, left-bias on concurrent), `ORSet` (observed-remove set with UUID tags). `stdlib/consistent_hash.march`: sorted virtual-node ring with configurable replica count; `add`, `remove`, `get` (successor lookup with wraparound), `nodes`, `node_names`, `size`. 5 new test files; design spec: `specs/2026-06-20-distributed-algorithms-design.md`.

### Database: Depot

- ✅ **Gate objects and validation (v1 + v2)** — `Depot.Gate` typed validation pipeline with `cast/validate/constraint` steps that accumulate errors without short-circuiting. v1: `cast/2` for string-pair params. v2: `cast_record/3` for record-based base+params with diff tracking — only changed fields recorded. New v2 API: `base/1`, `changed_fields/1`, `changed/2`, `apply_changes/1`. All validators (`validate_required`, `validate_length`, `validate_format`, `validate_inclusion`, `validate_exclusion`, `validate_number`, `validate_acceptance`, `validate_confirmation`, `validate_change`) work with both v1 and v2 gates. DB constraint mapping: `unique_constraint`, `foreign_key_constraint`, `no_assoc_constraint`, `check_constraint`, `apply_constraint_error`. 97 tests in `test/stdlib/test_depot_gate.march` (71 v1 + 26 v2). Spec: `specs/depot/changesets.md`, `specs/depot/integration-plan.md`.
- ✅ **`Depot.Schema` — record-based definition** — `Depot.Schema.define("table", { fields = { name = Type, ... }, belongs_to = { ... }, has_many = { ... }, ... })` returns a `Schema` value. Fields are a record where bare `Type` means no options, `(Type, { default = val })` adds opts. Associations grouped by kind. `Depot.Schema.blank(schema)` returns a record with all defaults. Inspection: `table/1`, `fields/1`, `field_names/1`, `field_type/2`, `field_opts/2`, `primary_key/1`, `is_virtual/2`, `associations/1`, `association/2`, `db_fields/1`. 7 record introspection builtins (`record_keys`, `record_values`, `record_entries`, `record_get`, `record_put`, `record_has_key`, `record_from_list`). `List.member/2` added to stdlib. 28 tests in `test/stdlib/test_depot_schema.march`. `stdlib/depot_schema.march`. Spec: `specs/depot/integration-plan.md`.
- ✅ **`Depot.Repo` — Vault-backed in-memory repository** — Phase 3 read+write API backed by Vault tables (one per schema table). `insert(schema, gate)` → validates gate, merges `apply_changes`, auto-generates UUID primary key, stores in Vault, returns `Ok(record)` or `Err(gate)`. `update(schema, gate)` → finds PK in gate base, merges changes, persists. `delete(schema, id)`, `delete_all(schema)`. Read API: `all(schema)`, `get(schema, id)` → `Option`, `get_bang(schema, id)` → panics if missing, `get_by(schema, clauses_record)` → scan+filter by field equality, `one(schema)` → `Some` if exactly one row, `count(schema)`, `exists(schema)`. 27 tests in `test/stdlib/test_depot_repo.march`. `stdlib/depot_repo.march`. Spec: `specs/depot/integration-plan.md`.
- ✅ **`Depot.Query` — composable lazy query builder** — `Depot.Query.from(schema)` creates a query, then pipe through `where_/2` (predicate fn, AND-combined), `select/2` (projection fn), `order_by/3`/`order_asc/2`/`order_desc/2`, `limit/2`, `offset/2`. Execution: `execute/2` applies where→sort→offset→limit→select in order. Convenience: `count/2`, `exists/2`, `first/2`. `type Query = Query(schema, wheres, select_fn, order_bys, limit_val, offset_val)`. Insertion sort for order_by. 16 tests in `test/stdlib/test_depot_query.march`. `stdlib/depot_query.march`. Spec: `specs/depot/integration-plan.md`.
- ✅ **`Depot.Migration` — DDL SQL generation + migration tracking** — `create_table(name, columns_record)` generates CREATE TABLE with column specs (type mapping, NOT NULL, DEFAULT, PRIMARY KEY, REFERENCES with ON DELETE/UPDATE). `alter_table(name, changes)` with `add`/`modify`/`remove`. `drop_table`, `create_index(table, cols, opts)` (unique, custom name), `create_index_simple`, `drop_index`, `rename_table`, `rename_column`, `execute(raw_sql)`, `references(table, opts)`. Migration tracking: `migrations_table()`, `record_migration(version)`, `applied_migrations()`, `is_applied(version)`. Vault-backed log. 17 tests in `test/stdlib/test_depot_migration.march`. `stdlib/depot_migration.march`. Spec: `specs/depot/integration-plan.md`.
- ✅ **`forge depot` CLI — migrate/rollback/migrations/reset/gen** — *(Moved to depot repo)* `cmd_depot.ml` removed from `forge/lib/` and ported to the depot repo. `forge depot migrate/rollback/migrations/reset/gen` subcommands now ship alongside the ORM they drive.
- ✅ **`Depot.Test` sandbox** — `start_sandbox()` enables test isolation, `checkout(tables)` snapshots Vault-backed Repo tables, `checkin()` rolls back to snapshot, `stop_sandbox()` deactivates. `sandboxed(tables, fn)` auto-wraps a function with checkout/checkin. `active()` checks state. 7 tests in `test/stdlib/test_depot_test.march`. `stdlib/depot_test.march`. Spec: `specs/depot/integration-plan.md`.
- ✅ **`forge bastion gen schema/auth/json` and `forge assets`** — *(Moved to bastion repo)* All Bastion and asset generator commands ported to the bastion repo. `forge bastion.gen.context` and `forge bastion.gen.html` similarly deferred to the bastion repo.

### LSP: Performance Insights

- ✅ **LSP Performance Insights — Phase 1 (AST-level)** — Three AST-only insights implemented: (1) tail call optimization — `tco_check` walks function bodies tracking tail position, emits `NonTailCall` Warning with blocking-op description and accumulator suggestion; (2) actor message copying — `send_copy_check` detects `ESend` calls with non-linear complex types, emits `ActorSendCopy` Warning; (3) closure capture size — `lambda_free_vars` + `closure_capture_check` emits `ClosureCapture` Hint when lambda closes over ≥3 values. `perf_insight` type + `perf_insights` field in `Analysis.t`. Wired into `diagnostics` and `perf_insight_at` hover query. 11 new LSP tests.
- ✅ **LSP Performance Insights — Phase 2 (AST heuristics)** — All three heuristics done: (1) memory reuse opportunities via single-use-allocation heuristic, surfaced as FBIP inlay hints (`♻ reused` / `⧉ copied` — see the "LSP semantic tokens + documentHighlight + FBIP inlays" entry below); (2) **indirect call detection** — `indirect_call_check` flags `EApp(EVar n, …)` where `n` is a parameter of the enclosing function (dispatched through a function pointer), `IndirectCall` kind, code `perf/indirect-call`; (3) **allocation in recursive arms** — `recursive_alloc_check` flags constructor/record/tuple allocations inside a match/if arm of a self-recursive function (`contains_call` detects self-recursion), `RecursiveAlloc` kind, code `perf/recursive-alloc`. Both are synchronous AST passes wired into `collect_perf_insights`, surfaced as Hint diagnostics + hover. +4 LSP tests. The `"march.inlayHints.performanceAnnotations"` client-config toggle also landed (`perf_annotations_from_settings` reads it from `workspace/didChangeConfiguration` into a server ref gating `inlay_hints_for ~perf_annotations`).
- ✅ **LSP Phase 5 — incremental typecheck engine** (`specs/plans/2026-06-13-lsp-incremental-engine.md`) — The dominant per-keystroke LSP cost was `analyse` re-running the whole-module typechecker over `stdlib @ deps @ user` on every edit. Now the stdlib is typechecked **once** into a cached base env (`lsp/lib/typecheck_cache.ml`: `base_env`/`derive`), and forge deps **once** into a deps env keyed by their content hash (`deps_env`/`clear_deps`); only the edited file's own decls are re-checked per edit, layered via the new `Tc.check_module_with_env_full` (env-returning incremental check; the REPL-JIT path now also exposes its final env). Mutable env state (`errors`/`type_map`/`pending_constraints`/`import_tracker`) is reset per analysis so the shared base is never polluted; `type_map` is copied so stdlib/dep span→type entries survive. Also: `run_tir_pass` insights memoized by source hash; `workspace/didChangeWatchedFiles` invalidates the workspace + deps caches; `did_change` debounced (coalesces keystroke bursts, version-guarded); JSON-RPC integration tests over stdio (`test_jsonrpc.ml`). The 159-test LSP suite stays green (identical hover/def/completion/diagnostics), proving the layered checker matches the whole-module checker. Deferred: a per-def in-file AST `sig_hash`/`impl_hash` firewall (Increment G) — needs full-AST canonical serialization and the dominant cost is already removed. 11 new tests (test_incremental + test_jsonrpc).
- ✅ **LSP parameter-name inlay hints at call sites** — `inlay_hints_for` gained `?param_names` (default on). For a call `rect(10, 20)` whose callee `rect` has params `width`/`height`, it emits `width:`/`height:` Parameter-kind inlays at the START of each positional argument. `build_param_name_map` builds a function-name → param-names table from the raw user AST (`t.decls`, recursing into `DMod`), reusing `clause_param_names`; only registers a function when clause-0 has a name for every param (avoids misaligned hints on pattern params). `callee_simple_name` resolves plain (`EVar`) and qualified (`EField`) callees; `walk_e`/`walk_decls` visit every `EApp` and pair params/args by position (arity-safe). Noise reduction (`suppress_param_hint`): single-arg calls suppressed; bare-identifier arg equal to the param name, or ending `.param`/`_param`, or a field access `x.param`, suppressed as redundant. Client toggle `march.inlayHints.parameterNames` (default ON) via `param_name_hints_from_settings` + server ref, mirroring the perf-annotations toggle. Limitation: only user-file functions; stdlib param names unavailable to the LSP. +6 LSP tests.
- ✅ **LSP Performance Insights — Phase 3 (async TIR pipeline)** — Run `lower → mono → defun → known_call → perceus → escape` asynchronously in the LSP. Three new `perf_insight_kind` variants: `StackPromoted`, `FbipReuse`, `TirIndirectCall`. New `tir_fn_insight` type tracks per-function counts of `EStackAlloc`, `EReuse`, `ECallPtr`, and `EAlloc` nodes. New `code_lens_item` type for the `textDocument/codeLens` response. `run_tir_pass` function reparses source and runs the full TIR pipeline (guarded by `try-with`, skipped when errors exist). Server: `codeLensProvider` capability registered; `on_notif_doc_did_open` and `on_notif_doc_did_change` spawn `Lwt.async` fibers that run `run_tir_pass`, update the doc cache, and push an incremental `publishDiagnostics`; `textDocument/codeLens` handler returns per-function lens items (e.g. "⚡ 2 stack-allocated · ♻ 1 in-place"). `lsp/lib/dune` now depends on `march_tir`. 6 new LSP tests (147 total).
- ✅ **LSP Actionable Run / Debug code lenses + `workspace/executeCommand`** — Code lenses are now runnable, not just static perf annotations. `code_lens_item` gained `cl_command : string option` + `cl_args : Yojson.Safe.t list`; the perf-summary lenses keep `cl_command = None` (informational, client-side noop). New `build_action_lenses` (`lsp/lib/analysis.ml`) walks the document's decls (recursing into `describe`/`mod`) and emits a `▶ Run` + `🐞 Debug` lens pair above every `test "…"` block (cmds `march.runTest`/`march.debugTest`, args `[file_uri, test_name]`) and above `fn main` (cmds `march.run`/`march.debug`, args `[file_uri]`); these are produced unconditionally in `analyse` (independent of the TIR pass) and `run_tir_pass` now *appends* perf lenses instead of replacing, so action lenses survive. Server (`lsp/lib/server.ml`): `executeCommandProvider` capability lists the four command ids; the `textDocument/codeLens` handler attaches the carried `Command.create`; new `workspace/executeCommand` branch in `on_unknown_request` dispatches via the pure `resolve_lens_command` resolver — RUN commands shell out (`forge test --filter=<name> <file>` / `forge run`) and return `{status, exitCode, message}`, DEBUG commands return a non-blocking structured echo (`{status:"debug", command, dap:"march dap", arguments}`) so the editor launches its own interactive DAP session rather than the LSP blocking on one. 7 new LSP tests (255 total).

### Compiler: Type System

- ✅ **Phase 2c/2d — `Tagged(X, T)` specialization tags + environment records** — `Tagged(X, T)` two-arg phantom type constructor added to `builtin_types` (arity 2) in `typecheck.ml`. `cap_paths_in_surface_ty` skips `Tagged` args to prevent false capability extraction. Check 7 added to `check_module_needs`: a function with `Tagged(_, Realtime)` parameter cannot also take `Cap(Alloc)`, `Cap(IO)`, or `Cap(Panic)` — the realtime exclusion narrowing rule from `specs/capability-system-design.md §3/§5`. 5 new tests in `tag_and_typestate` suite. Phase 2d (environment records) is a library convention with no new compiler work: bundle capabilities into records, narrow via ordinary record construction, test with function fields. Documented in `docs/capabilities.md §Phase 2c/2d`.
- ✅ **Phase 3f tooling — `forge cap coverage`, LSP `proof cap` go-to-def/find-refs, LSP cap inlay hints** — (1) **`forge cap coverage`** (`forge/lib/cmd_cap.ml`): static call-graph BFS from test entry points; collects `DNeeds` capability paths per file, walks `DFn`/`DTest`/`DDescribe`/`DMod` call graphs, BFS-expands reachable callees per test, aggregates `caps_for_fns`, and prints "Covered: / IO.Console 5 tests / ... / Uncovered: / IO.Clock / ... / N/M capabilities covered (X%)". Exposed as `forge cap coverage` (+ `--dir` override) in `forge/bin/main.ml`. (2) **LSP `proof cap` go-to-def/find-refs**: `proof_cap_defs : (string, span) Hashtbl.t` field on `Analysis.t`; two-phase collection pass in `analyse`: Phase 1 (`register_proof_caps`, recursing into `DMod`) registers each `DProofCap` name into both `def_map` and `proof_cap_defs`; Phase 2 (`collect_proof_cap_uses`) walks `Cap(X)` type annotations via `collect_cap_ty_refs` and `DNeeds` paths into `use_map`; `refs_map` then inverts `use_map` automatically so existing `definition_at`/`references_at` machinery handles both features. (3) **LSP cap inlay hints**: `⬡ IO.Console` appended at `println()` call sites (and 24 other builtins), emitted only when the file has `DNeeds` declarations to avoid noise; 25-entry `builtin_cap_tbl`; new `walk_cap_decls` pass inside `inlay_hints_for` after the param-name hints block. **Bug fix**: `DProofCap`, `DNeeds`, `DOpts`, `DTransitions`, `DAlwaysLinearType`, `DSetup`, `DSetupAll` now correctly included in `user_decls` filter (were silently dropped by `_ -> dummy_span` fallback, causing all capability declarations to be invisible to the LSP analysis). 4 new LSP tests; 766 compiler/stdlib tests unchanged.
- ✅ **Phase 2f tooling — transitions hover, `via` completions, `forge cap query`** — Three tooling items from `specs/capability-system-design.md §9`. (1) **Transitions hover (LSP)**: `Analysis.t` gains `transitions_index` (handle name → `Ast.transition list`) and `always_linear_names` fields, both populated from `user_decls` in `analyse`. New `typestate_hover_at` function matches `ty_at` against `transitions_index`; when the cursor is on a `Handle(R, S)` expression, hover shows "always-linear" badge + current state + all transitions from that state with resource/via labels. Wired via `query_typestate_hover_at` into the `server.ml` hover assembler (added as the `ts_str` part after perf-insight). (2) **`via` completions (LSP)**: `via_completions` in `analysis.ml` detects when the cursor is in a `via <word>` position inside a `transitions` arm (line contains `->` and ` via`); scans backwards in source for `transitions <handle_name> do`, then filters `a.vars` to functions whose type is `handle_name(...) -> handle_name(...)`. Returns nil when no matching fns exist to avoid polluting normal completions. (3) **`forge cap query`** (`forge/lib/cmd_cap.ml` + `forge/bin/main.ml`): walks all `.march` files under the project root (or `--dir`), parses each with `March_parser`, extracts `DNeeds`/`DAlwaysLinearType`/`DTransitions`/`DProofCap` declarations (recursing into `DMod`), and prints a grouped summary (file-path header, then `needs:` / `always_linear:` / `transitions:` / `proof_caps:` sub-sections). Exposed as `forge cap query`. `forge/lib/dune` gains `march_ast march_lexer march_parser` deps. 1470 tests unchanged; no regressions.
- ✅ **Phase 2c compiler-enforced transitions blocks** — `transitions Handle do R: S1 -> S2 via fn_name end` declaration verifies that each `via` function exists and has the correct transition-shaped type (`Handle(..., S1) -> Handle(..., S2)`). Mismatched from/to-state types and missing functions are compile errors. The typechecker scans local functions with transition-shaped types not listed in the block and emits a warning with the suggested declaration text (e.g. `Open: Closed -> Open via fn_name`). `DTransitions` AST node propagated through all pass-through sites. Format.ml emits valid `transitions ... do ... end` blocks. LSP `collect_decl` registers each `via fn_name` span in `use_map` enabling go-to-def on via names. 7 new tests (`tag_and_typestate` suite). 1470 total tests pass.
- ✅ **Phase 2b always-linear types + `tag` phantom labels** — `always_linear type T(params) = ...` declaration form (`ALWAYSLINEAR` token, `DAlwaysLinearType` AST node). Every binding of an always-linear type is auto-promoted to `Linear` by the typechecker — no per-site `linear` annotation needed. Drop-without-use and double-use are compile-time errors at all three binding sites (let-bindings, named fn params, lambda params). `tag Foo` keyword added as parser-level sugar for zero-arg phantom label types (`tag Open` ≡ `type Open = Open`). `stdlib/handle.march` ships `Handle(r, s)` as the canonical always-linear typestate handle. Validated end-to-end: wrong-state calls, handle leaks, and double-use all produce clear type errors. 319 tests pass.
- ✅ **Phase 2a proof capability tokens** — `proof cap Name` declaration syntax (`PROOFCAP` compound token, `DProofCap` AST node). Producer registry (`env.proof_caps : (string * string) list`) tracks full cap path → declaring module. Two new typecheck enforcements: Check 1 enhanced with declaring-module hint when a proof cap is used without `needs`; Check 6 rejects functions outside the declaring module that return a proof cap without receiving it as a parameter (pass-through allowed). No runtime changes — proof caps are erased like all `Cap(X)`. 6 new tests (capabilities 19–24). Spec: `specs/capability-system-design.md §1`.
- ✅ **Phase 3a — Explicit bounded type parameters** — `fn f[s : ConnState](h : Handle(s)) : ...` syntax. Parser: optional `[lower_name : BoundType, ...]` bracket clause between function name and `(`; AST: `fn_bounds : (string * ty) list` field on `DFn`/`DPFn`; typecheck: `check_fn` registers each bound var in `fn_tvars` with constraints (`CADTBound`, `CInterface`, `CTNatBound`) added to `pending_constraints`; `discharge_constraints` checks concrete-type vars against the ADT/interface/Nat bound at the declaration boundary. Error: "`Int` is not a variant of `ConnState`". Nat bounds discharge against `CTNatBound`. Interface bounds (`Eq`, `Ord`, etc.) resolve as `CInterface`. 9 new tests in `tag_and_typestate` suite. 228 compiler tests pass.
- ✅ **Phase 3b — Policy-tag DCE pass** — IR-level audit pass for `Tagged(_, P)` functions. New `lib/tir/policy_dce.ml` with policy table (`NoAlloc`, `NoPanic`, `NoIO`, `Realtime → [NoAlloc; NoPanic; NoIO]`), transitive panic/IO analysis (fixpoint seeded from `direct_panic_builtins` / `tm_io_fns`), `fold_expr` structural walker, and `audit` entry point. `tm_io_fns : string list` field added to `tir_module`; extracted from typecheck env's `module_caps` in `bin/main.ml` pipeline after Fusion and before Defun. JIT pipeline also wired. 8 new unit tests in `policy_dce` suite. 236 compiler / 780 total tests pass.
- ✅ **Phase 3c — No-panic modules (`cap no_panic`)** — `cap no_panic` module directive statically verifies no panic-surface calls, directly or transitively. `CAP_NO_PANIC` compound token (`"cap" whitespace+ "no_panic"`); `DOpts of string list * span` AST node; `no_panic_mod`/`no_panic_modules` env fields; `panic_surface_direct`+`panic_surface_stdlib` static tables; `calls_in_expr` AST walker; `check_no_panic_module` with seed+fixpoint transitive analysis; errors point at call-site span with per-site suggestion (e.g. `checked_div`). Stdlib: `Math.checked_div`, `Math.checked_mod` as safe alternatives. 12 new tests in `cap_no_panic` suite. 248 compiler / 1535 total tests pass. See `specs/capability-system-phase3.md §3`.
- ✅ **Phase 3c extension — Z3 division-safety for `cap no_panic` (2026-06-25)** — Division/modulo ops (`/`, `%`, `int_div`, `int_mod`, `int_div_euclid`, `int_mod_euclid`) moved from `panic_surface_direct` (syntactic ban) to a new `lib/refinecheck/division_safety.ml` pass that can *approve* them when the divisor carries a `{v : Int | v != 0}` (or `v > 0`, `v >= 1`, etc.) refinement. Syntactic fast-path handles common patterns without Z3; Z3 (`Refine.discharge`) handles the full linear-arithmetic fragment. Conservative soundness: `Unverified` (Z3 absent / timeout) → error, so the `cap no_panic` guarantee is preserved. Literal zero divisor → always error; literal non-zero → always safe. Complex divisor expressions → conservative error. 6 new `divsafety` tests; 3 existing division tests updated to `typecheck_with_divsafety` helper. 327 compiler tests pass.
- ✅ **Pattern guard path threading + multi-position return inference (2026-06-26)** — `division_safety.ml`: `iter_div_sites` now carries a `path : (A.expr * bool) list` context threaded through EMatch arms, EIf branches, and ECond arms. `path_proves_nonzero` syntactically checks whether a guard (e.g. `when b != 0`, `when b > 0`) implies the divisor cannot be zero. `check_var_divisor` uses this so `match b do b when b != 0 -> a / b | _ -> 0 end` in a `cap no_panic` module is safe without a type annotation on `b`. `return_infer.ml`: new `return_positions` traversal (mirrors `tails` in `refine_check.ml`) collects all leaf return positions with their accumulated guard contexts from `EIf`, `ECond`, and `EMatch when` arms; `infer_clause` probes each position with guard-derived Z3 assumptions added and intersects results — `abs(x : {v != 0}) : Int do if x > 0 do x else 0 - x end` now infers `r > 0`. 16 new tests (4 `guard-path-sensitivity` in `test_refinecheck.ml`; 5 `divsafety` guard + 3 `return_refine_infer` guard + 1 updated no-crash test in `test_compiler.ml`). 341 compiler / 58 refinecheck tests pass.
- ✅ **Phase 3d — Additional capability directives (`cap pure`, `cap no_extern`, `cap deterministic`)** — Three new module-level directives sharing the `DOpts` AST node and compound-token lexer pattern. `CAP_PURE`, `CAP_NO_EXTERN`, `CAP_DETERMINISTIC` tokens. `pure_mod`/`no_extern_mod`/`deterministic_mod` env fields. Check functions: `check_pure_module` (bans `spawn`, `send`, `print`, `println`, IO, vault, random, and time builtins); `check_no_extern_module` (structural scan for `DExtern` and `DNeeds` with `IO.Foreign` paths); `check_deterministic_module` (bans `random_int`, `random_float`, `random_bool`, `random_bytes`, `uuid_v4`, `now_ms`, `now_ns`, `monotonic_ms`). All three wired into `check_module_core` at both top-level and nested DMod sites. 11 new tests in `cap_pure_no_extern_det` suite. 332 compiler tests pass.
- ✅ **Epoch-based capability revocation** — `revoke_cap(cap)` builtin, global `revocation_table`, `is_cap_valid(cap)`, and VCap handling in ESend. C runtime: `march_revoke_cap` / `march_is_cap_valid`. 5 new tests in supervision phase3 group.
- ✅ **Supervisor restart policies: `rest_for_one`/`one_for_all`** — Both policies fully implemented in `eval.ml` and tested (tests pass).
- ✅ **Improve error messages for complex types** — Added `pp_ty_pretty` (line-wrapping at 60 chars with indented args), `find_arg_mismatch` (identifies which arg differs), and enhanced `report_mismatch` with multi-line format for long types and contextual notes identifying the mismatching arg/field.
- ✅ **Usability diagnostics for docs-surfaced limitations** — Three limitations found while writing docs, handled per the "better diagnostic over risky grammar surgery" rule. (1) Top-level `mod` + sibling top-level `fn` now gives "A file may have only one top-level `mod`; everything else must live inside it." (new `MOD … END error` production in `parser.mly`, zero new grammar conflicts) instead of menhir's generic "I got stuck here"; one-module-per-file stays by design. (2) Same-printed-name type collision (e.g. local `Config` vs stdlib `Config`) now adds a `same_name_note` in `typecheck.ml`'s `report_mismatch` explaining the global-namespace collision and suggesting rename/qualify, replacing the baffling "expected `Config` but got `Config`"; name resolution unchanged. (3) Deeply-nested inline `match`-arm bodies: investigated the reported needs-do/end parse failure, could not reproduce across ~12 variations — already handled by the contextual-NL `token_filter.ml`; left untouched, added a regression test. 3 new `error_improvements` compiler tests (321 compiler total).
- ✅ **Elm-like compiler diagnostics + LSP `relatedInformation`** — All eight error/warning improvements from `specs/2026-06-22-error-improvements.md`: (1) secondary span `labels` rendered as source snippets below the primary error; (2) `if`/`match` branch type mismatches label the offending branch; (3) arity errors label the definition site; (4) record field-not-found suggests nearest match via edit-distance; (5) `let?` mismatch includes actual RHS type; (6) redundant arm warnings via Maranget's usefulness algorithm with sigma-completeness termination fix; (7) parse errors unified with `render_diagnostic` — `parse_error_diagnostic` + `render_parse_error` route through the shared renderer producing `-- ERROR` header + indented notes; `if x then` error now notes "do/end blocks, not then"; (8) qualified-error messages return `(message, notes)` pairs. LSP: `diag_to_lsp` maps `labels` → `DiagnosticRelatedInformation`; `remap_diagnostic` remaps secondary-span locations. 14 compiler tests + 2 LSP tests.
