- ✅ **Capability body enforcement — Phase 2: body-scanning pass** — (Duplicate entry; see Phase 2 item in Done section.)

### Runtime: Hot Code Reloading

Spec: `specs/hot-code-reload.md`. Erlang-style live code swapping for deployed Bastion apps. **Two key findings from the design review:** (1) March's whole-program optimization (mono, defun, Perceus/borrow, LTO) is in tension with module isolation — hot reload must *forfeit* those at the boundary; two execution models, **Model A (interpreter trampoline)** shippable now, **Model B (native JIT)** research gated on a spike. (2) The CAS (`lib/cas/`) is the natural HCR substrate — per-SCC granularity, dual `sig_hash`/`impl_hash`, compiler/runtime identity already in the artifact key, two-tier content-addressed store — so it replaces the bespoke ABI table, source-diff, shipping protocol, and integer-ID scheme **once `did_hash` is Merkle-populated**. Phasing is CAS-prerequisite-first, then trampoline. **(RESOLVED prerequisite: the `impl_hash` Merkle-root population is DONE — see Phase 1 below and `progress.md` 2026-06-21 "Hot Code Reload — Phase 1". The CAS substrate is now available; remaining HCR work is Phases 2–7.)**

- [ ] **Phase 0 — Spike (MANDATORY before any Model B work)** — Throwaway prototype proving native code can be swapped across a boundary without memory corruption: two app modules with a `Conn`-style borrowed-record boundary call, frozen RC/borrow ABI, hot-swap callee, run under ASan → zero double-frees/leaks; introduce a new closure shape flowing through the boundary → prove a no-defun-at-boundary scheme works (no defun fault) or conclude Model B can't defunctionalize the boundary; measure the boundary deoptimization on `bench/list_ops.march` + a Bastion request bench. Deliverable: go/no-go on Model B with real numbers. (Model A needs no spike.)
- ✅ **Phase 1 — CAS prerequisite (highest leverage; also fixed a real stale-cache bug)** — `impl_hash` is now a true Merkle root: `hash_module` (`lib/cas/pipeline.ml`) folds, into each definition's hash, both (a) the `impl_hash`es of its earlier-SCC callees (walking SCCs dependencies-first) and (b) the base hashes of every named type it *transitively* references. A callee body change propagates into every direct/transitive caller; a record/variant layout change propagates into every definition that uses the type directly or through another type. This closes the cross-SCC stale-cache hole (`known_call` inlines across SCCs, so `compile_scc`'s key was serving stale inlined callees) **and** the type-layout hole (TIR references types by name, so a layout change moved GEP offsets while leaving the user's bytes/hash unchanged) — the latter is HCR Part 5's shared-type soundness gate, now a hash comparison. **Implementation notes:** threaded callee/type hashes into the hash computation rather than *emitting* `ADefRef` nodes or adding types to `compute_sccs` (same Merkle guarantee, no TIR mutation; per-def callee sets via `Scc.deps_of`, type refs via a TCon walk over sig+body; types are folded by transitive closure of their base hashes, so no type-SCC machinery is needed and recursive types are cycle-safe). Externally-defined types (stdlib/deps) have no base hash and are simply not folded, mirroring the cross-SCC callee rule. Intra-SCC refs (self-recursion, `Group` members) are excluded from the fn fold (covered by the group hash). Regression tests in `test_cas.ml` `def_id` group (6 new): direct + two-hop transitive call propagation, unrelated-def isolation, direct + transitive type-layout propagation, unreferenced-type isolation (cas suite 44→50; compiler/codegen/eval/stdlib all green).
- ✅ **Phase 2 — Boundary + versioned dispatch (compiler + main-build)** — Boundary classifier, NAME_ID interning, runtime dispatch table (`march_dispatch.{c,h}`), `Llvm_emit` dispatch-call emission (enter/leave wrapping), inliner no-inline guard, real `impl_hash`es in `publish`, and runnable end-to-end test all complete. Known limitation: bare entry-file fn names in the single-file compiler path (`@b` not `@App.b`) — `forge build` qualifies by package name so real multi-file builds work; single-file `--hot-reload` only matches nested/library modules.
  - ✅ **Boundary classification** (`lib/tir/hot_reload.ml`, `is_reloadable`): a module is reloadable iff it is app code (under the package module prefix) or force-included, and not force-excluded (excludes win); module-path-aware prefix match (`MyApplication` is not under `MyApp`), so stdlib/deps/lookalikes are excluded. Pure, 6 TDD cases (`test/test_hot_reload.ml`).
  - ✅ **NAME_ID interning** (`Hot_reload.Name_table`): dense `name ↔ id` map with ids assigned in sorted-name order, so the mapping is a deterministic function of the boundary name set (not a source-order int that shifts on unrelated edits). Cross-build reload activation is keyed by name, so dense per-build ids suffice. 5 TDD cases.
  - ✅ **Runtime dispatch table** (`runtime/march_dispatch.{c,h}`): dense slot array indexed by `NAME_ID`, each slot a per-version ring (`MARCH_MAX_LIVE_VERSIONS`=2) of `{fn_ptr, atomic refs, impl_hash, kind, live}`. `march_dispatch_enter(name_id, &v)` pins the current version and reports its ring index so `leave(name_id, v)` unpins *that* version even after a concurrent `publish` advanced `current`; `publish` advances current and reclaims a non-current slot only when its refs have drained (else returns -1 → purge needed, a later phase). Release/acquire ordering on `current`. 7 TDD groups / 23 checks in `test/test_dispatch.c` (wired into `dune runtest`). (Epoch/grace reclamation for the enter-vs-reclaim race is deferred — noted in `march_dispatch_enter`.)
  - ✅ **`Llvm_emit` dispatch-call emission** — `emit_module ?hot_reload:Hot_reload.config option` (default `None` → existing codegen byte-identical). `ctx` carries the config, a `Name_table` of reloadable fn names → NAME_ID, and the current caller module; a `module_of_name` helper derives a fn's module from its qualified name. At a `Tir.EApp` call site, when `Hot_reload.needs_dispatch` holds (caller+callee both reloadable, callee a known top-level fn with a NAME_ID), the direct `call @callee(..)` is replaced by `alloca i32`; `%fp = call ptr @march_dispatch_enter(i32 NAME_ID, ptr %ver)`; indirect `call %fp(..)`; `load %ver`; `call @march_dispatch_leave(NAME_ID, %ver)`. Dispatch externs declared in the preamble. IR-level TDD in `test/test_hot_reload.ml` (`llvm_emit` group): with the flag the boundary call emits enter/leave and drops the direct call; without it the direct call is preserved. **Regression-clean: compiler 296, codegen 320 green** (the real compile path is unchanged with the flag off).
  - ✅ **Inliner no-inline guard** — `Inline.boundary_config` (set by `Opt.run ?hot_reload`) excludes reloadable fns from the inline-candidate set so a boundary→boundary call survives `Opt.run` as a real call site (small boundary callees would otherwise be inlined away before codegen). `module_of_name` promoted to `Hot_reload` as the single source of truth. 2 TDD cases (`test_hot_reload.ml` `inline_guard`): boundary callee not inlined under a config, inlined when off. Regression-clean (compiler 296, codegen 320 with the flag off).
  - ✅ **Runnable end-to-end — first hot-reload binary runs and genuinely dispatches.** Startup registration (`@main` emits `march_dispatch_init(n)` + a `march_dispatch_publish(NAME_ID, @fn, ptr null, NATIVE)` per boundary fn before user main spawns; impl_hash is null baseline, reload server stamps real hashes on activation — IR-tested). `march_dispatch.c` linked into the compile path (`bin/main.ml` `extra_c_files` — note there are *two* runtime-source lists; the `--compile` clang path uses `extra_c_files`, not `extra_files`). `--hot-reload <Prefix>` flag in `bin/main.ml` builds `Hot_reload.default_config Prefix`, threaded into both `emit_module` calls + `Opt.run`, and added to **both** `cas_flags` sites. **Verified:** a nested-module program (`Core.a`→recursive `Core.b`) compiled with `--hot-reload Core` emits the full dispatch sequence (init/publish/enter/leave), `Core.a`/`Core.b` survive the inliner (guard works in the real pipeline), the binary runs and prints identical output to the plain build. Regression-clean (compiler 296, codegen 320).
  - [ ] **Remaining (smaller follow-ups):** **boundary-naming for the entry module** — a single-file program's *top-level* functions are emitted bare (`@b`, not `@App.b`), so `--hot-reload <Prefix>` only matches *qualified* (nested/library) modules; the real `forge build` path qualifies modules by package name so this is the right model there, but the raw single-file compiler can't reload bare entry-file fns. ✅ **Real `impl_hash`es threaded into `publish`** — `emit_module` takes an optional `~impl_hashes:(name → 64-hex)` map; `bin/main.ml` builds it from the same `Pipeline.hash_module` SCC hashing that keys the artifact cache (`hd_impl_hash` per `FnDef`, across `HSingle`/`HGroup`), in both the `--compile` and `--emit-llvm`-only paths. In `hr_setup` each boundary fn's hash is emitted as a private NUL-terminated string global (`@.hr_hashN = [65 x i8]`) and passed to `march_dispatch_publish` as `ptr @.hr_hashN` instead of `ptr null` (null fallback retained for non-CAS paths). ✅ **Automated end-to-end test** (`test_stdlib_suite.ml` `adversarial-regressions`, Slow): compiles the nested-module `Core.a`→`Core.b` program with `--compile --hot-reload Core`, asserts it prints `dispatch works`, that the plain `--compile` build prints identically, and that `--emit-llvm --hot-reload Core` IR contains a real `call ptr @march_dispatch_enter` (not just the preamble `declare`). `--hot-reload` plumbed through `forge` + `config` from `forge.toml [hot-reload]`. Main-build symbol export (`-rdynamic`/un-stripped) — only needed for Model B (Phase 6) JIT resolution, not Model A; defer.
- ✅ **Phase 3 — Native reload server + `--compile-so` (shippable milestone, `dlopen` variant)** — Background pthread on a Unix-domain socket (`MARCH_HOT_RELOAD_SOCKET`). Text protocol: `PING → PONG`, `ABI_QUERY → SLOT id hash...END`, `ACTIVATE name impl_hash so_path → OK impl_hash | ERR reason`. Name registry (`march_dispatch_register_name`) maps human-readable function names to dispatch slot IDs at startup. `--compile-so` compiler flag produces a patch shared library (no `@main`, `-shared -fPIC`, `-undefined dynamic_lookup` / `--allow-shlib-undefined`): boundary functions exported as named symbols (`Server.square`, etc.). ACTIVATE: `dlopen(RTLD_NOW|RTLD_GLOBAL)` + `dlsym(handle, name)` + `march_dispatch_publish` — atomically replaces the function slot at runtime. `scripts/march-reload` client script: `--src` auto-compiles, `--so` uses pre-built. Verified end-to-end: `square(7) = 49 → 343` with live server, zero downtime. Works on Linux + macOS. `-Wl,-rdynamic` (Linux) / `-Wl,-export_dynamic` (macOS) export server symbols for patch resolution. `--compile-so` keyed into both `cas_flags` sites so cache can't silently ignore the mode. Demo: `demo/hcr_server.march` + `demo/hcr_client.march` + `scripts/rpc/hot-deploy.sh`. **Key design finding:** boundary functions must be in NESTED modules (`mod App do mod Server do fn square...end end`) — top-level module names are dropped in TIR, so `--hot-reload Prefix` only matches qualified (`Prefix.fn`) names. Remaining: Model A interpreter trampoline (zero-downtime without pre-compiling a patch); actor state migration (Phase 5); fleet tooling (Phase 7).
- ✅ **Phase 4 — `forge deploy hot` (CAS-native)** — Reload set = `impl_hash` set-diff vs the running process's active set (via `ABI_QUERY`), NOT a source diff. Safety gate = per-changed-def `sig_hash` comparison (ABI break rejected). Distribution = inline CAS over the SSH-tunneled Unix socket (`CAS_CHECK`/`CAS_PUT`). `VERSIONS` for crash-restart drift detection. Ed25519 signing: `march_deploy_hot keygen`/`show-pubkey` (`lib/ed25519/` OCaml library wrapping vendored `runtime/tweetnacl.c`); `--signing-pubkey` embeds the public key in the server binary; `ACTIVATE <name> <impl_hash> <cas_hash> <sig64>` verifies before dlopen. `--compile-so` emits a `.hcr_manifest` sidecar (cas_hash + per-fn impl_hash/sig_hash). `bin/main.ml`: fixed `remote_sig_hashes` (now passed to emit_module for all fns, not just RPC), manifest writing, `tweetnacl.c` linked with main binary for server-side verify. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-24-hcr-phase4-design.md`. Implementation: `runtime/tweetnacl.{h,c}`, `runtime/march_dispatch.{h,c}` (sig_hash, baseline_impl_hash, id→name), `runtime/march_reload.c` (CAS_CHECK/CAS_PUT/VERSIONS/signed-ACTIVATE), `forge/lib/cmd_hot_reload.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`, `forge/lib/project.ml` ([hot-reload] section), `lib/ed25519/`.
- ✅ **Phase 5 — Actor state migration** — Per-actor versioned handler dispatch with migrate-BEFORE-run ordering. **Runtime (Part A):** `MARCH_MIGRATE_TAG` system message + `march_migrate_msg_t` (malloc/free, not RC); `dispatch_name_id` field in `march_actor_meta`; `actor_green_thread` dispatches MIGRATE before the alive gate, calling a `migrate_fn` pointer that swaps the actor's state ptr atomically; `march_actor_set_dispatch_id` (called from spawn wrapper); `march_actor_broadcast_migrate` (two-phase: snapshot actors under lock, inject messages outside lock to avoid deadlock). State indirection in `--hot-reload` builds: actor struct holds `$f_state: TCon(State,[])` at field 2 (pointer-swappable); handlers load state fields from `$f_state`, reuse updates the inner record + writes the new ptr; spawn passes initial state record directly. `__migrate_*` LLVM alias exported from `-so` builds so `march_reload.c` can `dlsym` it. ACTIVATE extended with `migrate_required` flag; `march_reload.c` dlsyms `__migrate_<actor>` and calls `broadcast_migrate`. Spawn wrapper pattern in `llvm_emit.ml`: `*_spawn` emitted as `*_spawn_impl` + thin wrapper calling `march_actor_set_dispatch_id`. **Type guardrails (Part B):** `.schemas.json` sidecar emitted alongside `.so`/`.hcr_manifest` (actor name → `compat` + `state_fields[]`); `forge/lib/schema_diff.ml` (pure OCaml JSON parser + `diff_schemas`/`check_compat`/`requires_migration`); `@compat(forward|full|any)` actor annotation (`lib/ast/ast.ml` `actor_compat` field, parser extended); deploy-time compat check in `cmd_deploy_hot.ml` aborts if new schema is incompatible; `migrate_required` computed from `schema_diff.requires_migration`. Demo: `demo/hcr_actor_demo/counter_v1.march` + `counter_v2.march` (adds `history: List(Int)` field with `migrate_state`) + `run_demo.sh`. Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-24-hcr-phase5-plan.md`.
- [ ] **Phase 5C — Capability-safe hot deploys** — Make hot deploys *authorized*, not just *authenticated* (Phase 4 ed25519 signs but doesn't gate what the patch may *do*; static caps are erased so a signed patch can call any IO). Three parts: **(A)** emit each boundary fn's transitive capability closure (the data `check_module_needs` already computes, `typecheck.ml:5125`) + a `cap_root` digest into the `.hcr_manifest`; **(B)** **monotonicity gate** at `forge deploy hot` — a deploy may *narrow* the live system's authority freely but *widening* (a new cap not subsumed by any prior-held cap, via shared `cap_subsumes`) aborts before ACTIVATE unless an explicit signed `--grant-cap <C>` authorizes it (the capability dual of Phase 5 Part B's schema variance table); **(C)** node-local admission — server loads `MARCH_DEPLOY_POLICY`, recomputes `cap_root` from the CAS manifest (tamper check vs the signed value), rejects artifacts whose authority exceeds policy (`err_cap_policy`) before `dlopen` — the runtime checkpoint that re-materializes erased static caps at the trust boundary. Plus a compile-time **`migrate_state` IO-free bound** (it runs in the migration window ahead of user messages). Factor `io_cap_hierarchy`/`cap_subsumes` into a shared `lib/caps/cap_lattice.ml` (+ generated C table) so typecheck/forge/runtime can't drift. All gates opt-in/additive (legacy artifacts + no policy ⇒ permissive). **Part B is the genuinely novel piece** — a temporal capability-monotonicity discipline over a live hot-reloaded process. Follow-ons: IR-level NoAlloc/no_panic `migrate_state` bound (needs `policy_dce`); refinement-verified migration totality (Z3); capability leases across deploys. Spec: `specs/plans/2026-06-25-hcr-phase5c-capability-safe-deploys.md`.
- [ ] **Phase 6 — Model B: native JIT upgrade (gated on Phase 0 go)** — ORC JIT both platforms (pinned thread; macOS `MAP_JIT` + `pthread_jit_write_protect_np` + `allow-jit` entitlement note). **ORC end-to-end, NO `dlopen`** (ORC produces in-memory symbols, not an ELF `.so` — conflated in the first draft). Background upgrade of trampolined SCCs to native O2 (keyed by the same `impl_hash`); no-defun-at-boundary scheme per the spike; measured perf.
- ✅ **Phase 7 — Fleet, registry, tooling (P1–P6 of 7)** — `--env` server lists + `--canary <n>` health-check gate (`cmd_deploy_hot.ml`): `deploy_env` fans out to `[[hot-reload.env]]` servers (TOML parser now supports `[[array-of-tables]]`; `project.ml` gains `hr_envs : hot_reload_env list`; sequential deployment with optional canary window + PING health-check polling; rollback on canary failure prints error, leaves remaining servers untouched). Audit log: every ACTIVATE (pass or fail) appends one JSON line to `$MARCH_AUDIT_LOG` (default `$HOME/.local/share/march/audit.jsonl`) in `march_reload.c` — fields: `ts/type/fn/impl_hash/signer/cas_hash/result`. `VERSIONS_DETAIL` protocol command: `SLOT <id> <name> <impl_hash> <activated_at_ms> <signer_hex>`, backed by new `activated_at_ms`/`signer_hex` per-slot metadata in `march_dispatch.c`. `forge hot-reload status`: connects via SSH tunnel, sends `VERSIONS_DETAIL`, prints a dashboard table. `forge hot-reload init`: `git log` analysis → suggest `[hot-reload] exclude = [...]` for rarely-changing modules. `forge hot-reload keygen --rotate`: versioned `ed25519_secret_v<N>.key` file + `forge hot-reload use-key <path>`. Remaining: third CAS tier (`s3://`/`https://` pull-through; P7 of 7).
- ✅ **Phase 8 — Cross-module function signature evolution** — Coordinated upgrade gate: `.hcr_manifest` emits a `callers:` field per exported function; server stores caller set per slot (`callers_str` in `MarchDispatchSlot`); ACTIVATE command parses the optional `callers:` suffix and stores it via `march_dispatch_set_callers`; ABI_QUERY responses include `callers:` when set. Hard sig_hash rejection replaced with: if all server-recorded callers of the changed function are included in `to_activate`, allow the deploy; otherwise error naming the uncovered callers. **Phase 9 (epoch-tagged dispatch):** compiler emits `@__march_hcr_epoch = private global i32 0` (file-static cell) and `@__march_init(i32)` (exported init fn) into every `--compile-so` output; `GET_EPOCH` server command assigns a monotonic epoch per deploy batch; after `dlopen`, the server calls `dlsym(handle,"__march_init")` to stamp the epoch; boundary call sites in `.so` mode call `march_dispatch_enter_gen(NAME_ID, epoch, &v)` which routes to the newest ring slot whose epoch ≤ caller_epoch, so old deployed code naturally calls the old function version until it is redeployed; Phase 8 hard block downgrades to advisory warning when Phase 9 active. Key files: `forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`, `runtime/march_dispatch.c`, `runtime/march_reload.c`, `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`. Spec: `specs/plans/2026-06-26-hcr-cross-module-versioning.md` (Phase 8) and `specs/plans/2026-06-26-hcr-phase9-epoch-dispatch.md` (Phase 9). **dlclose GC:** `MarchFnVersion.handle` stores the `dlopen` handle; `march_dispatch_set_handle` wires it in after ACTIVATE; `slot_dlclose` releases it on ring-slot reclaim (in `march_dispatch_publish`) and at server shutdown (`march_dispatch_shutdown`). **Phase 10 (cluster-aware hot deploy):** shared epoch — `get_epoch_from_server` fetches once from the epoch-master node (first in `[[hot-reload.env]]` list); all ACTIVATEs in the batch carry the same epoch so cross-node epoch routing is meaningful. Rolling deploy (default strategy) — `rolling_deploy` iterates servers one at a time; after each success forge optionally GETs the configured `health_check_url` (curl exit 0 = healthy) and stops on failure. `strategy = "simultaneous"` option for parallel fan-out. `VERSIONS_DETAIL` response now includes a 6th `epoch` field (`march_dispatch_epoch` accessor + `%u` in snprintf). `forge hot-reload status` extended: multi-node fan-out, Node column, epoch column, `← drift` marker when impl_hash differs across nodes. `--env` flag added to filter by env name. `[hot-reload] health_check_url` and `strategy` fields added to `project.ml`. Key files: `runtime/march_dispatch.{h,c}`, `runtime/march_reload.c`, `forge/lib/project.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_deploy_hot.ml`, `forge/bin/main.ml`. Spec: `specs/plans/2026-06-26-hcr-clustering.md`.

Still passthrough: Option/Result fields with Float/Unit/List/nested payloads. **Codec completeness done (2026-06-22):** `forge ffi gen-c` now fully types Option/Result fields with `Float`/`Unit` payloads (fully-boxed `Option_Float_c` / mixed-niche `Option_Unit_c`), nested record/variant payloads in Option/Result fields (niche-safe heap-ptr path), and `List(T)` fields as malloc-owned C arrays (decode via spine traversal + `march_list_length`, encode via reverse-loop `march_list_cons`). Args use NATIVE SLOT REP (raw machine int for Int, NOT march_make_int); return is the generic tagged word. Verified: `ffi_apply1`/`ffi_count_matching` → `test/native/ffi_upcall` → 42/5. **Env-routed errors done (2026-06-21):** the `raises fn … : Result(T,E)` modifier + `march_env`/`march_raise` — the binding takes a hidden `march_env*` and returns the bare Ok payload, calling `march_raise(env,e)` to fail; a compiler-emitted call-site wrapper materializes Ok/Err (`test/native/ffi_raise` → 7/bad int; `gen-c` emits the env signature; `Result(Float,_)` Ok payload deferred). **Phase 8 `raw` DROPPED as redundant** — March pushes marshalling into the binding (borrow accessors / generated codecs), so heap params are already passed verbatim and the borrow/`consume` modes already cover RC; `raw` would add no capability. **Rust layer done (2026-06-21):** the `march` crate (`rust/march` + `rust/march-macro`) — `#[march]` (extern "C" shim: decode → catch_unwind → encode; `Result` routes Err/panic via `march_err`), `#[derive(Encoder, Decoder)]` (structs↔records, enums↔variants), `ResourceArc<T>`, `march::init!` (generates the March extern block), and `forge ffi add-rust` (scaffolder). Verified end-to-end (`scripts/verify-rust-ffi.sh`, `test/native/rust_ffi/` → 42/HELLO/7/25/divide by zero/33/75/24/41); forge 88. Doc: `specs/c-ffi-rust-layer.md`. **Rust follow-ons done (2026-06-22):** (1) `Option<f64>` fully-boxed ABI in `rust/march` — `sys.rs` declares `march_some_boxed`/`march_none_boxed`; `value.rs` adds `encode_option_f64`/`decode_option_f64` helpers; `#[march]` and `#[derive(Encoder/Decoder)]` detect `Option<f64>` and call these helpers (not the niche-form blanket impl); verified `maybe_float`/`unwrap_float_or` in `test/native/rust_ffi/`. (2) `ConsumeResourceArc<T>` — `resource.rs`: does NOT `march_dup` on `FromMarch` (March transfers ownership); `Drop` calls `march_drop`; `#[march]` emits `consume h` in the extern block; verified `counter_finish` in `test/native/rust_ffi/`. (3) Auto cargo build — `forge.toml [ffi.rust] crate = "..." lib = "..."` triggers `cargo build --release` in `forge build` and passes `target/release/lib<name>.a` as `--ffi-link`; `forge ffi add-rust` now prints the `[ffi.rust]` snippet; verified manually. Remaining: crate publishing (path-only today), `[[ffi.rust]]` (array-of-tables, TOML parser limitation), OCaml layer. **Core-ABI finishers done (2026-06-21):** (1) ABI version handshake — the interpreter verifies the dlopened runtime `.so`'s `march_ffi_abi_version()` and rejects a mismatch (compiled path is version-consistent by construction); (2) `Result(Float,_)` raises Ok payload (wrapper boxes the bare double via `march_make_float`); (3) `Option(Float)`/`Option(Unit)` returns via new `march_some_boxed`/`march_none_boxed` (Float fully boxed, Unit boxed-Some + niche-None; gen-c emits the right variant). All verified by `test/native/ffi_float` + extended C ABI unit test — **the core C ABI is v1-complete.*** Remaining FFI: `forge ffi import` (libclang), multi-lib `[[ffi]]`, the Rust follow-ons above: A stable native ABI (`runtime/march_ffi.h`, the `erl_nif.h` analog) plus the C binding path built on it, extending the existing `extern` scaffold. Tiered marshalling (primitives by value, String/Bytes borrowed, opaque state via refcounted `resource` handles with destructors, `raw` zero-copy escape hatch); explicit `borrow`/`consume` ownership modes for Perceus correctness; `fast`/`blocking` scheduler classification; Result-based error protocol; interpreter parity via dlopen+libffi; forge `[[ffi]]` build integration; C binding generators (`forge ffi gen-c` from a March `extern` block, `forge ffi import` from a C header with sidecar `.ffi.toml` for the irreducible ownership/nullability decisions). Rust `#[march]` (Rustler-style; Rust ownership maps 1:1 onto borrow/consume) + OCaml layers designed-for but deferred (consume the same ABI). 9-phase plan; critical gate is the RC-leak test harness (live-bytes flat across borrow/consume/return/resource). Spec: `specs/2026-06-19-c-ffi-abi-design.md`. **Interpreter FFI gap 1 (user shims) DONE (2026-06-22):** `[ffi] sources = [...]` C files now auto-compiled to a content-addressed temp `.so` (`~/.cache/march/march_ffi_shim_<hash>.so`) and dlopened with `RTLD_GLOBAL` before the first extern call; `--ffi-so <path>` accepts a pre-built shim; symbol lookup falls back to `RTLD_DEFAULT` so both runtime and shim symbols resolve; macOS gets `-undefined dynamic_lookup`; verified `test/native/ffi_interp_shim`. **Interpreter FFI gap 2 (closures/upcalls) clean error DONE (2026-06-22):** `TyArrow` params in extern calls now emit a clear actionable diagnostic ("closures/upcalls not yet supported — run with --compile") instead of crashing or producing wrong results. Full upcall support (JIT trampolines) deferred — see `specs/c-ffi-gaps.md`. Verified `test/native/ffi_interp_upcall.march`.

## P4 — Long-Term / Research

### Metatheory

- [ ] **Lean 4 mechanized metatheory** — Proof of type safety (progress + preservation) for the core March type system. Plan documented in `specs/plans/lean4-metatheory-plan.md`. Requires: formalized grammar, operational semantics, type rules, mechanized proofs of canonical forms lemma, substitution lemma, and soundness theorem.

### Compiler Backend

- [ ] **Query-based/demand-driven compiler architecture** — Current pipeline is linear (parse → desugar → typecheck → eval/TIR). Design goal is a query-based architecture (like `rustc`'s `salsa`) for fine-grained incremental recompilation. Deferred post-v1.
- ✅ **Constraint solver for type-level naturals (v1)** — `normalize_tnat` reduces concrete arithmetic (`2+3 → 5`, `(1+2)*3 → 9`) and applies identity/annihilation laws (`n+0 → n`, `n*1 → n`, `n*0 → 0`). `solve_nat_eq` in `unify` solves linear equations (`a+2=5 → a=3`). Parser extended: `INT` as `TyNat`, `+`/`*` in type position via `ty_nat_add`/`ty_nat_mul` levels. 9 new tests in `type_level_nat` group.
- ✅ **Record field auto-satisfy (Feature 2)** — Zero-declaration rule: when discharging `CInterface(Iface, TRecord[...])`, auto-satisfy single-method accessor-shaped interfaces (`a -> T`) when the record has a matching field. Applies only to anonymous `TRecord` types (not named aliases), single-method interfaces, and exact field name+type match. 9 tests in `record_auto_satisfy` group. Spec: `specs/structural-interface-satisfaction.md`.
- ✅ **`satisfy` declaration (Feature 1)** — New `satisfy Iface for T` keyword that generates an `impl` block from matching functions already in scope. `SATISFY` lexer token, `DSatisfy` AST node, `expand_satisfy` desugar expansion, passthrough in eval/lower/typecheck/format/LSP/coverage/refactor/span_remap. 10 tests in `satisfy_decl` group. Spec: `specs/structural-interface-satisfaction.md`.

### Compiler Optimizations (Planned)

See `specs/optimizations.md` for full catalog with effort/impact/dependency details.

- ✅ **Borrow inference and elision (P7)** — `lib/tir/borrow.ml` + `lib/tir/perceus.ml` integration. Pre-Perceus analysis marks TCon/TString/TPtr params as borrowed when they are only read (pattern-matched, field-accessed) and never stored, returned, or passed to owning positions. Fixpoint inter-procedural: params passed only to other borrowed positions remain borrowed. RC elision: (a) call sites skip EIncRC for borrowed args still live after the call; (b) callee suppresses scrutinee EDecRC for borrowed params; (c) last-use borrowed args get caller-side EDecRC instead of callee-side. Eliminates 100% of RC overhead for read-only middlewares (HTTP Conn pattern). 10 new tests in `borrow_inference` group.
- ✅ **Known-call optimization** — `lib/tir/known_call.ml`: replaces `ECallPtr` with direct `EApp` when the closure allocation is visible in scope. Runs between Defun and Perceus + in Opt fixed-point loop. Inline threshold raised 15→50. Struct update fusion (`Fusion.run_struct`) fuses chains of `EUpdate` operations into a single update. 8 new tests (known_call × 4, struct_fusion × 4).
- ✅ **Mutual TCO** — Shared loop with dispatch: `find_mutual_tco_groups` (Tarjan SCC + qualification), `emit_mutual_tco_group` (combined dispatch fn + thin wrappers), `EApp` back-edge for mutual calls. 5 tests in `mutual_tco_codegen` group. `bench/mutual_recursion.march`.
- ✅ **Case-of-known-constructor / Beta-ADT (P11)** — `lib/tir/beta_adt.ml`: matches `let r = EAlloc(tag, args) in ECase(r, [{tag; vs; body}], default)` and reduces to `let v0=args[0] in … in body`, dropping the dead EAlloc (DCE removes it). Run pre-Perceus (before RC insertion); safe post-Perceus only in the matching branch when the scrutinee is not referenced in the branch body. `tags_match` handles qualified (`Result.Ok`) vs bare (`Ok`) tag naming via `short_name`. 3 new tests in `beta_adt` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P11.
- ✅ **Variable copy propagation (P12)** — Extended `lib/tir/cprop.ml` with `avar_env : (string * Tir.var) list` to propagate `let x = y` variable aliases in addition to `let x = literal`. Excludes closure types (`TFn`/`TVar`) to protect ECallPtr dispatch (llvm_emit is name-sensitive for closures). Uses `~allow_avar:false` for ECallPtr's function argument. 3 new tests in `cprop` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P12.
- ✅ **FBIP cross-tag constructor reuse (P8)** — Extended Perceus FBIP to reuse heap cells across constructors with the same field count (not just the same constructor name). `add_scrutinee_free_for` encodes the consumed ctor's arity as dummy `TUnit` type-args in the DecRC var's type (`TCon(qualified_tag, [TUnit; …])`). `same_arity` replaces `shape_matches` in `try_fbip_sink` and `fbip_expr`, comparing `List.length dummy_args = List.length new_alloc_args`. Cross-ctor reuse is safe: March allocates `[tag + nfields × ptr]` blocks so same-arity ⟺ same block size; the new tag is written into the reused cell by llvm_emit. 5 new tests in `fbip_p8` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P8.
- ✅ **Let-floating past ECase / join points (P1) — Layer 1 (alpha-merge, pre-Perceus)** — `lib/tir/join_points.ml`: hoists `let` bindings appearing identically at the start of EVERY case branch above the `ECase`. Two entry points: `run` (conservative, identical binder names — runs first in `lib/tir/opt.ml`'s post-Perceus fixed-point loop) and `run_pre` (Layer 1 — allows arms to bind the shared RHS under *different* fresh ANF names, floating one `$jp…` binder and substituting each arm's binder onto it via `rename_expr`). `run_pre` is wired in `bin/main.ml` BEFORE `Perceus.perceus` (renaming RC-decorated bindings post-Perceus is unsafe — double-free class) so RC is inserted once for the hoisted binding. Because TIR is in ANF, this captures the spec's motivating shared-inline-subexpr example. 6 tests in `join_points` group (`pre_float_alpha`, `pre_no_float_diff_rhs` added). See `specs/optimizations.md` §P1.
- [ ] **P1 Layer 2 — non-leading / interleaved common lets** — Today only the *first* let of each arm is peeled. Generalize to a common pure binding anywhere in an arm's leading let-chain whose RHS does not depend on arm-specific earlier bindings, with reordering. Medium. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P1.
- [ ] **P1 Layer 3 — general non-let-bound cross-arm CSE** — Hoist shared sub-expressions ANF did not already lift to a head let; gate on `Purity.is_pure`. Mostly subsumed by ANF, low marginal value. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P1.
- [ ] **P8 Layer 2 — delayed/non-immediate FBIP reuse** — Current cross-tag reuse only fires when destruction and reconstruction are adjacent; extend to reuse across intervening code. Medium-High. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P8.
- ✅ **`HashMap` stdlib module** — HAMT-backed persistent map using structural `==` and polymorphic `hash` — no comparator at any call site. Full API: `new`, `put`, `get`, `get_or`, `has`, `delete`, `size`, `fold`, `keys`, `values`, `entries`, `from_list`, `to_list`, `update`, `map_values`, `filter`, `merge`, `merge_with`. `stdlib/hash_map.march` (~430 lines); 40 tests in `test/stdlib/test_hash_map.march`. `Enum.uniq` and `Enum.uniq_by` rewritten O(n) via HashMap; `Enum.frequencies` rewritten O(n) with first-appearance order preserved; `test_helpers.ml` wired for enum tests. `docs/stdlib.md` and `docs/cookbook/basics.md` updated with HashMap reference.
- [ ] **Fold numeric builtins on literal args** — Extend `lib/tir/fold.ml` to constant-fold pure numeric builtins when all arguments are literals (mirrors the existing `string_byte_length("..")` / int-arith folds). LLVM *cannot* do this — these lower to opaque `call @march_*` instructions, not arithmetic, so the fold must happen at TIR level where the builtin's semantics are known. **Safe set (exact results, zero cross-libm bit-drift):** `int_pow`, `int_abs`, `float_abs`, `int_to_float`, `float_to_int` (truncation), `floor`, `ceil`, `round`. **`int_pow` must reuse the 63-bit range guard** from the `string_to_int` overflow fix — `int_pow(2, 100)` stays a runtime call rather than baking in a corrupt constant; likewise skip negative exponents and `/0`/`%0`. **Defer `sqrt`/`pow`** on non-trivial inputs: compile-time OCaml `libm` and runtime C `libm` aren't guaranteed to agree on the last ULP, so folding could embed an off-by-one-bit value. Each fold must bit-match the interpreter oracle. Lift: ~1 session (~50 lines + ~10 tests), low risk, additive — no new pass or pipeline wiring. Payoff: modest (only fires on builtins over literal constants, e.g. `let max_val = int_pow(2, 31) - 1`) but correctness-clean and removes work LLVM provably can't.
- ✅ **Boolean short-circuit absorber peepholes (P15)** — Extended `lib/tir/simplify.ml` with 3 patterns: `x && false = false` / `false && x = false` (absorber law for AND), `x || true = true` / `true || x = true` (absorber law for OR), `not true = false` / `not false = true` (literal folding). These fire after cprop propagates boolean constants into guards and conditionals. 5 new tests in `simplify` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P14.
- ✅ **Fold boolean comparison identities + integer comparison folding** — Extended `lib/tir/fold.ml` with: `x == true → x`, `true == x → x`, `x == false → not x`, `false == x → not x` (arise after inlining boolean predicates); and `a OP b → bool` for integer comparisons (`<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `==`, `!=`) on two literal arguments. 4 new tests in `fold` group.
- ✅ **EField of known record/update (P13)** — `lib/tir/cprop.ml`: separate `field_env` tracks `ERecord`/`EUpdate`/alias bindings; `EField(AVar r, k)` folds to the atom at position `k` when `r` is in fenv. `EUpdate` merges new fields over the base record's known fields. 2 new tests in `cprop` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P13.
- ✅ **Extended simplify peepholes (P14)** — `lib/tir/simplify.ml`: `if x then true else false → x`, `if x then false else true → not x`, `x == x → true` (non-float), `x != x → false` (non-float). 5 new tests in `simplify` group. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P14.
- ✅ **Boolean absorber peepholes + integer-literal comparison folding (P15)** — `lib/tir/simplify.ml`: `x && false → false`, `false && x → false`, `x || true → true`, `true || x → true` (Boolean absorbers; 4 new tests). `lib/tir/fold.ml` (new file): boolean-comparison identities (`x == true → x`, `x == false → not x` and symmetric) and integer-literal comparison folding (`LitInt(a) OP LitInt(b) → LitBool(result)` for `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `==`, `!=`; fires from `cprop` literal-env hits). 5 new tests in `fold` group; folding integrated into `lib/tir/opt.ml` fixed-point loop.
- ✅ **P10 Phase 2 — NativeArray compiled path** — `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`: `native_int_arr_*` and `native_float_arr_*` builtins registered in `is_builtin_fn` (prevents erroneous RC wrapping), `builtin_ret_ty` (correct i64/double/ptr return types), and the LLVM preamble (16 explicit `declare` lines). `runtime/march_runtime.c`: `native_int_arr_from_list` now untags tagged-integer pointers (`(raw & 1) ? raw >> 1 : raw`) before writing to the flat array; `native_int_arr_to_list` tags before storing (`(v << 1) | 1`) so March's List(Int) untagging recovers the correct value. 2 new IR-level tests in `native_arrays` group confirming all builtins appear with correct LLVM types and call instruction return types. See `specs/optimizations.md` §P10.
- ✅ **P6 Milestone 2 — Option-shaped niche encoding** — Option-like types (one nullary ctor + one single-field ctor) use `None=0, Some(x)=x` — no heap allocation for either variant. `lib/tir/repr.ml`: `Repr` extended with `Niche { payload; tagged }` variant; `niche_payload_ok` admits `TInt`, `TBool`, `TString`, `TPtr`, and heap-Boxed types (all guaranteed nonzero in a ptr slot); excludes `TFloat` (0.0 bitcasts to null), `TUnit` (raw 0), `TVar` (unknown), and nested Niche types (Some(None)=0=None). `payload_needs_tag` covers `TBool` alongside `TInt`. `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`: `EAlloc` niche fast path emits tagged i64→ptr for scalar payloads, bare ptr for pointer payloads, or null (i1 false→ptr) for the nullary ctor. `EReuse` skips reuse for niche types (no heap cell to reuse). `emit_case` Niche arm: null-check dispatch, then `ptrtoint+ashr 1` for tagged scalars or bare `ptrtoint` for pointers. `llvm_param_ty` suppresses `nonnull dereferenceable(16)` on niche-typed parameters. `record_get` special case passes a payload `kind` char (`'i'` Int/Bool, `'p'` heap ptr, `'f'` Float, `'g'` generic) as 3rd arg to `march_record_get`. Runtime: `runtime/march_extras.c` — `make_some(val)=val`, `make_none()=(void*)0`, kind-aware `rec_some_k`/`rec_none_k` (scalar/ptr → niche null, Float/generic → boxed heap cell); `march_record_get` accepts `int64_t expected_kind` and calls these. New native integration test `test/native/option_niche.march`. 6 new repr unit tests (`niche_int`, `niche_string`, `niche_bool`, `niche_float_boxed`, `niche_unit_boxed`, `nested_niche_boxed`). Adversarial regression test: vault scalar (Bool/Int) round-trips. All tests pass: compiler 281 + eval 224 + codegen 317 (315 pass; 2 pre-existing str_concat simplify failures) + stdlib 782.

- ✅ **P6 Milestone 1 — Unboxed single-field constructors (newtypes)** — Single-variant single-field ADTs (`type Counter = Counter(Int)`) are now stored as their raw payload instead of a heap cell. `lib/tir/repr.ml`: new `Repr` module with `repr_of_ty` (classifies monomorphic TCon as `Newtype payload | Boxed`) and `payload_needs_tag` (returns true for `TInt`/`TBool` and newtypes-over-scalars). Scalar payloads are tagged `(v<<1)|1` so `IS_HEAP_PTR` never mistakes raw integers for heap pointers; Float-payload newtypes stay `Boxed` (tagging float bits is unsafe). `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`: three fast paths — `EAlloc` for construction (tag & inttoptr for scalar, coerce-to-ptr for pointer payloads); `EReuse` for FBIP-generated reconstruct (skip RC reuse, emit new payload directly with manual DecRC of old box); `emit_case` for matching (ptrtoint + ashr to recover scalar, bare ptr coerce for pointer payloads; strip Perceus-inserted `EDecRC` inside the branch since ownership transfer is net-zero). Global `cur_type_defs` ref keeps `repr_of_ty` available without threading params through all emit functions. New native integration test `test/native/newtype_counter.march` (Counter(Int): `bump`/`unwrap` across function boundaries; expected output 42). All tests pass: compiler 281 + eval 224 + codegen 309 (307 pass; 2 pre-existing str_concat simplify failures) + stdlib 782 + native newtype_counter green.

## Done (recently completed)

- ✅ **`march_project_root` off-by-one — resolved to `_build`, not the repo root (`test/test_helpers.ml`, `test/test_ir_verify.ml`, 2026-07-02)** — the helper was `Filename.dirname` ×3 of `Sys.executable_name`, which for `<root>/_build/default/test/run_*.exe` lands on `<root>/_build` — one dirname short. Its doc comment claimed the compiled-regression tests `cd` there "so the compiler resolves its CWD-relative runtime/ and stdlib/ directories", but that never happened: from CWD `<root>/_build` the compiler's CWD-relative `stdlib`/`runtime` candidates are dead, so resolution silently depended entirely on the exe-relative `_build/default/` copies (populated only by a FULL `dune build` — after a targeted `dune build test/run_codegen.exe bin/main.exe` every compiled-regression test failed with confusing stdlib-missing errors), `--compile` runs dropped `.march/cas` trees inside `_build/`, and the `march_project_root`-based fallbacks in `test_snapshots.ml`/`test_ir_verify.ml` were dead. Rewritten to walk the exe's ancestors to the parent of its `_build` directory, verified by `dune-project` at that root, loud `Alcotest.failf` otherwise (never silently wrong; correct at any depth, incl. dune `.sandbox` trees). Verified by the CAS relocation (`.march/cas` now created at the repo root, none in `_build/`); codegen 346, stdlib 791 (Slow incl.), snapshots — all exit 0.

- ✅ **Z3 solver process leak — one immortal `z3 -in` orphaned per compiler/test process (`lib/refine/solver.ml`, `lib/refine/refine.ml`, `bin/main.ml`, 2026-07-02)** — two compounding defects: (1) `Refine.shared_solver`, the lazily-created per-process singleton behind every `Refine.discharge`, was never closed on any production path (`Solver.close` had zero callers outside two test files), so every `march --check`/`--compile` run and every test binary left its z3 child running at exit; (2) `Solver.create` built its pipes without `~cloexec:true`, so the z3 child inherited the write end of its own stdin pipe — after the parent died the pipe never delivered EOF and the orphan blocked in `read(0)` forever (1,174 immortal orphans at 0% CPU, ages 2h–24h, found reparented to PID 1 on 2026-07-02). Fixed: cloexec on all four pipe ends; `Solver.close` SIGKILLs before `waitpid` (reaping can never block on a wedged solver); new `Refine.shutdown` closes + clears the singleton, registered `at_exit` on first spawn and called explicitly at the end of both refinement-pass scopes in `bin/main.ml`; `discharge` supervises the solver (dead/wedged → reap, respawn once, then mark unavailable for the run); SIGPIPE ignored (only if at default) so solver death is a catchable error, not parent death. Regression tests in `test/test_refine.ml` ("lifecycle" suite, pgrep-based, loud-skip without z3): spawn-count check (3 discharges → 1 child during, 0 after `shutdown`) and an orphan probe (re-exec via `MARCH_Z3_ORPHAN_PROBE=1`, exit without cleanup, assert z3 dies ≤5s; verified RED against pre-fix pipes). End-to-end verified with a `MARCH_Z3` pid-logging shim: one z3 per `--check` run, reaped before exit. Full suite green (compiler 384, eval 230, codegen 337, stdlib 792 incl. Slow, stdlib_march 53, refinecheck 66, refine 16).

- ✅ **P0: RC underflow (double-free) when the SAME scrutinee is re-matched inside a sibling arm's sub-path (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`, 2026-07-02)** — found during Wave 2 Task 4's TIR snapshot audit (`test/snapshots/perceus/scrutinee_borrowed_conservatism.expected`), fixed here. Minimal repro (compiled, `xs = Nil`):
  ```march
  mod MinimalScrutDbl do
    fn f(xs : List(Int), flag : Bool) : Int do
      match xs do
        Cons(h, t) ->
          if flag do
            h
          else
            match xs do
              Cons(h2, _) -> h2
              Nil -> 0
            end
          end
        Nil -> -1
      end
    end
    fn main() do println(f(Nil, true)) end
  end
  ```
  aborted with `march: RC underflow (rc was 0) at 0x... — aborting` (exit 134) when compiled; the interpreter printed `-1` correctly (compiled-only). Root cause (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`'s `ECase` handling, `insert_rc_expr`): the `Cons` arm re-matches the SAME scrutinee `xs` inside its `else` sub-path, so the documented "scrutinee-borrowed conservatism" (~line 1058-1080; a deliberately path-INSENSITIVE approximation the comment calls "memory-safe, minor leak") marks `xs` borrowed for the `Cons` arm and re-adds it to that arm's `live_before_br`. `add_cross_decrcs` (~line 1146-1182) then unions `live_before_br` across ALL sibling arms and emits a `dec_rc` at the head of any arm where a variable is "dead here, live elsewhere" — so the `Nil` arm (and the synthesized non-exhaustive-match `_` default) each got a cross-branch `dec_rc xs` **in addition to** the normal `add_scrutinee_free_for` scrutinee-free every arm already receives when the scrutinee is dead-after-case (~line 984-1017), producing two `dec_rc xs` on the same reference. **Fix:** `add_cross_decrcs`'s `dead_here` computation now excludes the scrutinee's own variable name (derived from the `ECase`'s atom) from the cross-branch liveness set — the scrutinee's fate is exclusively owned by `add_scrutinee_free_for`, which already correctly decides per-arm whether to free it, so it must never be treated as ordinary cross-branch "dead here, live elsewhere" liveness (that liveness was itself only an artifact of the scrutinee-borrowed re-add, not a real use by the sibling arm). The Case-2 "leak, not crash" conservatism (single sub-path scrutinee use, no re-match) is untouched; niche/newtype scrutinee-free skip and the `$fbip$` arity-marker encoding are unmodified. `MARCH_DEBUG_PERCEUS=1` confirms exactly 60 fewer `dec_rc` insertions module-wide (14766→14706), `inc`/`cancelled`/`reuse` unchanged; the repro now exits 0 printing `-1`, matching the interpreter. Snapshot regenerated (`UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS=1 ./run_snapshots.exe`): `test/snapshots/perceus/scrutinee_borrowed_conservatism.expected` loses exactly the two redundant `dec_rc xs;` lines — audited byte-for-byte; no other snapshot in the 29-test corpus churned. New regression test `test/test_codegen.ml`'s `scrutinee_borrowed_cross_branch_dec_codegen` suite (compiled + interpreter parity, exit 0). **All six runners green: 384 compiler / 230 eval / 347 codegen (+1) / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots.** RC benchmarks unaffected (tree_transform/list_ops/binary_trees/mutual_recursion all correct output, timings within noise). Bonus re-test of the known pre-existing `bench/heapsort.march` crash: still crashes both before and after this fix (same call site, `Sort.heap_merge_h`, confirmed via `lldb`), but the exit code/signal changed (134 RC-underflow → 139 SIGSEGV) because this fix shifted the binary's memory layout — the underlying bug is a separate, deeper, pre-existing RC issue in `heap_merge_h` unrelated to this fix (a small hand-reduced repro of the same merge shape does not crash; only the full 10,000-element heapsort volume triggers it), not introduced by this change.
- ✅ **W2.0 — kill vacuous greens: in-repo failures fail loudly, only genuine tool-absence may skip (`test/test_helpers.ml`, `test/test_codegen.ml`, `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`, 2026-07-02)** — Wave 2 Task 2. Three vacuous-green classes fixed (harness-only; `lib/` untouched). **(1)** `setup_jit_runtime` returned `None` on ANY `.so` link failure with stderr discarded, indistinguishable from "clang not installed" — RED-demonstrated via file-copy sabotage (temporarily dropping `march_dispatch.c` from the runtime source list, confirmed to genuinely break the link) showing the codegen/stdlib suites reported full "Test Successful" (341/791 tests) anyway. Fixed: `clang_available ()` PATH probe distinguishes legitimate skip (counted via `record_jit_skip` + an `at_exit` summary printer) from a real link failure (`Alcotest.failf` with the captured linker stderr, no longer redirected to `/dev/null`). **(2)** ~23 near-identical `if not (Sys.file_exists main_exe) then () else ...` / `if compile_rc <> 0 then ()` silent-skip guards across both files consolidated into shared helpers (`find_main_exe`, `march_project_root`, `compile_march`/`compile_march_or_skip`/`compile_march_raw`) with the same clang-absence-vs-real-failure policy. **(3)** Making (2) loud immediately caught two previously-hidden issues: a test using an unparseable `Int.min` literal (fixed: compute arithmetically instead of via a literal whose magnitude exceeds March's max positive literal) and a genuine `lib/tir/mono.ml` bug ("Monomorphization limit reached" compiling a trivial self-recursive nested closure) — out of scope to fix here, converted to a documented, message-matched `Alcotest.skip` (P0 entry added above). **(4)** New canary `test_setup_jit_runtime_gate_is_live` fails if the JIT gate is ever silently broken again. Step-1 sabotage re-run post-fix now correctly FAILS the suite (was vacuously green); restored clean. **384 compiler / 227 eval / 342 codegen (+1) / 791 stdlib (790 pass + 1 documented skip) / 53 stdlib_march all exit 0.**

- ✅ **record_put/record_update_dyn int-vs-pointer misclassification (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, `runtime/march_extras.c`, 2026-07-02)** — compiled `record_put(r, k, v)` SIGSEGV'd for any even Int `v >= 4096`: the call site passed scalars in NATURAL (untagged) repr and `rec_field_norm_in`'s `REC_PLAUSIBLE_HEAP` sniff misclassified them as heap pointers, so `march_incrc` dereferenced the integer's value (reported as a "TCO loop crashes above 10k iterations" — really the value `i*2` crossing 4096). - ✅ **Archive task MARCH_LIB_PATH parity with `forge build` (`forge/lib/archive_store.ml`, 2026-07-02)** — `lib_paths_for_root` (used by `find_task` to assemble MARCH_LIB_PATH for `forge <archive>.<task>`) omitted the archive root's `config/` and `.forge/generated/` directories, unlike `cmd_build.lib_path_env` — so an archive task whose modules import `Config` (e.g. forgepm's seed task with forgepm registered as a path archive) failed typecheck with "Unknown module `Config`". - ✅ **Distributed OTP — P4 (load gossip + work dispatch) + P5 (anti-entropy, cross-node monitors, dist supervisors) (`stdlib/swim_driver.march`, `stdlib/cluster_load.march`, `stdlib/work_dispatch.march`, `stdlib/global_registry.march`, `stdlib/net_kernel.march`, `stdlib/dist_link.march`, `stdlib/dist_supervisor.march`, `runtime/march_monitor_registry.{h,c}`, `runtime/march_runtime.c`, 2026-06-25)** — two independent deliverables landed together. **P4 (L6 load-aware dispatch):** `ClusterLoad.NodeLoad` gains a `sampled_at : Int` timestamp; `to_ints`/`from_ints` wire-codec updated to 5 elements; `local/2` takes a caller-supplied `now_ms` for testability. `SwimDriver` extended with `peer_loads : Map(String, NodeLoad)`, `GossipWithLoad(Member, NodeLoad)` event, `SwimGossipLoad` wire msg (tag=3, 5-element array with piggybacked load), `load_stale_ms()` / `anti_entropy_period_ms()` constants, `anti_entropy_peers`/`bump_anti_entropy`/`peer_load`/`fresh_peer_loads` public API (anti-entropy timer without changing `step`'s return type). `WorkDispatch` module: `pick_dispatch_target` (power-of-two-choices over fresh `NodeLoad` samples), `pick_two` (caller-sampled indices), `dispatch` (load-aware RPC with local fallback + `NoConnection`/`DeadlineExceeded` retry), `dispatch_affine` (ConsistentHash affinity with load-aware fallback). **P5a (Merkle anti-entropy):** `GlobalRegistry` gains `root_hash` (Merkle tree over sorted name→entry pairs), `diff_entries` (CRDT join when hashes differ, no-op when equal), `all_entries` (full snapshot for wire transfer), plus the private `entry_to_bytes` serialiser. **P5c (cross-node monitors):** `net_kernel.march` adds MONITOR_REQ (tag=7) and MONITOR_FIRE (tag=8) frame codecs + `frame_tag` peek helper. New `runtime/march_monitor_registry.{h,c}`: mutex-guarded per-pid watcher table, `march_dist_monitor_register`/`fire_pid`/`fire_nodedown`/`clear_fd`, mini msgpack encoder for MONITOR_FIRE frames. `march_runtime.c` hooks `march_dist_monitor_fire_pid` on actor exit. `stdlib/dist_link.march`: `DownReason = Normal | Killed | Crash(String) | NodeDown`, `MonitorRef = MonitorRef(Int)`, `PendingMonitor`, `MonitorTable`, wire codecs (encode/decode req + fire), table operations, `make_monitor` factory, `impl Eq(MonitorRef)` + `impl Eq(DownReason)`. **P5d (dist supervisors):** `stdlib/dist_supervisor.march`: `RestartStrategy = Permanent | Transient | Temporary`, `SupervisionStrategy = OneForOne | OneForAll | RestForOne`, `should_restart`, `restart_indices`, `find_child_by_ref`, `update_child`, `child_count`. Tests: `test_swim_driver.march` +9 tests (SwimGossipLoad round-trip, msg_to_event, peer_loads-via-step, stale detection, anti_entropy_peers, bump_anti_entropy); `test_global_registry.march` +8 tests (root_hash properties, diff_entries merge); `test_work_dispatch.march` (11 tests: pick_dispatch_target, pick_two, ClusterLoad scorers); `test_dist_link.march` (17 tests: reason codecs, MONITOR_REQ wire, MONITOR_FIRE wire, MonitorTable ops); `test_dist_supervisor.march` (11 tests: should_restart × 3 strategies, restart_indices × 3 strategies, find_child_by_ref, update_child, make). All 53 stdlib_march test suites green (was 49; +3 new suites = 52; swim_driver + global_registry existing suites extended). Full suite green — compiler 321, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786, stdlib_march 53/53.

- ✅ **`node_rpc` interpreter gap closed — `remote_check`/`remote_invoke` eval builtins (`lib/eval/eval.ml`, 2026-06-23)** — `node_rpc.march`'s `handle_request` C-registry fallback called `remote_check`/`remote_invoke`, which were codegen-only → `unbound variable: remote_check` under the interpreter (`test_stdlib_march` 46/47). Added them as eval builtins consistent with the interpreter's empty/no-op registry (`remote_register_stub`/`remote_count` are already no-ops): `remote_check` → 0 (NoTarget), `remote_invoke` → None — matching `march_remote_check_march` on an empty registry and the "unknown target yields NoTarget" test. `test_stdlib_march` now 47/47; full suite green (compiler 321, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786).

- ✅ **Capacity-aware routing demo — end-to-end cross-host (`demo/load_server.march`, `demo/load_client.march`, `scripts/rpc/route-smoke.sh`, 2026-06-23)** — closes the original goal: a client queries each node's live CPU/memory headroom over RPC (`report_load`), picks the node with most spare CPU via `ClusterLoad.pick_by`, and routes the expensive call there. Verified cross-host: `load_server` cross-compiled for linux/amd64 on a 1-core droplet; the Mac client (14 cores, heavily loaded) queried both over an ssh tunnel and routed `square(7)=49` to the droplet (more spare CPU). Fixed a demo naming collision (`connect` helper vs `Socket.connect` → renamed `dial`). Local smoke test added. Full suite green. Depends on the whole arc: resource builtins + ClusterLoad + niche codegen fix + stable cross-binary RPC impl_hash.

- ✅ **RPC CAS impl_hash stable across separate compilations (`lib/cas/serialize.ml`, `bin/main.ml`, `test/native/rpc_hash_*`, 2026-06-23)** — the two-node RPC admission check (`remote_ref_hashes` + `march_remote_register`) previously only worked for trivial leaf functions: a non-leaf body (calling stdlib) got a different impl_hash in the caller vs server binary, so admission returned NoTarget and the call hung. Two root causes fixed: (1) the impl serializer hashed generated temp-variable names verbatim → now alpha-normalizes bound variables to binding indices (free callee names kept), so the hash is invariant to temp-name counters that vary with surrounding code; (2) RPC hashes were taken from post-opt SCC hashes for live functions but pre-opt `hash_fn_def` for eliminated ones → now built uniformly from pre-opt `hash_fn_def` for all functions (a dedicated `rpc_impl_hashes`/`rpc_sig_hashes` passed to `emit_module`, separate from the HCR map). Result: identical impl_hash across separate client/server compilations of the same non-trivial source; a real `report_load` RPC over TCP returns metrics instead of hanging. NoTarget already returns an error (verified). Regression: two separately-compiled modules with an identical stdlib-calling `probe` (one live via `__rpc_stub`, one eliminated with padding) must print equal impl_hashes. Full suite green (compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786; rpc_auto_enroll + node_call_loopback pass). Unblocks the capacity-aware routing demo.

- ✅ **Codegen hardening — niche match on an unresolved (`'_`) scrutinee type (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, `test/native/lazy_niche`, 2026-06-23)** — fixes a silent miscompile/segfault: a lazily-loaded stdlib module (not in `stdlib_file_list`) has its body lowered without type inference, so a `match List.nth_opt(...)`-style niche-`Option` match reached codegen with a `TVar "_"` scrutinee → `repr_of_ty` returned `Boxed` → heap-tag match strategy dereferenced a tagged int → crash. `emit_case` now recovers the niche shape from the branch constructors and, for an unresolved scrutinee, uses the niche strategy with `tagged=false` (payload taken verbatim; the erased-i64 convention conditionally untags it at concrete use sites). Regression fixture `stdlib/lazy_niche_probe.march` (intentionally excluded from `stdlib_file_list`) + native compile-and-diff test. Full suite green (compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786). Complements the earlier `cluster_load.march` `stdlib_file_list` registration — that fixed the one module; this hardens codegen for *any* lazily-loaded niche match.

- ✅ **Per-node CPU/memory introspection + `ClusterLoad` capacity-routing API (`stdlib/cluster_load.march`, `stdlib/system.march`, 2026-06-23)** — lets a language user route an expensive call to whichever cluster node has CPU/memory headroom. New cross-platform `sys_*` builtins (`sys_cpu_load_milli`, `sys_mem_total_bytes`, `sys_mem_available_bytes`) sample real local resources (runtime/march_extras.c via getloadavg/sysctl/host_statistics64 on macOS, getloadavg/sysinfo//proc/meminfo on Linux; wired through llvm_emit + defun + eval), exposed as `System.cpu_load_milli/0` etc. `ClusterLoad` provides `NodeLoad`, `local/1` sampling, `spare_cpu_milli`/`spare_mem_mb` scorers, `pick_by/2` (argmax with caller-supplied selector — raw metrics exposed), and `to_ints`/`from_ints` wire marshalling. **Also fixed a silent-miscompile compiler bug:** a new stdlib module not registered in `stdlib_file_list` is loaded lazily (signatures only, no body typecheck) → expression types stay `'_` → niche-`Option` matches lower to the heap-tag strategy → segfault; fixed by registering `cluster_load.march`. Full suite green (compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786); deeper lazy-load hardening tracked separately.

- ✅ **Two-host RPC over TCP — name-resolution + remote-hash fixes, Linux portability, repeatable harness (`demo/two_node_server.march`, `demo/two_node_client.march`, `scripts/rpc/`, 2026-06-23)** — two independent executables (not one in-process demo) doing real RPC: the server registers `square` via the `__rpc_stub` mechanism and serves a TCP accept loop; the client connects, derives the CAS hash pair with `remote_ref_hashes`, and calls `square(7)`/`square(12)` over the wire → `49`/`144`. **Two compiler fixes made this work:** (1) `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` now resolves a bare call against `ctx.top_fns` **before** `unqualified_fns` at every call site, so a same-module top-level function (the server's recursive `serve_loop`) shadows a cross-module suffix match (`NodeCall.serve_loop`) — previously the server exited right after `tcp_listen`; (2) `bin/main.ml` adds a second hash pass over all remaining pre-opt functions so `remote_ref_hashes` constant-folds to the correct hash even when the caller-side `pfn` is optimized away (previously it folded to empty strings → admission `NoTarget` → call failed). **Linux portability:** `/dev/urandom` fallback (`march_runtime.c`), `_GNU_SOURCE` for `MAP_ANONYMOUS`/`RTLD_DEFAULT` (`march_scheduler.c`, `ffi_marshal.c`, `eval_ffi_stubs.c`), `dlopen` `./`-prefix for bare `.so`, and `-lLLVM-${VER}` (`detect_llvm.sh`) — all `#if defined(__linux__)`-guarded. **Harness:** `scripts/rpc/{smoke-local,build-linux,deploy-droplet}.sh` (local assert 49/144; Docker `linux/amd64` cross-compile; droplet-localhost + macOS-client-over-`ssh -L`-tunnel cross-host proofs). Full macOS suite green (compiler 318, eval 224, codegen 320, stdlib 786). Known-orthogonal pre-existing gap: `node_rpc` eval test fails `unbound variable: remote_check` (admission builtins are codegen-only, not yet interpreter builtins — tracked separately).

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 compiler-emitted `@main` enroll/stub integration test (`test/native/rpc_auto_enroll.march`, 2026-06-23)** — closes the L4 feature loop: the compiler auto-registers marshalling stubs at program startup without any user-written `remote_register_stub` call. Server defines `add(a, b) : Int` + `pfn add__rpc_stub` (MessagePack shim); compiler (a) detects the `__rpc_stub` suffix and pins stubs in `tm_exports` so `Dce.run` keeps them alive, (b) saves `pre_opt_tir` before `Opt.run` so trivially-inlined base functions (`add = a + b`) can still be hashed, (c) for each stub whose base is absent from `hr_impl_hashes` after CAS hashing, looks it up in `pre_opt_tir` via `March_cas.Hash.hash_fn_def`, and (d) emits `march_remote_register(impl_hash, sig_hash, &add__rpc_stub)` in `@main`. Two new builtins (`remote_check`, `remote_invoke`) lower to `march_remote_check_march` / `march_remote_invoke_march` in `runtime/march_remote_registry.c`; `NodeRpc.handle_request` falls through to them on `Targets` miss. Client calls `NodeCall.call` with `remote_ref_hashes("RpcAutoEnroll", "add")`, receives `result:15` (= 7 + 8). Port 29851. Test added to `test/dune`. All 1630 tests pass.

- ✅ **`stdlib/ring_buf.march` — mutable RingBuf with non-sendable enforcement (2026-06-23)** — `RingBuf(a)`: fixed-capacity circular buffer backed by the existing `'a ring` OCaml struct in `eval.ml`. All 10 operations O(1); allocation-free after `make()`. `push` overwrites oldest when full. `pop` removes oldest (FIFO). Index 0 = oldest. Shared-reference semantics within one actor. `VRingBuf of value ring` value constructor (ring type hoisted before `value` ADT); `ring_pop_oldest` helper added. `check_sendable`/`non_sendable_types` added to `typecheck.ml`; fires at every `send()` callsite and rejects `RingBuf` payloads with a diagnostic pointing to actor init state as the correct sharing mechanism. 28 tests in `test/stdlib/test_ring_buf.march`. All quick-suite tests pass.

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — P2 two-node SWIM discovery integration test (`test/native/node_discovery.march`, 2026-06-22)** — end-to-end integration test of the authenticated peer-connection + SWIM discovery stack over real TCP loopback. `node-a` accepts, `node-b` connects; both run `ClusterConn.handshake` over the live socket and confirm each other in their `PeerRegistry`; `node-a` sends a `SwimPing` via `SwimDriver.encode_msg`, `node-b` replies with `SwimPingAck`, and both call `Swim.on_gossip` + `Membership.alive_members` to confirm the peer is `Alive` (`live_peers=1`). All I/O is real TCP; the green-thread scheduler interleaves acceptor and connector naturally. Also fixed `swim_driver.march` bug: `String.of_int` → `String.from_int` in `int_to_status` and `decode_msg` (the undefined symbol was silently preventing linker resolution of those functions from other modules). Test added to `test/dune` as a native compile-and-diff rule on port 29760.

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 Node.call loopback (`test/native/node_call_loopback.march`, 2026-06-22)** — integration test for the full RPC call path: NodeCall transport shim (`stdlib/node_call.march`) layered over NodeRpc dispatcher and TCP sockets. Three scenarios over real TCP on port 29850: matching hashes → `ok:7`, sig_hash mismatch → `err:TypeMismatch`, unknown target → `err:NoTarget`. Both server and client in one process, interleaved by the green-thread scheduler. `NodeCall.serve_one` / `NodeCall.call` wired to `NodeRpc.handle_frame` / `RemoteCall.interpret`. Test added to `test/dune` as a native compile-and-diff rule.

- ✅ **FBIP niche self-reference fix (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, 2026-06-22)** — The FBIP (frame-based in-place) optimization in `llvm_emit.ml` wrongly generated a self-referential object for niche-encoded patterns like `Some(result) -> Ok(result)`. For niche `Option`, the payload IS the scrutinee (no wrapper allocation), so the FBIP reuse branch overwrote the payload's own structure (`store ptr result, ptr result+16`) creating a cycle. The fresh branch also wrongly called `march_decrc` on the reuse atom, decrementing the very value about to be stored as Ok's field. Fix: in the `EReuse (reuse_atom, TCon(ctor,_), args)` handler's fallback branch (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`), detect when `reuse_atom`'s parent type is niche-shaped (`Repr.is_niche_shaped`) and skip FBIP entirely — allocate fresh without touching the reuse atom's RC (ownership flows from niche payload → branch var → Ok field). Root cause: Perceus's `add_scrutinee_free_for` insertes `EDecRC(scrutinee)` because the scrutinee and branch var have different TIR names even though they're the same runtime pointer under niche encoding; `args_alias_reuse` can't catch value aliasing. Verified: `dune build` clean, 300 compiler + 320 codegen tests all pass. This unblocks `NetKernel.recv_frame` which was hanging due to the self-referential Ok object confusing `List.length` in the caller.

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — clustering & RPC reference guide (`docs/clustering.md`, 2026-06-22)** — 391-line reference covering all five distributed layers: node identity (`NodeIdentity`, `NodeId = sha256(pubkey)`, incarnation), authenticated handshake (`ClusterAuth` HMAC challenge/response, `Handshake` nonce binding), `Membership` CRDT (incarnation-ordered merge, SWIM `Alive|Suspect|Dead` states), SWIM failure detection (`Swim` pure FSM + `SwimDriver` I/O glue, probe/indirect/gossip lifecycle), `GlobalRegistry` CRDT (vector-clock per name, deterministic concurrent tiebreak), `GlobalPid` wire type, `RemoteRef`/`CallError` taxonomy, and the `NodeRpc` responder pattern. Notes the current pure-module status (no live socket wiring yet) and what remains for P2 (two-node integration test) and P3 (compiler-emitted enroll/stub).

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 compiler enroll/stub (`remote_ref_hashes` + `remote_register_stub` + `remote_count` builtins, 2026-06-22)** — the compiler touchpoint that closes the CAS-hash gap: functions now carry their real identity into the runtime. Three new builtins wired through all three layers (typecheck `builtin_bindings`, eval `builtin_env`, codegen). **`remote_ref_hashes(mod, fn)`** is constant-folded at codegen time — the `emit_expr` handler intercepts the `EApp` before it becomes an LLVM call, looks up the Blake3 64-char hex hashes from the CAS pipeline's `hash_module` output, and emits a heap-allocated `(String, String)` tuple. Two fixes were required: (1) added all three builtins to `defun.ml`'s `builtin_names` so defun doesn't wrap them in closures (keeping them as `EApp`, not `ECallPtr`); (2) top-level user-module functions have no module prefix in their TIR `fn_name` (`"fib"` not `"TestRRH.fib"`) so the lookup tries the unqualified name when the qualified lookup misses. **`remote_register_stub(impl, sig, stub)`** maps to `march_remote_register` (runtime C registry). **`remote_count()`** maps to `march_remote_count`. **`march_remote_init()`** is auto-emitted in `@main` before user code runs. Eval path uses FNV-1a 32-char hex (deterministic, non-empty). Tests: 8 eval tests in `test_stdlib_march` (`test_remote_ref_hashes.march`) + 1 compiled native test (`test/native/remote_ref_hashes.march` — verifies 64-char Blake3 hashes with a recursive `fib` to survive DCE). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.

- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 function-by-identity registry (`runtime/march_remote_registry.{c,h}`, 2026-06-22)** — increment 3a: the one genuinely net-new runtime mechanism (the spec flagged `runtime/` had no function-by-identity registry). A mutex-guarded chained hash table keyed by a target's CAS `impl_hash` → its marshalling stub (`void *`), recording `sig_hash` for the admission check. `register` (idempotent on identity) / `lookup` / `sig_hash` / `has` / `count` / `init` / `shutdown`. Distinct from the HCR `march_dispatch` table (NAME_ID-indexed + versioned); this is identity-keyed for cross-node dispatch. Linked into compiled binaries at both `bin/main.ml` sites; CAS key auto-includes it (dir-glob). 7-group C unit test (`test/test_remote_registry.c`, in `dune runtest`); codegen 320 still green. **Next L4:** `remote_ref` lowering (sig_hash/impl_hash from CAS — Touchpoint 1), serializable-signature gate, compiler-emitted marshalling stubs + `@main` register calls + DCE pinning, then wire to the live net-kernel socket. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 RPC dispatcher (`node_rpc.march`, 2026-06-22)** — increment 2 of L4: the pure receive-side dispatcher + caller-side logic between the net-kernel transport and a node's enrolled targets. `Target = {sig_hash, impl_hash, invoke : args-bytes -> result-bytes}`; `Targets` is an injected `name → Target` table (modelling what the compiler-emitted enroll/stub mechanism must supply). `handle_request` → lookup → `RemoteCall.verify` admission → invoke-or-map-failure (`NoTarget` / `TypeMismatch` / `VersionSkew` / `RemoteExit` / `Returned`); `handle_frame` decodes + dispatches. Caller side: `interpret`, `matches` (correlation), `timed_out`, `peer_down_error`. Added `NoTarget` to `RemoteCall.CallError`. 12 TDD asserts with a real invoker stub; pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 43→44). **Next (final L4 increment):** the enroll/stub/registry runtime mechanism (function-by-identity registry + marshalling stubs + DCE pinning + `remote_ref` builtin — Compiler Touchpoint 1), then wire to the live net-kernel socket. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L4 remote-call safety core (`global_pid.march` + `remote_call.march`, 2026-06-22)** — the first increment of the headline RPC layer: the wire protocol + CAS admission decision (enroll/stub runtime mechanism + socket transport are follow-on increments). `GlobalPid` (`Pid = {node_id, local_pid, creation}`): the serializable cluster-wide process handle (`creation` distinguishes a restarted node's reused pids); `make`/`eq` + MessagePack codec. `RemoteCall`: `RemoteRef = {module_name, fn_name, sig_hash, impl_hash}` + **the safety core `verify(local_sig, local_impl, fref)`** → `Accept` / `Reject(TypeMismatch)` (sig mismatch, never invoke) / `Reject(VersionSkew)` (body mismatch); `CallError` taxonomy (`DeadlineExceeded|NoConnection|RemoteExit|TypeMismatch|VersionSkew`) for failure isolation; full MessagePack codecs for `CallRequest`/`CallReply` (framable in a NetFrame). 13 TDD asserts; pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 41→43). **Next:** the enroll/stub/registry runtime mechanism (function-by-identity registry + marshalling stubs + DCE pinning + `remote_ref` builtin — Compiler Touchpoint 1), then `Node.call` dispatch + failure isolation over the net-kernel. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L5 global registry CRDT (`global_registry.march`, 2026-06-22)** — `GlobalRegistry`: the cluster-wide name registry as a composite CRDT mapping `name → {node_id, pid}`. Each binding carries a `VectorClock` (causally orders updates to that name) + a presence bit so `unregister` converges like an LWW-element-set removal. Per-name resolution: causally-newer wins (`VectorClock.compare` → `After`); older/`Equal` keeps existing (idempotent); `Concurrent` broken by a deterministic `(node_id, pid)` tiebreak → every replica converges. `merge` is associative/commutative/idempotent (the gossip/anti-entropy join). API: `register`/`unregister`/`lookup`/`contains`/`names`/`size`/`observe`/`merge`; built on the `VectorClock` primitive. 11 TDD asserts (`test_global_registry.march`: register/lookup, tombstone, causal override + stale-ignore, concurrent convergence both orders, idempotence, union, removal-converges). Pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 40→41). **Remaining:** ConsistentHash-partitioned ownership + Merkle anti-entropy (`diff()`); serializable `Pid` (H5); load-aware dispatch (L6). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L3 SWIM runtime driver (`swim_driver.march`, 2026-06-22)** — the I/O glue for the pure Swim FSM. `State = {Swim.State, Rng, period_ms, period_end}`; `Event = PingAck(node_id) | GossipFrame(Member) | PeerDown(node_id)`; `step(state, now, events)` drives probe periods (end → expire suspects → refute self → begin new with random target), mid-period indirect escalation, and event folding. `PeerDown` skips Suspect and marks Dead at the current incarnation so the verdict supersedes any prior Alive. Wire protocol: Msgpack arrays with integer tag dispatch (0=SwimPing, 1=SwimPingAck, 2=SwimPingReq, 3=SwimGossip); `encode_msg`/`decode_msg` round-trip. `dispatch`/`dispatch_all` fan Actions to peers via `PeerRegistry` + `NetKernel`. 17 TDD asserts; pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 44→45). Gotcha: `else if` chains are not yet implemented — use `match n do 0 -> ... end` for multi-branch integer dispatch. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — L3 SWIM probe loop (`swim.march`, 2026-06-22)** — the pure SWIM failure-detection state machine over the `Membership` CRDT. Each transition takes state + clock + injected randomness (probe target, helper set) and returns the next state plus the I/O `Action`s the runtime must perform (`SendPing`/`SendPingReq`/`Gossip`); the socket/timer glue lives in the runtime layer (same split as `NetKernel`/`ClusterConn`), keeping detection deterministic + unit-testable. Protocol period: `begin_period` → direct ping; overdue ack → `escalate_indirect` (k-way ping-req, once); period end unacked → Suspect + Gossip + arm suspect→dead timer; `expire_suspects` → Dead; `on_gossip` folds deltas (higher-incarnation Alive refutes + cancels the timer); `refute` bumps our incarnation when falsely suspected. All state rides `Membership.observe` (incarnation-ordered CRDT merge) so views converge order-independently. 11 TDD asserts (`test_swim.march`); pure + eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 39→40). Parser gotchas recorded: `init` is a reserved keyword; `let x = V` before a `(`-led tuple return glommed as application `V(...)` (fix: bind to bare name). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`.
- ✅ **Distributed OTP — P1 net-kernel (L1+L2) + L3 membership CRDT (2026-06-21)** — Eight new stdlib modules building the authenticated peer connection + member set, plus a compiler change to expose raw TCP accept. **L1/L2 (transport + net-kernel):** `net_frame` (`NetFrame`, length-prefixed partial-read-safe framing), `node_identity` (`NodeIdentity`, `{name, NodeId=sha256(pubkey), incarnation}` + msgpack codec), `cluster_auth` (`ClusterAuth`, shared-secret HMAC challenge/response), `handshake` (`Handshake`, `Hello` codec + `authenticate` — proof must bind to the nonce we issued), `net_kernel` (`NetKernel`, byte bridge `List(Int)`↔Socket `String` + framed `send_frame`/`recv_frame` + symmetric authenticated `handshake` over a socket), `peer_registry` (`PeerRegistry`, one-connection-per-peer table by NodeId), `cluster_conn` (`ClusterConn`, `listen`/`connect_to_peer`/`accept_one` lifecycle). **L3:** `membership` (`Membership`, SWIM-style member view `{Alive|Suspect|Dead, incarnation}` with a CRDT merge = lexicographic max over `(incarnation, severity)`; associative/commutative/idempotent so gossip converges). **Compiler:** `tcp_listen`/`tcp_accept` are exposed to March (`Int -> Result(Int, String)`) — typecheck sigs + `IO.NetListen` cap, eval `Unix` impls returning a tagged `Result` (the runtime + codegen already had the raw-int64 primitives; the typechecker didn't, so the server-accept side was unwritable). Exposed concurrently on `main`; an earlier branch commit that exposed them as `Int -> Int` was superseded by this Result-typed design. The C `march_tcp_listen/accept` still return raw int64, so the compiled path's codegen ret-type stays `TInt` — a known surface/runtime divergence for the (unexercised) compiled path. All pure logic eval-tested (`test_stdlib_march` 30→38); socket I/O typecheck-clean + runtime-exercised (eval harness doesn't run sockets, per `ChannelServer`). Also fixed `char.march` (Phase-2 `Char`-migration left `char_to_int(c:Char)` mismatching the `String`-typed builtin → realigned to String). Specs: `specs/2026-06-21-distributed-otp-design.md`, `specs/progress.md`. **Remaining:** SWIM probe loop (ping/timers), global registry CRDT (L5), load-aware dispatch (L6), a loopback integration test, constant-time proof compare.
- ✅ **Refinement types: record param refinements in postcondition VCs — Gap #1 Part B (2026-06-25)** — `lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`: the `scope` entry gains an optional record-sort tag `(binder, pred, sort_opt)`. `refined_scope_ty` admits both `Int` and registered-TyCon record refinements. `scope_add_param`/`scope_add_binding` use it; `sig_of_clause` keeps `refined_int_ty` (Decision R3 — no call-site VCs for record params). `scope_facts` returns `(decls, assumptions, has_record: bool)`: record entries declare an opaque `SData sort_name` const, build a `make_field_resolver`, and reflect the predicate via field selectors. `check_post` triggers the preamble when RETURN OR scope is record-sorted; a composite `scope_field_resolver` threads into `scalar` so `old.count` in the return resolves via selectors. With `scope_has_record = true`, a SAT counterexample from the first discharge is a real violation and is reported. `make_field_resolver` moved before `scope_facts`; `reflect_scalar` updated to 3-tuple. **62 tests** (61 pass; `resolution.0` pre-existing). B1: `keep(old:{s:State|s.count>=0}):{v:State|v.count>=0} → {count:old.count}` verifies; B2: `drop(...) → {count:old.count-1}` refuted (SAT cex: old.count=0); B3: `migrate → {count:0,history:Nil}` with `v.count>=0 && len(v.history)==v.count` verifies; `→ {count:old.count,history:Nil}` refuted (cex: old.count=1). Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-25-refine-check-gap1-full.md`.
- ✅ **Refinement types: record postconditions + measures over record fields — Gap #1 Part A (2026-06-25)** — `lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml` extended to discharge predicates over record-typed return values, including measure applications on record fields (e.g. `len(v.history) == v.count`, `mlength(v.history) == v.count`). (1) `TDRecord` types registered as 1-ctor SMT datatypes (`M_<name>` sort, ctor name = type name, selectors `<Ctor>_<idx>`) in the existing two-pass `register_adt_names`/`register_field_sorts` machinery; new `ctor_field_names` table maps ctor → field names in declaration order.
