(2) `smt_of` gains optional `?resolve_field` and `?resolve_measure_app` parameters; `EField(EVar x, fname)` emits field selectors; `ECon(ctor, args)` reflects zero/multi-arity constructors (`Nil`, `Cons`) to SMT applications when the ctor is registered in `ctor_field_sorts`; measure application on non-variable args is routed through `resolve_measure_app`. (3) `return_refine_ext` extends `return_refine` to also return the SMT sort name for record-typed return refinements. (4) `check_post` extended with `?record_sort`, `reflect_record_literal`, `make_field_resolver`, and `resolve_measure_app`; `concrete_len`/`concrete_measure_app`/`selector_reduce` evaluate measure applications at the OCaml level (avoids Z3 quantifier axioms for concrete list/ADT terms); `needs_axiom_preamble` flag selects between full quantified preamble and lightweight `type_only_preamble()` (avoids Z3 `unknown` from `forall` axioms on SAT queries). (5) `measure_arms` now accepts `PatWild` (`_` in pattern positions like `Cons(_, t)`) by assigning generated names (`_w1`, …), fixing a root-cause bug that prevented any measure with a wildcard arm from being axiomatized; this unblocked axioms-ma, list-axioms-mb, mutual-mc, flag-gating tests simultaneously. Verified: `len(v.history)==v.count` and `mlength(v.history)==v.count` both discharge correctly on `{count:0,history:Nil}` (pass) and `{count:1,history:Nil}` (violation reported). **58 tests in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`** (+7 in `record-postconditions` group, 57 pass; `resolution.0` is a pre-existing failure). Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-25-refine-check-record-spike.md`. **Remaining (Gap #1 full):** lift `is_int_base` gates for record-typed *param* refinements; `@invariant` on `State` type. Gaps #2–3 (persist invariant in `.schemas.json`, `--check-migration` CLI mode): `specs/2026-06-25-refinement-verified-state-migration-design.md`.

- ✅ **Refinement types Path 1: use/alias-aware call resolution (2026-06-21)** — replaced the fragile "qualified key OR globally-unique bare name" matching (`build_table`/`collect_sig_list`) with proper lexical resolution that mirrors the typechecker. `collect_all_defs` records EVERY function (refined or not) under its fully-qualified key; a new `resolve_call` resolves a call name through (1) lexical module scope — the nearest enclosing module that defines the name wins, with shadowing, (2) alias expansion (`alias X.Y as P` → `P.foo` resolves to `X.Y.foo`), (3) `use`-imported bare names (`use M.{f}` / `use M.*`). The resolution context (module path + in-scope aliases/uses, inherited by nested modules) is threaded through `visit`/`visit_decls`. Recording non-refined defs is what makes it sound: a bare call resolving to a non-refined sibling no longer falls through to a refined function of the same name in an enclosing module (which would check the wrong predicate — a false positive). This both CATCHES violations the old code dropped on any cross-module name collision AND stays false-positive-free. Wrong resolution can at worst *skip* (only a refined resolution triggers a check). Verified end-to-end through the full pipeline (a `use`-imported `take_pos(-7)` is caught). 5 new tests (51 total in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`: collision-resolution, local-vs-colliding, alias, use, shadowing-safety); a new desugaring test harness (`has_refine_error_d`) for qualified calls, since the parser emits `EField` and desugar flattens to a dotted `EVar`. **Remaining Path 1:** concrete unfolding of user measures over literal constructors; use/alias module *paths* resolved relative to the top scope only (deeply-nested `use` of an inner-of-inner module isn't prefix-walked — rare). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-refinement-types-state-and-forward-design.md`.

- ✅ **Refinement types: measure axioms M-c (cross-measure + mutual recursion) + performance/flag-gating (2026-06-21)** — **M-c**: lifted M-b's self-recursion-only restriction. A `@[measure]` may now call *another axiomatisable measure* (argument still required to be a structural component, so every call — self or cross — decreases and the whole system is total). Axiomatisability is resolved by a **greatest fixpoint** over the candidate set (a measure calling a non-axiomatisable one cascades to symbolic), and all `declare-fun`s precede all axioms so forward/mutual references resolve. **Mutually-recursive datatypes** are emitted as the transitive ADT closure in one combined `declare-datatypes`, and a constructor field of *any* registered ADT (not just self) reflects to that ADT's sort so cross-ADT calls are well-sorted. Verified: `tsize`/`fsize` over mutually-recursive `RTree`/`RForest` compute structurally (depth-2 forest → size 2, out-of-bounds caught). Gate still covers self-recursion; a non-structural mutual cycle isn't a hard error but stays unaxiomatised (sound symbolic fallback). **Performance + flag-gating**: measured the cost (cold VC cache) — 40 concrete-structure measure-bound VCs take **69.9 s** with axioms vs **0.45 s** symbolic (~155×; many near the 3 s timeout), but warm-cache rebuilds are ~0.5 s. Key fix: the datatype/quantifier preamble is now attached to a VC **only when it references an axiomatised measure** (postconditions, which reflect measures symbolically, never get it), so 150 plain Int/Bool VCs alongside a defined measure cost ~1 s — the axiom tax is isolated to measure-using VCs. New `--no-measure-axioms` flag reflects measures symbolically and disables the gate (changes only diagnostics, not the artifact, so not in the CAS key). Axioms stay **on by default** (non-measure code unaffected; measure consumers are rare; warm cache covers iterative dev). 6 new tests (46 total in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`: 3 mutual-recursion, 3 flag-gating). **Remaining:** M-d (relational postconditions — needs reflecting an operation's *result* as a datatype term); a measure call on the whole matched value (`A(t) = B(t) + …`); per-query timeout tuning. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-measure-axioms-design.md`.

- ✅ **Refinement types: measure axioms M-a (quantified recursion equations) + M-b (soundness gate + built-in `List`) (2026-06-21)** — **M-a**: a `@[measure]` over a user ADT is *axiomatised*, not just reflected symbolically. The ADT is modelled with `declare-datatypes` (element type opaque `Elem`, self-recursive fields), the measure as an uninterpreted `declare-fun`, and each `match` arm as a `:pattern`-triggered recursion-equation axiom — so the solver computes measure values *structurally* (`size(Node(Leaf,x,Leaf)) = 1`, out-of-bounds tree indices caught). 3s solver timeout keeps hard quantified queries `unknown` → definite-failure skip (never hang, never unsound). **M-b**: (1) the **hard soundness gate** — a `@[measure]` is a promise of a total, terminating, pure function; the encoding trusts it, so a measure that is effectful (`spawn`/`send`/`dbg`/`assert`), divergent (`panic`/`todo`/`exit`), non-total (a `/`/`%` that can divide by zero, or a non-exhaustive `match` on its ADT param), or not structurally recursive (a recursive call whose argument is not a component of the matched parameter) is now a **compile error**, not a silent symbolic fallback (a sound-but-unaxiomatisable measure — multi-arg, element-inspecting, `let`-bodied — still falls back silently). (2) **built-in `List(a)`** modelled as `Nil | Cons(a, List(a))`, so user list measures (`length`) axiomatise like user ADTs (`length([10,20]) = 2`). (3) datatype sort names moved to a private `M_` namespace so no ADT name collides with a z3-reserved sort (`List`, `Array`, `Seq`, …); axiom bodies restricted to self-recursion only (cross-measure/`len` calls deferred to M-c — their symbols aren't declared). 6 new tests (40 total in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`). **Remaining (per spec):** M-c (measures calling measures, mutual recursion, mutually-recursive datatypes), M-d (relational postconditions like `size(insert(t,x)) == size(t)+1`), performance measurement / flag-gating. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-measure-axioms-design.md`.

- ✅ **Refinement types Path 1: collision-safe resolution (P1a) + assert-as-assume (P1c) + user measures (P1b) (2026-06-21)** — P1a: `build_table` registers qualified names always but a bare name only when one definition uses it, so colliding bare names across modules are conservatively skipped (no false positives); qualified resolution unchanged. P1c: `assert(p)` acts as an `assume`, extending the path context (reuses `EAssert`). P1b: `@[measure]` functions register as measures; any `m(x)` reflects to a consistent SMT symbol `m$x`, so guarded bounds over user-defined structure (`size`/`depth`) verify via path sensitivity (`len` keeps concrete-for-literals + non-negativity; user measures symbolic, no axiom in v1). P1d: counterexamples rendered for humans (`m$x` → `m(x) = v`) on pre- and post-condition violations, plus a `note:` line guiding the fix. P1b follow-up — non-negativity inference: a syntactically non-negative `@[measure]` gets the sound `m(x) >= 0` axiom (so count measures work unguarded — a `size`-returning function verifies a `_ >= 0` postcondition; `_ < 0` is caught). 31 tests total in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`. **Remaining Path 1:** P1b quantified recursion-equation axioms (SMT datatype modeling — the one large follow-up; **design speced** in `specs/2026-06-21-measure-axioms-design.md`, gated on a hard totality/termination/purity soundness check) + concrete unfolding of user measures over literal constructors. Known limit (RESOLVED 2026-06-21): qualified-string matching was fragile for *nested* modules — now replaced with use/alias-aware lexical resolution (see the Path 1 use/alias resolution entry above). Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-refinement-types-state-and-forward-design.md`.

- ✅ **Refinement types: TRefine carried in the internal type (2026-06-21)** — increment 1 (`TRefine of ty * string * Ast.expr`, transparent via `repr`-strip, 8 match sites) merged; increment 2a (`generalize`/`instantiate` preserve the wrapper) on branch. Behavior-preserving foundation for a future refinement-subtyping core. **Key finding:** transparent-`repr` TRefine cannot do higher-order checking (unification strips the refinement).

- ✅ **Refinement types: path sensitivity, postconditions, stdlib hardening (2026-06-21)** — (1) **Path sensitivity**: `if`/`when`/`match` guards become SMT assumptions (then-branch gets the condition, else gets its negation); variable args reflected as stable constants so a guard about `i` discharges a precondition about `i`. Guarded code (`if i >= 0 && i < len(xs) do at(xs, i)`) now verifies; partial guards skip; contradicting guards are rejected. (2) **Postconditions**: a function's return-position (tail) expressions are checked against its return refinement (`fn f(..) -> {Int | _ >= 0}`), threading path/scope; refined params contribute assumptions. (3) **Hardening**: `--check` over the *entire* annotated stdlib (all 57 modules walked) yields **zero false positives**, while genuine violations (`List.chunks(xs, 0)`) are still caught. All three keep the definite-failure soundness stance (report only when a predicate can never hold). 12 new tests (28 total in `test/test_refinecheck.ml`). **Finding:** clean *checkable* stdlib consumers are rare — defensive clamping + recursion (un-provable without induction) limit them to a few (`chunks` size, `Decimal.div` precision). Remaining gap: higher-order/indirect calls + interface dispatch, which need TRefine-in-the-type (35-site typecheck.ml rewrite, deferred).

- ✅ **Refinement types A2 (minimal): `len` measure + bounds checking (2026-06-21)** — extend `lib/refinecheck` with the `len` measure, cross-argument predicate resolution (a predicate on `i` can reference sibling param `xs`), and static list-literal length. Bounds like `{Int | _ >= 0 && _ < len(xs)}` are checkable: `at([10,20,30], 5)`/`3`/`-1` rejected (out-of-bounds, off-by-one, negative), `1`/`0` pass, **unknown-length args conservatively skipped**. **Soundness fix:** a violation is reported only when the precondition can *never* hold (definite-failure check — `discharge(¬goal)` Verified), so symbolic lengths never false-positive. 6 bounds tests (`test/test_refinecheck.ml`). Next: a non-Option stdlib indexer as a bounds consumer; then TRefine-in-type for robust integration.
- ✅ **Refinement types A1b (minimal): call-site precondition checking (2026-06-21)** — `lib/refinecheck/`: post-typecheck pass collects `{Int | predicate}` params and discharges precondition VCs at call sites through the A0 Z3 bridge. Catches literal violations (`take_n(-3)`, `chunks(xs, 0)`) with counterexamples; passes valid literals and refined-local propagation; conservatively skips unconstrained args (no false positives). Wired into `bin/main.ml` (`--check`/`--compile`). 5 integration tests (`test/test_refinecheck.ml`). Stdlib annotations as genuine consumers: `List.chunks(xs, size : {Int | _ > 0})` (size<=0 infinite-loops) and `Decimal.div(..., precision : {Int | _ >= 0})`. **Finding:** most March stdlib `Int` params defensively *clamp* negatives, so they have no true precondition — naive `_ >= 0` would be wrongly strict; genuine preconditions are rarer (unguarded divisors, chunk sizes). **Scope/limits:** Int/Bool over `_` only, direct named calls, no path sensitivity, bare-name sig collisions possible; needs `MARCH_STDLIB` in the worktree (the `_build` stdlib copy is stale). Next: TRefine-in-internal-ty for robust HO/cross-module integration + the `len` measure (A2).
- ✅ **Refinement types A1a: surface syntax + erasure (2026-06-21)** — `{T|p}` / `{v:T|p}` parse to `Ast.TyRefine` and erase to their base type through the whole pipeline (typechecks identically; no checking yet). `_` is now an expression atom. 11 exhaustive `Ast.ty` matches updated; faithful formatter rendering. 3 parser tests; full suite green. **Next: A1b** — emit Int-only precondition VCs at call sites through the A0 bridge, discharge via `Refine.discharge`, report counterexamples. Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-21-refinement-types-a1-typechecker-wiring.md`.
- ✅ **RRB.Vec v2 + atomic RC in scheduler threads (2026-06-24)** — `stdlib/rrb_vec.march` rewritten to Array-backed 32-way trie (`ptype Vec(a) = Vec(Array(a))` / `ptype Slice(a) = Slice(Array(a), Int, Int)`). `chunk(v, n)` zero-copy creates `n` Slices all pointing to the same Array; `fold(s, z, f)` iterates `[lo, hi)` without allocating. Fixed `array.march` off-by-5 in `descend`: `trie_slot` must receive `s - 5` not `s` when entering an existing branch. Runtime fix: `march_incrc_local`/`march_decrc_local` now delegate to atomic `march_incrc`/`march_decrc` when `march_sched_in_scheduler()` — multiple tasks sharing an Array via zero-copy slices raced on the RC field across 4 OS scheduler threads causing segfaults ("local RC underflow" aborts). Stress-tested: `Parallel.psum(RRB.range(1, 130))` × 20 runs clean; n ∈ {10,50,64,100,129,256,1000,10000,100000} with 4–8 workers all pass. Test count unchanged: **321 compiler / 224 eval / 786 stdlib / 49 stdlib_march**.
- ✅ **Perceus dead br_var EDecRC leak fix (2026-06-24)** — In the Perceus pass (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`), pattern-match branches that bind constructor fields but never use them (e.g. `PVec(n, _, _, _) -> n`) caused a permanent RC leak. When the scrutinee was shared (RC > 1), `llvm_emit`'s shared path incremented RC for all heap-typed br_vars (root, tail); the branch body only returned `n`, so root/tail RCs inflated permanently. Fix adds two pieces to the `ECase` handler in `insert_rc_expr`: **(1) br_var ctx population** — before processing each branch body, all br_vars are added to `_var_ctx` (with save/restore for shadowing) so the cross-branch dead-variable pass can see them and emit `EDecRC` for fields dead in some branches but live in others; **(2) dead br_var EDecRC** — after `insert_rc_expr` on the branch body, emit `EDecRC` for each br_var that is heap-typed (`needs_rc`), not in `live_before_br` (never referenced in the branch body), not in `_closure_fvs`, and not in `_borrowed_field_vars`. In the unique (FBIP) case the field was moved from the freed constructor and is released here; in the shared case the llvm_emit shared-path inc_rc is balanced by this dec_rc. The `scrutinee_borrowed = true` case is handled correctly: all br_vars are re-added to the live set so the dead-check suppresses EDecRC for borrowed fields. Also registers `rrb_vec.march` and `parallel.march` in `stdlib_file_list` (`bin/main.ml`) so `--compile` finds the `RRB` and `Parallel` modules; and fixes the double-untag in `task_await_unwrap` (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`): for i64 results, the thunk stores 4*n+3 (doubly tagged) — one coerce gives 2*n+1 (still tagged); a second conditional `ashr` recovers the raw integer (parallel reductions were returning `2×correct + N_workers`). Test count unchanged: **321 compiler / 224 eval / 786 stdlib / 49 stdlib_march**.
- ✅ **RRB.Vec + Parallel stdlib modules (2026-06-24)** — `stdlib/rrb_vec.march` (module `RRB`): persistent sequence backed by `List` in v1; `ptype Vec(a)` / `ptype Slice(a)`; full API: `empty`, `singleton`, `from_list`, `from_array`, `tabulate`, `range`, `length`, `is_empty`, `get`, `push`, `concat`, `slice`, `fold`, `chunk`, `to_list`, `to_array`, `each`, `map`, `fold_left`. `stdlib/parallel.march` (module `Parallel`): bulk parallel map-reduce on `Vec` via `task_spawn`/`task_await_unwrap`; `pmap`/`pmap_n`, `preduce`/`preduce_n`, and convenience wrappers `psum`, `psum_float`, `pcount`, `pany`, `pall`. Tests: `test/stdlib/test_rrb_vec.march` (47 cases, registered in `test_stdlib_march.ml` + `all_stdlib_decls` load list). Docs: `docs/cookbook/parallel-data.md` (full worked examples: word frequency, image filter, statistics, pfilter via preduce); `docs/parallelism.md` updated with RRB/Parallel comparison table and link. Interpreter-correct; real multi-core parallelism in compiled mode. Test count: **321 compiler / 224 eval / 786 stdlib / 49 stdlib_march**.

- ✅ **Parallel list ops + parallelization lint (2026-06-21)** — `stdlib/list.march` gains `pmap`, `pmap_n` (bounded concurrency), `pfilter`, and `preduce` (parallel associative reduction; associativity is the caller's contract). All order-preserving, identical results to the sequential forms; chunked via `List.chunks` over one task per chunk on the existing multithreaded scheduler (real parallelism in compiled code — verified ~281% CPU; sequential in the interpreter). New nullary builtin `pmap_threshold() -> Int` (typecheck `Mono Int`, interpreter `Eval.pmap_threshold_value`, LLVM constant) gates a sequential fallback; configured by `--pmap-threshold=N` (default 1024), wired into interpreter + codegen, **included in the CAS cache key** (else cached binaries ignored the flag), and passed through by forge via `MARCH_PMAP_THRESHOLD`. LSP adds a `perf/parallelizable` Hint on pure `List.map`/`filter` with a "Convert to `List.pmap`/`pfilter`" quick-fix (purity via shared `March_tir.Purity.impure_builtins`; folds never flagged). Tests: `test/stdlib/test_list_parallel.march` (registered) + a Slow compiled parity test + 6 LSP tests. Deferred (designed): `--auto-parallel` TIR rewrite. Spec: `specs/2026-06-21-parallel-list-ops-design.md`; plan: `specs/2026-06-21-parallel-list-ops-plan.md`.

- ✅ **Refinement types A0: standalone Z3 bridge (2026-06-20)** — `lib/refine/` renders VCs to SMT-LIB2 (Int/Bool linear-arithmetic + EUF), drives a long-lived `z3 -in` process (push/pop per VC), parses `unsat`/`sat`(+counterexample model)/`unknown`, and caches verdicts BLAKE3-keyed under `.march/cas/vc/`; `Refine.discharge` ties cache→solver→outcome. Standalone, no typechecker coupling; 14 unit tests (`test/test_refine.ml`), solver cases skip gracefully when z3 is absent. Next: A1 — wire VC emission into `typecheck.ml` for Int-only preconditions. Spec: `specs/2026-06-20-dependent-types-refinements-design.md`; plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-20-refinement-types-a0-z3-bridge.md`.

- ✅ **Browser REPL HTTP client (2026-06-18)** — `HttpClient.get` / `HttpClient.post` now work in the js_of_ocaml playground via a synchronous-XHR platform hook. New `http_fetch` / `http_fetch_available` builtins (`lib/eval/eval.ml`) with a `http_fetch_hook` ref; native leaves it unset and keeps the socket transport, the browser build (`js/march_browser.ml`) sets it to a sync `XMLHttpRequest` impl. `HttpTransport.request` branches on `http_fetch_available()` (handles both http and https in the browser, bypassing the native `require_plain_http`/TLS path). `http.march`/`http_transport.march`/`http_client.march` added to the browser stdlib loadlist. CORS-limited, non-streaming, server side still unsupported. 5 new eval tests; verified end-to-end via a Node bundle smoke test with a polyfilled sync XHR. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-18-browser-http-client.md`.

- ✅ **`let?` result-propagation binding** — `let? p = e` binds the `Ok` payload and short-circuits on `Err`, propagating the error upward automatically. Implemented end-to-end across all 14 compiler passes: `ELetQ` AST node (`lib/ast/ast.ml`), `fold_letq` parser helper that right-folds a block of `let?` bindings into nested `ELetQ` continuations (`lib/parser/parser.mly`), desugar structural recursion, native typechecking with precise error messages (empty-continuation detection, non-Result RHS, mismatched error types), evaluator, TIR lowering (desugars to `match Ok(p) -> cont | Err($e) -> Err($e)`), span remap, formatter, dumper, linter, refactorer, coverage, and LSP (analysis/depot/workspace). 9 new tests (4 eval + 5 compiler). Test count: **1485** (219 compiler + 219 eval + 267 codegen + 780 stdlib).

- ✅ **Phase 2b always-linear types + `tag` phantom labels** — `always_linear type T(params) = ...` (unconditional linearity, no per-site annotation); `tag Foo` parser sugar (≡ `type Foo = Foo`); `stdlib/handle.march` `Handle(r, s)` typestate type; thread-through idiom validated. 319 tests pass.

- ✅ **LSP Depot-aware intelligence** — New `lsp/lib/depot.ml` module gives the LSP understanding of the Depot query-builder / schema / migration library. Schema model (A0–A3): extracts `Depot.Schema.define` calls into `depot_schemas`; resolves `from_table("t") |> where_eq("col", v)` pipes to their schema via `depot_col_occs`. Column intelligence (B1–B3): column-name completion inside `where_eq`/`order_asc`/… args; `depot/unknown-column` Warning with Levenshtein did-you-mean; go-to-def jumps to the field's span in the schema; hover returns the field type. Table intelligence (B4): table-name completion in `from_table` args; `depot/unknown-table` Warning with did-you-mean. Migration intelligence (C1–C4): `migration_ops` extracts `CreateTable`/`AlterTable`/`CreateIndex`/`References` ops; `depot/schema-drift` and `depot/missing-migration` Hints compare schemas vs migrations; `depot/unknown-fk-column` Warning for bad `references(table, { column = … })`. SQL injection (D1): `depot/sql-injection` Warning when `Connection.simple_query` gets a non-literal SQL arg. All conservative: silent when schema/table is unresolvable. +19 LSP tests (**319** total `march-lsp`). Files: `lsp/lib/depot.ml` (new), `lsp/lib/analysis.ml` (extended), `lsp/test/test_lsp.ml`.

- ✅ **Named record types render by name in `pp_ty` (nominal-name recovery for LSP)** — Record types are structural in the typechecker (`TRecord of (string * ty) list`, no nominal name), so a declared `type R = { … }` used in an annotation or returned from a fn was rendered by `Tc.pp_ty` as `{ a : Int, b : Int }` instead of `R`. Fix is **rendering-only, zero unification-semantics change** (so no risk to the 82 `TRecord` construction/match sites across typecheck + TIR + codegen): a module-level index `_record_names` in `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` maps a record's *field-name signature* (sorted, comma-joined field names) → declared name, populated via `register_record_name` at all 5 `TDRecord` declaration sites (bare + qualified names). `pp_ty`'s `TRecord` case calls `recover_record_name` and prefers the name when unambiguous. Ambiguity (two distinct records with identical field sets) poisons the signature → falls back to structural `{ … }`; an unqualified name is preferred over a qualified alias of the same simple name. Downstream LSP wins (all route through `Tc.pp_ty`): hover now shows `Int -> R`, inlay hints show `R`, and the **"Add type annotation"** code action now offers named-record returns/bindings — the `annotatable_ty_str` guard in `lsp/lib/analysis.ml` (skips types containing `{`) now lets named records through automatically since they no longer render with braces (truly unnameable records still skipped). +3 LSP tests, 1 stale test updated (`test_no_annotation_for_record_return` → `test_annotation_for_named_record_return`). 1442 main-suite tests + 228 LSP tests pass. Caveat: the LSP's `AnnFnReturn` action inserts `-> T ` but March return syntax is `: T` — pre-existing bug, unrelated to this change (fails for `Int` too); flagged separately. With the correct `: R` form the recovered annotation parses cleanly.

- ✅ **Fire-and-forget task safety (RC fix) + 250K-task scheduler stress test** — `march_task_spawn_thunk` and `march_task_spawn_with_cancel_thunk` (`runtime/march_runtime.c`) now call `march_incrc(task)` immediately after allocating the Task object, bumping RC to 2. The trampoline (`march_thunk_trampoline`) calls `march_decrc(task)` after storing the tagged result and release-storing `done=1`. This prevents a use-after-free when callers do fire-and-forget (`let _ = task_spawn(...)`) — the caller's `decrc` drops RC 2→1 (not 0), so the task object outlives the trampoline's write. Previously fire-and-forget could UAF because the trampoline's raw `wa->task` pointer outlived the heap object. Stress test: `test/native/sched_stress.march` — 250 batch tasks × 1000 workers = 250,250 green threads; each worker computes `fib(8..19)`. Throughput: **250K tasks in 1.32s wall, ~189K tasks/sec**. Scaled to 1M tasks: 4.79s wall, ~209K tasks/sec. Scheduler exits cleanly (EXIT:0) as tasks drain. All 1450 tests pass.

- ✅ **Fix parallel task result tagging + `task_await_unwrap` + scheduler LIFO livelock** — Three bugs causing wrong parallel results and deadlock in recursive parallel tasks. **(1) Result tagging** (`runtime/march_runtime.c`): `march_thunk_trampoline` now stores `(raw << 1) | 1` (tagged) in `task[3]` instead of raw i64; the `task_await` path goes through `mk_ok(result)` → `Ok(n)` pattern-match which loads the field as `ptr` (TVar→"ptr" in `llvm_ty`) and applies conditional `ashr 1` untag — without tagging, integers were halved. Added `march_task_await_value` that returns the tagged raw value directly (no `Ok` wrapper). **(2) `task_await_unwrap` codegen** (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`): was reading field index 0 (proc ptr, offset 16) with no spin-wait; now calls `march_task_await_value`, coerces tagged ptr→i64 via conditional ashr, and for ptr-typed inner results applies bare `inttoptr`. **(3) Scheduler LIFO livelock** (`runtime/march_scheduler.c`): added `last_yielded` flag — when the previous task yielded (PROC_READY), try to steal from other workers before re-running it. Without this, all workers could spin on their local yielded tasks while the leaf tasks (at steal ends of other deques) never got scheduled. Verified: `par_fib(N, 3)` N=7–20 correct; prime counting `par(k)` matches sequential.

- ✅ **Async mailbox dispatch — `receive()` cooperative blocking** — `exception BlockedOnReceive` in `lib/eval/eval.ml`: `receive()` builtin raises it when the actor mailbox is empty; `run_scheduler` catches it, re-inserts the consumed message at the front of the mailbox, and does not mark forward progress — the `while !changed` loop terminates naturally when all actors are blocked. Compiled path: `receive()` → `@march_sched_recv()` in `llvm_emit.ml` with `declare ptr @march_sched_recv()` in the LLVM preamble and `"receive"` registered in `builtin_ret_ty`. 4 new tests: blocking until sub-message, no-deadlock on all-blocked, FIFO ordering, LLVM declaration. Plan: `specs/plans/async-mailbox-dispatch.md`.

- ✅ **Green-thread scheduler Phase 5 — language-level wiring** — `bin/main.ml`: `march_scheduler.c` now unconditionally included in compiled binaries (was HTTP-gated). `runtime/march_runtime.c`: `march_task_await` spin-blocks on `PROC_DEAD` then returns `Ok(result)`; `march_thunk_trampoline` stores task return value in `task[3]`; Task objects extended from 24 → 32 bytes. Cancel-token wrappers: `march_task_spawn_with_cancel_thunk`, `march_task_cancel_by_id`. `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`: `task_await` → `call ptr @march_task_await`; `task_yield` → `call void @march_sched_yield()`; `task_reductions` → `load i64, ptr @march_tls_reductions`; 5 cancel-token codegen cases added. `lib/scheduler/*.ml`: "interpreter-only scaffolding" header comment. 4 IR correctness tests added. Plan: `specs/plans/green-thread-scheduler.md`.

- ✅ **Actor struct field-order bug + `march_send_checked`** — `lib/tir/lower.ml`: actor sentinel fields renamed `$dispatch` → `$d_dispatch` and `$alive` → `$e_alive` so alphabetical sort in `get_record_fields` matches TDRecord allocation order (was `$alive < $dispatch` so word[2] and word[3] were swapped, corrupting dispatch). `runtime/march_runtime.c`: `march_send_checked` now validates the capability's revocation status via `revoc_contains` before `find_meta`, frees the `Some(())` return value of `march_send` (was a 24-byte leak per call), and uses `march_cancel_token_is_cancelled` returning `int64_t` (ABI match with `declare i64`). Native test: `test/native/actor_counter` — spawns Counter actor, sends `Increment(5)`, probes, verifies `value=5`. Plan: `specs/actor-lowering.md`.

- ✅ **`forge refactor bundle` — introduce a parameter object** — New `bundle_fn` in `lib/refactor/refactor.ml` + `forge refactor bundle <Fn> [--record NAME]` subcommand. Bundles a function's parameters into a generated record (`type <Fn>Args = { p : T, ... }`), rewrites the signature to take it (`fn f(args: <Fn>Args)`), rewrites the body's unshadowed parameter references to field accesses (`args.p`, via a scoped free-variable-style walk), and migrates **every call site project-wide** to pass the record literal (`f({ p = v, ... })`) — the cross-file part the cursor LSP can't do. Re-parse safety gate as with the other commands. v1 limits: single-clause function, all params type-annotated (else a clear error), recursive calls rewritten as call sites may need manual touch-up. **Notable bug found & worked around:** string-literal spans in the parser only cover the opening quote, so argument source can't be sliced per-span — `bundle` recovers args by splitting the call's parenthesised text on top-level commas (`split_top_commas`, string/nesting aware). +2 refactor tests (9 total). This is the "values that travel together → struct" / Introduce-Parameter-Object refactor; built in the forge engine rather than the LSP because the valuable part (rewriting all callers) is project-wide.
- ✅ **`forge refactor bundle` — fix doc-comment insertion (silent no-op on documented fns)** — `bundle_fn` inserted the generated `type <Fn>Args = {…}` at the start of the target function's line, i.e. **between** an attached `doc "…"` comment and its `fn`. A `doc` must immediately precede the decl it documents, so `doc` → `type` failed to parse and the engine's own re-parse safety gate silently rejected the file ("skipped: result would not parse / 0 files changed") — bundle no-op'd on *any* documented function. Fix (`lib/refactor/refactor.ml`): when `fn.fn_doc <> None`, walk up from the `fn` line past any `@[…]` attribute lines to the doc block's opening line (`is_doc_start`: the `doc` keyword followed by a string literal, single- or triple-quoted, so multi-line docs and identifiers like `doctor` aren't mismatched) and insert the type **above** the doc. Found while building the `bastion_todos` LSP demo. +1 refactor test (`documented target fn`, 10 total); verified end-to-end on the demo controller (`render_link` with a doc now bundles + migrates its call site).
- ✅ **Cross-module typeclass dispatch — bare interface-method binding** — A bare interface-method call (`summarize(x)`) from a *different* module than the one defining the `interface`+`impl` failed with "I cannot find `summarize`". Root cause: the whole-program forward-reference pre-pass `prebind_mod_members` (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`) bound only the **qualified** method names (`Iface.method`, `Mod.Iface.method`) for sibling modules, never the **bare** name — but the single-file path (`check_decl` `DInterface`) does `bind_var m.md_name.txt`, which is why typeclasses worked within one file but not across files (sibling `mod` blocks are exactly how the resolver presents a multi-file project). Fix: bind the bare method name in `prebind_mod_members` too, skipping when already bound so a builtin/user fn of the same name isn't clobbered. Now the idiomatic pattern (interface in one module, each `impl` co-located with its type, dispatch from anywhere) typechecks and runs. +1 test (`interface cross-module dispatch`). Known remaining restriction (orphan impls): `impl Iface(T)` where `T` is defined in *another* module than the impl still fails to resolve `T`, because sibling-module types are registered qualified-only in `env.types` — co-locate the impl with its type (the usual coherence rule).
- ✅ **Cross-module typeclass dispatch — record-typed impls** — Follow-up to the above: a typeclass `impl` on a **record** type was invisible to a call site in a different module ("`T` does not implement `Iface`"), even with the impl co-located with its type. Root cause: two paths register an impl into `env.impls` and disagreed on record representation — `check_decl`'s `DImpl` handler uses `surface_ty`, which **expands a record type name to its structural form** (`TRecord [...]`), but the forward-reference pre-pass `register_impl_shape` used a lenient converter that mapped `TyCon "T"` → nominal `TCon "T"` and never consulted `env.records`. So when a dispatch in module A is discharged *before* the impl's own `DImpl` is reached in pass 2 (the cross-module / multi-file order the resolver produces, `extra_decls @ entry_decls`), only the nominal pre-pass shape was registered, and `impl_matches_ty(TCon "T", TRecord […])` failed. Variant impls were unaffected (both paths produce `TCon`). Fix (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`): `register_impl_shape`'s `lenient_ty` now expands a record type name structurally via `env.records`, mirroring `surface_ty`, so the forward-ref shape matches. +1 test (`interface cross-module record dispatch`). Verified end-to-end on the **compiled** path (TIR/native dispatches correctly by type). Two separate, deeper limitations were noted at the time: (a) the tree-walking **interpreter** dispatches a user interface by last-impl-wins rather than by type — even within one module — so user typeclasses only dispatch correctly when compiled (`forge build`); (b) the **LSP's per-file** analysis couldn't see an interface declared in a different file from an `impl`. **(b) is now fixed** (next entry); (a) remains.
- ✅ **LSP cross-file interfaces — per-file analysis resolves a sibling-module interface** — An `impl` in one project file referencing an `interface` declared in another reported a spurious "Unknown interface" in the editor, even though whole-program `forge build` was clean. Root cause: the LSP's per-file path uses the **incremental** checker `check_module_with_env`, whose own pre-pass `prebind_mod_members_inc` handled `DFn`/`DType` for sibling `DMod`s but **silently dropped `DInterface`/`DImpl`** (`_ -> e`) — unlike `check_module_core`'s `prebind_mod_members`, which registers them. So sibling functions and types resolved cross-file but interfaces never reached `env.interfaces`. (The siblings were correctly discovered and loaded — `with_deps.interfaces` had the interface — the incremental pre-pass just ignored them when checking the user file.) Fix (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`): extracted the interface-prebinding (register the name qualified + bare, and each method) into a shared top-level `prebind_interface_decl ~prefix` used by **both** pre-passes so they can't diverge again, and registered sibling impls in the incremental `DMod` handler. Now cross-file interfaces resolve in the editor across lib subdirectories, matching `forge build`; a genuinely-undeclared interface still errors. +2 LSP tests (`cross-file`: interface-resolves, unknown-still-errors), using a real temp forge project with the impl in `lib/sub/` and the interface in `lib/`. This is the last of the four cross-file-typeclass layers (typecheck dispatch, record impls, native codegen, LSP) — only the tree-walking interpreter's last-impl-wins dispatch remains, a separate eval-backend change.
- ✅ **Contextual test-DSL keywords (`test`/`describe`/`setup`/`setup_all` usable as identifiers)** — These were hard lexer keywords, so `fn describe(...)`, a `setup` parameter, etc. were parse errors ("I got stuck here"). They are only ever keywords in a fixed position — `test`/`describe` immediately before a `STRING`, `setup`/`setup_all` before `DO` (March calls always use parens, so a `STRING` can never follow an identifier except in the DSL). Fix in `lib/parser/token_filter.ml`: a one-token-lookahead demoter wraps the base lexer inside `Token_filter.make` (covers every parse entry point), emitting `LOWER_IDENT` for these tokens unless they're in keyword position, with lexbuf position save/restore so spans stay correct. The test DSL is unchanged in its real position. (`assert` stays reserved — it's a genuine expression keyword.) +2 tests (idents + DSL-still-parses). Note: this unmasked a latent stdlib-collision in an LSP fixture (`type Color` vs stdlib `Color`) that had been vacuously passing because its `fn describe` never parsed — fixture renamed to `Hue`.

- ✅ **LSP code actions — Phase 3 (type-directed refactors)** — Three more actions in `ast_code_actions` (`lsp/lib/analysis.ml`). **Destruct / case-split:** cursor on a variant-typed expression → replace it with an exhaustive `match` over the type's constructors (enumerated from `a.ctors`/`a.ctor_arities`), each arm binding the constructor's fields and stubbed with `?`; only fires when the type is concretely known (a polymorphic param yields nothing). **Extract function:** the expression at the cursor → a sibling top-level `fn extracted(...)` whose parameters are the expression's *free locals* (a scoped free-variable walk respecting lambda/let/match binders and excluding module/global names), parameter types inferred from `type_map`; the site is replaced with the call. **Organize imports:** sort + de-duplicate a module's `use` declarations by rewriting the actual source lines (no AST re-rendering), offered only when it changes something (`source.organizeImports`). Also extended the `user_decls` filter to retain `DUse`/`DAlias`. +5 LSP tests (213 total). Deliberately skipped as non-idiomatic for March: generate getter/setter (records are public + immutable; `x.f` and `{ x with f = v }` already minimal) and generate-constructor (variant/record literals already are the constructor).

- ✅ **`forge refactor` — project-wide refactoring CLI** — A new parser-based refactoring engine (`lib/refactor/`, library `march_refactor`) plus `forge refactor` subcommands (`forge/lib/cmd_refactor.ml`, registered in `forge/bin/main.ml`). The engine discovers every `.march` file under the project, parses the raw AST (so it edits real constructs and never touches strings/comments), produces byte-offset edits applied in descending order, and **re-parses every edited file before writing** so a transform never leaves source that does not parse. A `site`-tagged name visitor (`iter_names`) powers `--kind fn|type|ctor|module|field|var|any` filtering. Commands: **rename** (`OLD NEW`, project-wide incl. qualified `Mod.x`; `--pattern` regex with `\1` backrefs; `--dry-run`), **fix** (turns lint naming findings — camelCase functions — into project-wide snake_case renames), **move** (`DECL DEST.march`, relocates a top-level declaration, creating `DEST` with a module wrapper if new), and **replace** (`'f($a,$b)' 'g($b,$a)'` structural codemod: parses pattern/template with `$metavariables`, matches call-shaped expressions, binds metavars to argument source and instantiates the template). All commands support `--dry-run`. Tests: `test/refactor/test_refactor.ml` — 7 cases (cross-file rename, string-safety, regex bulk, dry-run-writes-nothing, naming fix, move-to-new-file, call-shape codemod). Known v1 limitation: rename is name-based, not scope-resolved (a local shadowing the target is also renamed — use `--kind`). Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-14-forge-refactor-plan.md`.

- ✅ **DAP debugger v1 — editor debugging over the Debug Adapter Protocol** — Exposes the interpreter's existing trace/breakpoint engine to editors. **Engine** (`lib/eval/eval.ml`): new `step_mode` type and `dc_breakpoints`/`dc_step`/`dc_on_pause`/`dc_last_line` fields on `debug_ctx`; a `maybe_pause` check in the universal `eval_expr` wrapper stops on editor-set gutter breakpoints (keyed by file+line) and on step over/into/out, with last-line de-duping for line-granular stepping (zero overhead when no debug ctx installed). **Server** (`lib/dap/`): `dap_json.ml` (Content-Length framing) + `dap_session.ml` — a `launch`-model DAP server that runs the program on a worker `Thread` and answers requests on the protocol thread, rendezvousing via `Mutex`+`Condition`; handles `initialize`/`launch`/`setBreakpoints`/`configurationDone`/`threads`/`stackTrace`/`scopes`/`variables`/`continue`/`next`/`stepIn`/`stepOut`/`pause`/`evaluate`/`disconnect`; emits `initialized`/`stopped`/`continued`/`output`/`terminated`/`exited`. Call stack reconstructed from the named march-stack + pause location; Locals = the paused frame's env (builtins filtered). **Entry**: `march dap` subcommand in `bin/main.ml` (reuses `load_stdlib` + `run_module`), with the program's stdout redirected to DAP `output` events so the protocol stream stays clean. **Editors**: complete VS Code debug extension (`editors/vscode-march-debug/`, exports validated via mocked `vscode` host) **and** functional Zed client (Rust `get_dap_binary`/`dap_request_kind`/`dap_config_to_scenario` in `zed-march/src/lib.rs`, wasm rebuilt). Program stdout is flushed before termination so it reliably streams to DAP `output` events. **Tests**: `test/dap/test_dap.ml` — 5 end-to-end cases (breakpoint+stack+locals+continue, step-into a function, output events, panic-still-terminates, multiple breakpoints). v2 backlog (engine already supports the first): reverse debugging (`stepBack`/`reverseContinue`), conditional/hit-count breakpoints, watch, structured-value expansion, actor-as-thread. Plan: `specs/plans/2026-06-14-dap-debugger-plan.md`.

- ✅ **LSP code actions — Phase 2+ (13 AST-driven refactorings)** — `lsp/lib/analysis.ml`: the analysis result now retains the raw user AST (`Analysis.t.decls`) plus `protocols`, enabling a new `ast_code_actions` pass that powers the remaining planned code actions. Implemented: **Introduce pipe** (`f(g(x))` → `x |> g() |> f()`) / **Remove pipe** (collapses a whole `|>` chain back to nested calls); **Extract variable** (lifts the sub-expression at the cursor into a `let`, name inferred from its type); **Inline variable** (replaces every use with the RHS via the `consumption` use-spans, deletes the `let`); **Expand / collapse function capture** (eta-expand a function-valued identifier to `fn (_x) -> f(_x)`, collapse `fn (x) -> f(x)` to `f`; callee positions excluded); **Typed hole fills** (`?` → literals/constructors/in-scope values ranked by the expected type from `type_map`); **Auto-import** (`use Mod.{name}` for an unresolved name found qualified in `def_map`); **Generate interface impl scaffold** (stubs the interface's missing methods inside an `impl` block); **Actor boilerplate** (generates a `<Actor>Client` module of `send` wrappers, one per handler); **Session-type scaffolding** (generates a `handle_<proto>` skeleton walking `proto_steps` with send/receive/loop/choice); **Convert if → match** (boolean or equality-chain scrutinee, the "case-to-match" item); **Linear consumption audit** (flags a `linear`/`affine` param as never-consumed or reports its consumption-site count); **Batch fix-all** (`source.fixAll`: prefixes every unused binding in the file with `_`). New AST helpers: `iter_expr`/`iter_decl_exprs`/`all_exprs`/`smallest_expr_at`/`slice_span`/`surface_ty`/`split_arrow`. `user_decls` filter extended to keep `DProtocol`/`DTest`/`DDescribe`. 16 new LSP tests (179 total). Plan: `specs/plans/lsp-code-actions-plan.md`.

- ✅ **Fix lib-module type errors silently swallowed when MARCH_LIB_PATH is relative (user_files canonical-vs-as-given mismatch)** — the resolver (since the LSP/CLI unification, commit 2545039) registers loaded files in `loaded_paths` under their **realpath-canonicalised** spelling, but parses them under the **as-given** path — diagnostic spans carry the as-given string, so with a relative `MARCH_LIB_PATH` (or a relative entry importing source-dir siblings) `is_user_file`'s `List.mem f user_files` failed for every lib-module diagnostic and the errors were tolerated as "stdlib-internal". Ill-typed programs then compiled into corrupt binaries or died at link time with undefined pattern-binder symbols (the depot repro: **70 swallowed type errors**, surfacing only as `use of undefined value '@$f27180'` from clang — an `Ok(v)` binder of an `ECase` whose Bool-typed scrutinee took the integer-switch path that never binds `br_vars`). The pre-existing `entry_imports_ill_typed` fixtures were live failures after the canonicalisation landed. Fix in `lib/resolver/resolver.ml`: `user_files` now records **both spellings** (`record_user_path` at the entry pre-mark, the explicit-import `load` path, and auto-discovery phase 2, which now threads the as-given path through from phase 1); `loaded_paths` stays canonical-only for dedup. Regression fixture: `test/imports/lib_path_ill_typed/` (relative `MARCH_LIB_PATH`, ill-typed lib module, entry never calls the broken fn — `--check` and `march test` must both exit 1).

- ✅ **Fix indirect call through an erased-type closure (ECallPtr callee not coerced to ptr)** — a closure parameter captured into another lambda's environment loses its `TFn` type through the erased env slot (TVar → i64), and `ECallPtr` dispatch in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` fed the raw i64 SSA value to the fn-pointer `getelementptr`, producing invalid IR (`'%ldNN' defined with type 'i64' but expected 'ptr'`) — clang failed on any native build containing the pattern (depot's `Depot.Migration.run`: `__try_call(fn _ -> thunk())`). The callee atom is now coerced to ptr (`inttoptr`) before the field load, matching how the same dispatch path already coerced its arguments. Fixture: `test/imports/erased_clo_native/` (compile-and-run). Found while verifying the record introspection builtins against the depot repro.

- ✅ **Cross-session-safe ~/.cache/march: content-hash-keyed artifacts + atomic writes** — the shared cache dir let concurrent sessions/worktrees corrupt each other. Two fixed-name artifacts were the worst: `libmarch_runtime.so` (`bin/main.ml ensure_runtime_so`, invalidated only by `march_runtime.c`'s mtime — edits to the other ~10 runtime C files never invalidated it) and `libmarch_rt_test.so` (`test/test_march.ml setup_jit_runtime`, existence-check only — NEVER invalidated). A worktree with diverged runtime sources overwrote the shared file with an ABI-mismatched binary, hanging whichever session dlopen'd the wrong build (observed: 22-minute test_march.exe hangs); a reader could also dlopen a half-written .so mid-compile. Fix: both are now keyed `libmarch_runtime_<hash16>.so` / `libmarch_rt_test_<hash16>.so` where the digest covers every compiled .c file, every runtime/*.h, and (for the runtime) the full clang flag string — identical inputs share one artifact, divergent worktrees get separate files — and ALL five cache writers (those two plus `stdlib_prelude_<h>.so`/`.names` in `lib/jit/repl_jit.ml`, `stdlib_ast_<h>.bin` in `bin/main.ml`, `stdlib_tcenv_<h>.bin` in `lib/repl/repl.ml`) now write to a pid-suffixed temp and `rename(2)` into place, so no reader ever sees a partial file. The prelude `.names` is renamed BEFORE its `.so` so a visible .so always implies a complete names file. `ensure_runtime_so` with no source tree falls back to the newest cached `libmarch_runtime_*.so`. Verified: fresh-cache REPL eval, warm-cache reuse (mtime unchanged), runtime-source edit produces a NEW key leaving the old artifact intact, JIT test group green on isolated HOME, no `.tmp` leftovers.

- ✅ **MARCH_LIB_PATH auto-discovery regression + codegen guard for type-incorrect TIR** — independent rediscovery of the user_files path-spelling bug fixed concurrently by 78afbbc, traced from a fresh symptom: `MARCH_LIB_PATH=lib:test march --compile --test entry.march` reported NO type errors for an ill-typed lib module (depot's `Transaction.run` violating `__try_call`'s `(Bool -> Bool) -> Result(Bool, String)` contract) and died later in clang with `use of undefined value '@$fNNNNN'` — the `Ok(v)` binder of a `match` over an i64-typed scrutinee was never bound (integer-switch path doesn't bind `br_vars`; the unbound var fell into emit_atom's 0-arg-global-call fallback). The typechecker **was** generating all the errors; `user_files` held only realpath-canonicalised paths while spans carry the as-parsed (relative) path, so `is_user_file` (`bin/main.ml`) dropped every lib-path diagnostic as "stdlib-internal". The resolver fix landed on main as 78afbbc; this branch adds the two pieces it lacked: (1) regression `test/libpath_ill_typed/` + `test/libpath_entry.march` — a relative `MARCH_LIB_PATH` module with a body type error, never imported by the entry, must fail `--check` with exit 1 (covers the **auto-discovery** parse path specifically; the existing `entry_imports_ill_typed` rules only exercise explicit `use`; red/green verified against the pre-78afbbc resolver). (2) Defense-in-depth: `emit_case` (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`) now `failwith`s with a "type-incorrect TIR reached codegen" message — naming the pattern and scrutinee — when an ADT constructor branch with binders is reached on a non-pointer scrutinee, instead of silently emitting an integer switch whose branch bodies reference unbound binder symbols that only surface as clang link errors.

- ✅ **Fix native miscompilation dropping non-pointer tuple fields (module-nested default-arg fns)** — a module-nested fn with default-arg params (`fn greet_d(name, loud : Bool \\ false, suffix : String \\ "?")`) desugars — because `FPDefault` forces `desugar_fn_def`'s general match-merge path — into `match (a, b, c) do (x, y, z) -> …`, a tuple built from the args and immediately destructured. Native codegen silently corrupted any non-pointer (`Bool`/`Int`/`Float`) field: `ETuple`/`EAlloc` **build** the tuple storing each field with its concrete LLVM type (a `Bool` is a raw `i64`), but `emit_case` **destructured** via the synthetic `$TupleN` `ctor_info` entry whose fields are all `TVar "_"` → `ptr`, so the load untagged (`ashr 1`) a value that was never tagged — `loud = true` (`1`) arrived as `false` (`1 >> 1 = 0`). The repro printed `Def!` instead of `Def *!` natively while the interpreter stayed correct. Fix in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`: `resolve_ctor_fields` gained a `TTuple ts -> ts` case, and `emit_case`'s field-binding loop now loads tuple fields with their **concrete element types** (matching `ETuple`/`EField`) instead of the polymorphic `ce_fields` fallback; variant ctors keep `ce_fields` (their generic `TVar` fields ARE stored tagged via `coerce i64→ptr` in `EAlloc`). Also a general bug — any `(_, Bool, _)` tuple destructured natively was affected, not just default-arg fns. Regression wired into `dune runtest`: `test/native/default_args_nested.march` is compiled natively, run, and its stdout diffed against `test/native/default_args_nested.expected`. Downstream: bastion's `test/test_form.march` "required marker appended to label" test (`Form.Input.render`'s `required = true` was dropped) now passes natively.

- ✅ **Native lowering for the 7 record introspection builtins (record shape registry)** — `record_keys`, `record_values`, `record_entries`, `record_get`, `record_put`, `record_has_key`, `record_from_list` were interpreter-only; native builds emitted bare `call ptr @record_entries(...)` and died at link time. Records carry no field names at runtime, so a shape registry was added to `runtime/march_extras.c`: every record allocation (`ERecord`/`EUpdate`/`EReuse`) stamps an interned **shape id** into the previously-unused header pad word (offset 12, always 0 via `march_alloc`'s calloc) referencing a descriptor `"name:kind;..."` with per-field kind chars (`i` raw i64 scalar / `f` double bits / `p` heap ptr / `g` generic). The builtins are C functions reading the descriptor; `record_put` with a new key and `record_from_list` **intern new shapes at runtime** (sorted insert), supporting depot's dynamic `Depot.Schema.blank` fold. Value conventions: record slots natural repr; outputs into generic ADT slots (values list payload, `record_get`'s `Some` payload) low-bit-tag ints; `record_entries` pair snd stays natural per field kind. `llvm_emit.ml` special-cases the seven `EApp`s (natural-repr value passing + kind-char args, `record_from_list` payload-kind hint), and `EField` on statically-unknown record types now emits a dynamic by-name lookup (`march_record_field_dyn`, legacy slot-0 read for shape-0 cells). Eval's `record_keys/values/entries` now sort by field name to match the native sorted-by-name layout. Two enabling fixes shipped together: (1) `lower.ml` `rename_tir_vars` is now scope-aware — a local binder sharing a module fn name (e.g. a param named `fields` in a module defining `fn fields/1`) no longer has its references rewritten to the qualified global (was emitted as an undefined zero-arg call `@Depot.Schema.fields()`); (2) structural `==` for **tuples and records** — `ensure_adt_eq_fn` generates element-wise short-circuiting eq for `TTuple`/`TRecord` (previously pointer comparison, so `List.member(record_entries(r), ("age", 30))` was always false natively). WASM targets skip shape stores. Fixture: `test/imports/record_native/` (18 checks, compile-and-run). Borrow table: all `record_*` args borrowed.

- ✅ **Fix nested Bool/Int/Atom literal patterns inside constructor patterns miscompiled in the native path** — `match r do Ok(true) -> … Ok(false) -> … end` printed the FIRST bool arm for both inputs (T T E instead of T F E); nested Int/Atom literal matches segfaulted. The interpreter was always correct. Root cause in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` `emit_case`: the field bound by the outer constructor pattern is ptr-typed at the LLVM level (its TIR type is a `TVar` placeholder), so the inner literal test took the heap ctor-tag path — `getelementptr; load i32; switch` — but ADT fields holding immediates are stored low-bit-tagged (`(n<<1)|1`), not as heap pointers, making the tag load a wild dereference. With an `unreachable`-defaulted switch LLVM folded the test away entirely (bool case: first arm always wins) or it crashed (int/atom cases). Fix: literal-pattern tags are syntactically unambiguous (lowercase `true`/`false`, integers, `:atoms` — ADT ctors are capitalized), so `emit_case` now detects all-immediate-literal-tag cases and never treats the scrutinee as a heap pointer: bool cases reuse the existing untag (`ashr`) + `trunc` + `br i1` path; int/atom cases switch on the raw `ptrtoint` bits against `(lit<<1)|1` labels (comparing tagged-vs-tagged is exact; untagging would lose bit 63 of 64-bit atom hashes). Same shape existed in `stdlib/check.march` `format_error` — correct only by accident of arm order; now correct by construction. Resolves the deferred discovery from the `__try_call` fix below. 3 IR-level regression tests (`nested_lit_pattern_codegen` group) assert exactly one i32 ctor-tag switch (the outer `Ok`/`Err`) survives. 1423 → 1427 tests after merge.
- ✅ **Fix `hmac_sha256` typecheck signature disagreeing with every implementation** — the typechecker entry (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`) declared `Bytes -> Bytes -> Bytes`, but the eval builtin returns `Ok(Bytes)`, the native runtime (`march_hmac_sha256` in `runtime/march_extras.c`) reads `march_string` args and returns `Result(Bytes, String)` via `make_ok`, and llvm_emit's `builtin_ret_ty` says `Result(Bytes, String)`. Net effect: correct usage (`match hmac_sha256("k","m") do Ok(b) -> ...`) was rejected by the typechecker, while the only usage it accepted (treating the result as bare `Bytes` with `Bytes` args) would have crashed or miscompiled at runtime. Canonical signature is now `String -> String -> Result(Bytes, String)` — matching the C runtime, eval, llvm_emit, and the existing `stdlib_hmac_sha256` entry. The Bytes-domain variant `hmac_sha256_bytes : Bytes -> Bytes -> Bytes` (bare result, commit d07356d) was already consistent across all four sites and is unchanged. Typecheck-level regression tests added for both builtins (the existing tests only exercised eval, which is how the mismatch slipped through). 1424 → 1426 tests.
- ✅ **Fix compiled `Check.all` properties always failing ("returned false" on trivially-true properties)** — `__try_call` (runtime/march_runtime.c) stored the property thunk's raw i64 Bool into the Result Ok field untagged, while compiled March reads polymorphic ADT immediate fields with the uniform low-bit tag convention (`ashr 1`): `Ok(1)` read back as `0`, so `Check.is_failing` inverted every passing property into a failure on run 1 (interpreter unaffected — no tagging). Fix: store `((result << 1) | 1)` in `__try_call` and tighten its typechecker signature to `(Bool -> Bool) -> Result(Bool, String)` so heap-typed uses fail to typecheck rather than corrupt. E2E regression test compiles + runs a trivially-true property via `--compile --test` and asserts exit 0. Deferred discoveries: Bool literal patterns inside constructor patterns miscompile (first arm always taken — separate task); generic `to_string` prints `#<tag:N>` for lists in compiled failure reports. 1422 → 1423 tests.
- ✅ **Fix DCE dropping helpers referenced only via closures in multi-test-file `forge test` builds (`use of undefined value '@helper'`)** — test bodies inside a `DMod` (non-entry test files auto-discovered via MARCH_LIB_PATH) were lowered without `rename_tir_vars`, so they referenced sibling helpers unqualified (`helper`) while definitions were registered qualified (`TestB.helper`). Defun captured the helper as a free variable instead of a known top-level fn, DCE pruned the qualified definition (name mismatch in reachability), and llvm_emit's fallback emitted a dangling `@helper` trampoline → clang link error. Fix in `lib/tir/lower.ml` `collect_tests`: thread the enclosing module prefix + direct fn/let names through `DMod` descent and apply `rename_tir_vars` to lowered test/setup bodies, mirroring `lower_mod_decls`' handling of the module's own `DFn`s. Full-pipeline regression test in `test/test_march.ml` (adversarial-regressions). 1421 → 1422 tests.
- ✅ **Fix native-codegen miscompile when a local shadows a sibling module-level fn** — `rename_tir_vars` (lib/tir/lower.ml) qualified every `AVar` matching a sibling module fn name without checking whether the name was locally bound, so a param/pattern/let binding like `fields` in `pfn blank_fold(fields, acc)` next to `fn fields(schema)` was rewritten to the qualified global and emitted as the fn's address (SIGBUS) or a zero-arg `"gl"` call ("use of undefined value" at clang time). The rename walk now threads an in-scope binder set (fn params, ELet/ELetRec bindings, ECase branch vars) and skips shadowed names. Regression fixture `test/imports/param_shadow_native/` compiles natively and runs. Found via depot's `Depot.Schema.blank_fold`.
- ✅ **Fix nested-module default-arg functions boxing params into a tuple (erased-closure use-after-free)** — `expand_defaults_decl` runs only on top-level decls, so a default-arg function inside a nested module (e.g. `Form.Wrapper.render`) was never expanded; its `FPDefault` params kept the clause non-trivial, so `desugar_fn_def` routed it through the general path that boxes ALL parameters into a synthesised `$TupleN` and immediately destructures it (the function even reports the param tuple as its return type). With a type-erased closure parameter (an untyped trailing `render_inner`), that tuple adapter mismanaged the closure's refcount and freed it before its `call_ptr` — a use-after-free (`RC underflow in march_decrc_freed`; jump to `0x2` at `-O0`). Fix in `lib/desugar/desugar.ml`: `desugar_fn_def` gained a fast-path case for a single clause whose only non-trivial params are `FPDefault` — strip the defaults to plain `FPNamed` and keep the real signature, so no param tuple is synthesised (matching how top-level default-arg fns already behave once `expand_defaults_decl` expands them). Verified: bastion's full `Form.Wrapper.render` / CSRF test group now passes (was aborting mid-suite). Regression: `test/test_march.ml` "nested default-arg fn keeps signature (no param tuple)". 1422 → 1423 tests.

- ✅ **Fix string ordering comparison miscompiled in the native path (`List.sort_by` garbage order)** — string `<`/`<=`/`>`/`>=` in compiled code compared the string struct POINTERS as integers instead of comparing contents, so any compiled program ordering strings through a comparator (e.g. `List.sort_by`) produced wrong/no-op order while the interpreter stayed correct. Root cause in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`: the comparison-emission path (`is_int_cmp`) special-cased only string `==`/`!=` (→ `march_string_eq`); ordering operators fell through to the `icmp` integer/pointer fallback. Fix: added an `is_string_ord` branch that calls `march_compare_string(a, b)` (returns −1/0/1) and compares the result against 0 with the matching signed predicate. Regression tests added in `test/test_march.ml` (adversarial-regressions): an IR-level assertion that a polymorphic String comparator through a closure emits `call i64 @march_compare_string`, plus a value-level `List.sort_by` ascending/descending ordering check. 1418 → 1420 tests.

- ✅ **Fix `dune runtest` infinite hang (two root causes)** — `dune runtest` was hanging indefinitely on every run due to two independent bugs. (1) `lib/eval/compress_stubs.c`: `caml_march_deflate_decode` (and `caml_march_gzip_decode`) entered an infinite loop when `inflate()` returned `Z_BUF_ERROR` with all input consumed but no `Z_STREAM_END` — malformed input (e.g. the 3-byte `[1,2,3]` test vector) caused this on every run of the "deflate decode invalid → Err" test. Fix: explicit guard that detects `avail_in == 0 && avail_out > 0` after `Z_BUF_ERROR` and calls `caml_failwith`. (2) `test/test_heap.c` section 5c + `runtime/march_gc.c`: the GC abort-path tests fork child processes that call `abort()` via `march_gc_collect` → `gc_corrupt`. On macOS, `abort()` after `fork()` triggers the system crash reporter (ReportCrash), which intercepts the Mach exception and stalls for 10+ minutes per child process. Fix: added a weak `march_gc_crash()` hook in `march_gc.c` (defaults to `abort()`); the test binary overrides it with a strong definition that calls `_exit(134)` (128+SIGABRT convention), bypassing ReportCrash entirely. `expect_abort()` updated to accept both `WIFSIGNALED(SIGABRT)` and `WEXITSTATUS==134`. Full suite now completes in ~90s.

- ✅ **JIT Phase 3.2: actors work in the REPL** — `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md` Phase 3.2. Actor declarations parsed and typechecked fine, and `spawn`/`send`/`run_until_idle` compiled under the JIT — but the JIT backend calls into the runtime's native actor scheduler (`march_send`, `march_run_until_idle`) while the REPL registers actors only in the interpreter's scheduler. Result: sends succeeded, `run_until_idle()` returned `1` instead of `()`, and `println` inside message handlers vanished (they ran in a scheduler with no actor registered). Fix in `lib/repl/repl.ml`: a new `actors_declared` flag flips the moment any `DActor` is processed; once set, the three JIT paths (fn decl, let decl, ReplExpr) fall through to the interpreter in both `run_simple` and `run_tui`. Interpreter then owns spawn/send/receive for the rest of the session — consistent scheduler, visible `println` output. Smoke test: `actor Counter ... ; spawn ; send(Inc(5)) ; send(Probe()) ; run_until_idle()` now prints `5` and returns `()`, matching file mode.

- ✅ **JIT Phase 3.4: user modules from `MARCH_LIB_PATH` resolve in the REPL** — `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md` Phase 3.4. The REPL already loaded user `.march` files on `use UserMod` via `Resolver.resolve_imports` and typechecked/eval'd the returned `DMod`, but `Module_registry` (consulted by `qualified_error_msg` for `Mod.member` accesses) was never updated for user modules — so `MyMod.greet("world")` always tripped `Unknown module MyMod`. Fix in `lib/repl/repl.ml`: after `resolve_imports`, each `DMod` gets run through `Module_registry.extract_exports` and registered via `Module_registry.register`. Applied to both REPL paths (`run_simple` for stdin, `run_tui` for interactive). New public API in `lib/modules/module_registry.mli`: `extract_exports` is now a top-level export. Also verified that 3.1 (user impls for stdlib interfaces) and 3.3 (records) already work without additional changes. Smoke test: 54/54 PASS. Full dune suite: 147/147 PASS in 29.7s.

- ✅ **JIT Phase 2.3: in-process ORC LLJIT backend behind `MARCH_JIT_BACKEND=orc`** — `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md` Phase 2.3. New `lib/jit/jit_orc_stubs.c` drives LLVM's ORCv2 C API: `LLVMOrcCreateLLJIT`, `LLVMOrcLLJITAddLLVMIRModule`, `LLVMOrcLLJITLookup`, process-wide `DynamicLibrarySearchGenerator` so already-dlopen'd stdlib/runtime symbols resolve inside the JIT. libLLVM.dylib is `dlopen`'d lazily on first `Jit_orc.create` (fallback paths for Homebrew / system, `MARCH_LLVM_LIB` override); linker flag `-Wl,-undefined,dynamic_lookup` lets us reference LLVM symbols without a link-time dep (avoids +157 MB on every startup). `Repl_jit.compile_fragment` is now a backend dispatcher returning a `fragment_handle` sum; `lookup_sym` abstracts `Jit.dlsym` vs ORC lookup. Clang path still handles the stdlib prelude and is the default. **Measured gain on 30-fragment REPL: 4.52 s → 0.19 s (~24× end-to-end); per-fragment latency 145 ms → 0.2 ms after an 11 ms LLJIT warmup.** Smoke test: 54/54 PASS on both backends.

- ✅ **JIT Phase 2.2: fragment IR piped to clang via stdin** — `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md` Phase 2.2. `compile_fragment` in `lib/jit/repl_jit.ml` replaced the `.ll`-file + `clang path.ll` invocation with `Unix.open_process` feeding `clang -x ir ... -` on stdin. `MARCH_KEEP_LL=1` still mirrors IR to disk before compilation; compile failures also dump IR so `clang -x ir repl_N.ll` reproduces. Measured gain on 30-fragment REPL: zero (within noise) — the 15 KB file write is free on hot-cache APFS, and clang parses IR at the same cost either way. Kept as a simplification: common path no longer touches the filesystem, no stale `.ll` leak on SIGKILL mid-compile. Smoke test: 54/54 PASS.

- ✅ **JIT Phase 2.1: fragment clang dropped to `-O0 -fno-lto`** — `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md` Phase 2.1. `lib/jit/repl_jit.ml:97` changed from `-O1` to `-O0 -fno-lto`. Stdlib stays at -O1 (cached). Measured impact on current fragment sizes (~320 lines / 15 KB of IR): no observable speedup — clang wall time is ~40 ms at either optimization level, dominated by driver startup rather than opt passes. Kept as a defensive 1-line change (one-shot code shouldn't pay for optimization, and `-fno-lto` guards against future toolchain defaults). Smoke test: 54/54 PASS. Real fragment-latency budget is on the OCaml side (parse/typecheck/TIR lower/LLVM emit) — addressed by later phases.

- ✅ **JIT Phase 1 correctness: tuple pp tag bug, user ADT printing, retty recovery, tmp-file cleanup** — Five correctness fixes in `lib/jit/repl_jit.ml` from `specs/plans/2026-04-22-jit-improvements-plan.md` Phase 1. (1.1) `pp_field` gained a `~tagged:bool` parameter so tuples (untagged scalar slots) no longer shift-right-by-1; `(42, 99)` now prints `(42, 99)`, not `(21, 49)`. (1.2) New `global_type_defs` hashtable on the JIT context, populated from `tir.tm_types` on every `run_expr`/`run_decl`; `pp_heap_value` consults it for user-declared `TDVariant`/`TDRecord`, doing positional TVar→arg substitution via new `collect_tvars`/`subst_ty` helpers. User ADTs now render as `Red` / `Green(42)` instead of `#<tag:0>`. (1.3) `find_retvar` (renamed to `find_retty`) now recurses through `ECase` branches and prefers the EAtom var's concrete `v_ty` over a global hashtable lookup, recovering types for polymorphic match/if-shaped expressions. (1.5) `create` now wipes stale `repl_*.ll`/`repl_*.so` from `march_jit/` on startup (previously only a clean `cleanup` call reclaimed them — SIGKILL'd sessions leaked). `MARCH_KEEP_LL` opts out for debugging. (1.6) Stale cross-fragment-declare KNOWN ISSUE comment in `test/repl_smoke_test.sh` replaced; new acceptance tests added for tuple pp (4) and user ADT pp (3).

- ✅ **JIT monomorphization: TVar "_" wildcard specialization + Map.insert hash dispatch** — Two correctness fixes in `lib/tir/mono.ml`. (1) `match_ty`: `TVar "_"` (the lowering placeholder for spans absent from the REPL type_map) is now treated as lower-priority than any concrete type, so `{b→Int}` correctly replaces a spurious `{b→TVar "_"}` binding. Without this, `fold_left$List_Int$Int` recursively called the generic `fold_left$List_Int$V__` instead of itself, halving every result. (2) `rewrite_calls`: the "already monomorphic" guard now also excludes functions with `TVar "_"` params. Without this, `Map.key_hash(k : TVar "_")` was emitted as-is; the internal `hash(k)` call stayed as bare `@hash`, which resolves to OCaml's `hash.c` that asserts on non-integer inputs — crashing on any `Map.insert` call in JIT mode. Also updated `test/repl_smoke_test.sh` to correct match syntax, fold arg order, and Map test expectations (map now displays `HamtMap(HEmpty/HLeaf(...))`).

- ✅ **TOML type-conflict detection for table headers** — `stdlib/toml.march`: `ensure_table_at_path` silently replaced non-table values (e.g. `TInt`) with an empty subtable when processing `[a.b]` after `a = 1`, returning Ok instead of an error. Fix: return `[("__toml_conflict__", TNull)]` sentinel on type mismatch and propagate via `is_err_marker` check in the caller, which converts it to `Err(TomlError("type conflict at table header: ...", ...))`. Fixes the failing `"type conflict at key path is an error"` test in `test_toml.march`.

- ✅ **`Xml` stdlib module — non-validating XML parser** — `stdlib/xml.march`: pure-March iterative stack-based XML parser (no FFI, namespace-unaware). Handles: elements, attributes (double/single-quoted), text nodes, CDATA sections, comments, processing instructions, XML declaration (skipped), DOCTYPE (skipped), entity references (`&lt;` `&gt;` `&amp;` `&quot;` `&apos;`), self-closing tags, nested elements of arbitrary depth. Stack-based design (`XmlFrame` type) eliminates mutual recursion between element/content parsing — all recursive calls are tail calls. Public API: `parse/1`, `parse_exn/1`, `root/1`, `tag/1`, `text/1`, `attr/2`, `children/1`, `elements/1`, `find/2`, `find_all/2`, `elem/3`, `text_node/1`, `to_string/1`, `to_string_pretty/1`. `~xml"..."` sigil added to `stdlib/sigil.march`. 40 tests in `test/stdlib/test_xml.march`. Wired into `bin/main.ml`, `test/test_stdlib_march.ml`, and `sigil.march`.

- ✅ **Whole-program native codegen: cross-module ADT `==`, qualified ctor allocation, unit-wide impls, qualified-pattern exhaustiveness** — Four fixes surfaced by compiling depot's 43-file test suite as one unit. (1) `llvm_emit.ml` `ensure_adt_eq_fn`: ctor lookup now resolves the use-site TCon's (short) type name against build_ctor_info's module-qualified keys ("Ast.Expr.Col") — exact match, else dot-suffix candidates merged by per-tag layout compatibility (largest ctor set wins, e.g. Ast.SortDir ⊇ DataFrame.SortDir); genuinely conflicting layouts return None. Memoization in `emitted_eq_fns` moved AFTER ctor resolution — registering first made later requests return `Some` for an eq function whose body was never emitted ("use of undefined value '@__march_eq_Expr'" at clang time). (2) `lower.ml` `ECon`: module-/type-qualified ctor references ("AeLib.AeWrap") were embedded verbatim in the EAlloc key, producing "AeShape.AeLib.AeWrap" which matched nothing and silently defaulted the runtime tag to 0 — wrong values AND wrong eq results; now only the final segment is kept. (3) `typecheck.ml` pass 1: `register_impl_shape` pre-registers every `impl` (entry + sibling DMods) with leniently-converted types so CInterface constraints from modules checked earlier discharge against impls declared in modules checked later — impl visibility no longer depends on file order. (4) `typecheck.ml` `norm_pat`: qualified constructor patterns ("MarchType.TBool", "Ast.Query") kept their dotted text, so the exhaustiveness checker reported the bare ctor as a missing case; now compares last segments. Regression fixture `test/imports/adt_eq_native/` (3 modules incl. a same-short-name layout-compatible AeDir pair) compiles natively and RUNS, panicking on any wrong comparison. Full suite green.

- ✅ **`Toml` stdlib module — TOML v1.0 parser** — `stdlib/toml.march`: pure-March recursive-descent TOML v1.0 parser (no FFI). Handles: basic/literal/multiline strings (with escape sequences), integers (signed, underscore separators), floats (decimal, scientific notation, inf/nan via `string_to_float`), booleans, arrays, inline tables, `[table]` headers, `[[array-of-tables]]` headers, dotted keys, quoted keys, comments. Public API: `parse/1`, `parse_exn/1`, `to_string/1`, `get/2`, `get_in/2`, `get_str/2`, `get_int/2`, `get_float/2`, `get_bool/2`, `get_table/2`, `get_array/2`. `~toml"..."` sigil added to `stdlib/sigil.march`. 157 tests in `test/stdlib/test_toml.march`. Wired into `bin/main.ml`, `test/test_stdlib_march.ml`, and `sigil.march`.

- ✅ **`Socket` stdlib module — TCP socket client** — `stdlib/socket.march`: pure-March wrapper over `tcp_connect`/`tcp_send_all`/`tcp_recv_all`/`tcp_close` builtins. `SocketError` type: `ConnectionFailed | WriteFailed | RecvFailed`. Public API: `connect/2`, `write/2`, `recv/2`, `recv_timeout/3`, `close/1`, `with_connection/3`, `error_message/1`. 9 tests in `test/stdlib/test_socket.march` (constructors, error messages, connection-refused, with_connection propagation). Wired into `bin/main.ml` and `test/test_stdlib_march.ml`.

- ✅ **`Dns` stdlib module — hostname resolution** — `stdlib/dns.march`: wraps a new `dns_resolve` eval builtin that calls POSIX `getaddrinfo(3)`. `DnsError` type: `NotFound | ResolveError`. Public API: `resolve/1`, `resolve_one/1`, `is_ip/1`, `error_message/1`. New `dns_resolve` builtin added to `lib/eval/eval.ml` (returns `Result(List(String), String)`) and `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`. 11 tests in `test/stdlib/test_dns.march` (constructors, error messages, is_ip, localhost resolve, NXDOMAIN). Wired into `bin/main.ml` and `test/test_stdlib_march.ml`.

- ✅ **`Yaml` stdlib module — YAML 1.2 subset parser** — `stdlib/yaml.march`: pure-March YAML parser (no FFI). Handles: block mappings, block sequences, plain/single-quoted/double-quoted scalars, flow sequences `[]`, flow mappings `{}`, comments `#`, document separator `---`, implicit type coercion (bool/null/int/float). `YamlValue` type: `YStr | YInt | YFloat | YBool | YNull | YSeq | YMap`. Public API: `parse/1`, `parse_exn/1`, `get/2`, `get_in/2`, `get_str/2`, `get_int/2`, `get_bool/2`, `get_seq/1`, `to_string/1`. `~yaml"..."` sigil added to `stdlib/sigil.march`. 36 tests in `test/stdlib/test_yaml.march` (scalars, block seq/map, nested, comments, doc sep, flow, navigation, to_string, errors, sigil). Wired into `bin/main.ml`, `test/test_stdlib_march.ml`, and `sigil.march`.

- ✅ **`Msgpack` stdlib module — MessagePack binary serialization** — `stdlib/msgpack.march`: pure-March MessagePack encoder/decoder with no external dependencies. `Value` type: `Null | Bool(Bool) | Int(Int) | Str(String) | Bin(List(Int)) | Array(List(Value)) | Map(List((Value, Value)))`. Full format coverage: fixint (−32–127, 1 byte), int8/uint8/int16/uint16/int32/uint32/int64/uint64 (2–9 bytes), fixstr/str8/str16/str32, fixbin/bin8/bin16/bin32, fixarray/array16/array32, fixmap/map16/map32, fixnull/bool. Integer arithmetic avoids `float_bits` (uses sign-extension via `hi_s = if b0 >= 128 do b0 - 256 else b0 end` for 63-bit safe int64 reconstruction). `impl Eq(Value)` with recursive structural equality helpers. Public API: `encode/1`, `decode/1`, `decode_all/1`, plus convenience constructors `null/0`, `bool/1`, `int/1`, `str/1`, `bin/1`, `array/1`, `map/1`. 195 unit tests in `test/stdlib/test_msgpack.march` + 12 Msgpack property tests added to `test/stdlib/test_properties.march` (round-trips for all value types, encode non-empty invariant, fixint 1-byte invariant, decode_all list recovery). Wired into `bin/main.ml` and `test/test_stdlib_march.ml`.

- ✅ **Adversarial compiler audit: 8 safety/correctness bugs fixed** — Systematic adversarial review of eval, runtime C, and LLVM emit. (1) Float div-by-zero: `eval.ml` `/` and `/.` operators now guard `b <> 0.0`; `march_checked_fdiv` added to `march_runtime.c`; `llvm_emit.ml` calls it instead of emitting bare `fdiv`. (2) Array bounds: `march_typed_array_get/set` now call `typed_array_check_bounds` static helper. (3) String overflow: `march_string_concat` and `march_string_repeat` use `__builtin_add_overflow` / `__builtin_mul_overflow`. (4) Malloc NULL: `typed_array_alloc` checks `malloc` return. (5) Unsafe closure dispatch: `march_typed_array_filter/map/fold` now use `call_closure_1` / `call_closure_2` inline helpers instead of raw pointer arithmetic. (6) Perceus scrutinee-in-body: documented as intentional conservative choice (any-path `name_free_in` vs all-path) in `perceus.ml`. (7) TRecord sort invariant: `assert_trecord_sorted` debug assertion added to `typecheck.ml` (gated on `MARCH_DEBUG_TC`). 7 new regression tests in `adversarial-regressions` group. 1363 → 1370 tests.

- ✅ **Fix Perceus EApp callee spurious EIncRC (`@__` undefined symbol)** — `lib/tir/perceus.ml`: commit `831e315` changed `needs_rc(TFn _)` to `true`, causing Perceus to insert `EIncRC` for the callee name in every `EApp`. After defun, `EApp` callees are always top-level function symbols (code addresses), never heap-allocated closures. For operators like `&&` and `||`, whose `llvm_name` maps to `@__`, this produced a call to an undefined symbol that failed to link. Fix: removed the callee `f.v_name` from (1) liveness propagation in `live_before`, (2) `find_inc_vars` in `insert_rc_expr`, and (3) live-before propagation in the EApp arm — three targeted changes covering all call-paths. `ECallPtr` (indirect dispatch through closure structs) is unaffected. All 147 tests pass.

- ✅ **Fix let-generalization hole for forward-referenced polymorphic `pfn` helpers** — `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` `generalize`: when a function is generalized to a `Poly` scheme, the quantified `TVar` refs are now deep-copied with fresh, isolated allocations. Previously the scheme shared the original mutable `TVar` refs with forward-reference placeholders used by mutual-recursion partners processed later in pass 2.
