- ✅ **Logger module additions** — `Logger.level_from_string`, `level_rank`, `level_enabled`, `format_entry`, `log_if` added to `stdlib/logger.march`
- ✅ **TCE structural recursion warning** — `check_tail_position` in `typecheck.ml` now emits a Warning when a structurally-recursive (but non-tail) call is detected; distinguishes arithmetic reductions (`n-1`) from pattern-bound sub-components
- ✅ **stdlib: Random module** — `stdlib/random.march`: purely-functional pseudorandom number generation using xoshiro256** algorithm; `Rng` state record; `seed/next_int/next_float/next_bool/next_range/shuffle`; all functions pure (take Rng, return `(value, Rng)`); no global mutable state; registered in `bin/main.ml` stdlib load order.
- ✅ **stdlib: Stats module** — `stdlib/stats.march`: descriptive statistics over `List(Float)`; `mean/variance/std_dev/median/mode/min/max/range/percentile/covariance/pearson_correlation`; pure functional, no external dependencies; registered in `bin/main.ml`.
- ✅ **stdlib: Plot module** — `stdlib/plot.march`: SVG chart generation; `Color`/`Style`/`Series`/`Chart` types; `line_series/scatter_series/bar_series/histogram_series`; `new/add_series/set_title/set_size/set_padding/to_svg`; self-contained pure string building, no external dependencies; registered in `bin/main.ml`.
- ✅ **`describe` keyword for test grouping** — `describe "name" do ... end` syntax groups tests under a label; `DESCRIBE` lexer token; `DDescribe` AST node; desugared recursively; typechecked as Unit; `collect_test_decls` flattens describe groups with prefixed names (e.g. `"auth login success"`); fully wired through all compiler passes (lexer, parser, AST, desugar, typecheck, eval, TIR lower, formatter, LSP analysis).
- ✅ **FFI interpreter dispatch (`extern` blocks)** — `VForeign(lib, sym)` value; `foreign_stubs` hashtable in `eval.ml` mapping `(lib_name, symbol_name)` to OCaml stubs; `DExtern` in `eval_decl` binds each extern function to a `VForeign` stub; `apply_inner` dispatches `VForeign` via the stub table; pre-registered: 25 math functions (`sqrt`, `sin`, `cos`, `pow`, etc.), `puts`; `declared_names`/`make_recursive_env` updated to include extern bindings.
- ✅ **New March-native stdlib test files** — `test/stdlib/test_random.march`, `test_stats.march`, `test_plot.march`, `test_bigint.march`, `test_decimal.march`, `test_enum.march`, `test_math.march`, `test_queue.march`, `test_string.march` added.
- ✅ **Phoenix-style Channels stdlib (Part 6)** — `stdlib/pubsub.march` (sharded PubSub: `PubSubState`, subscribe/unsubscribe/get_subscribers, `topic_matches` with wildcard prefix, `topic_shard`, runtime stubs), `stdlib/channel.march` (Socket type, HandleResult, ChannelMsg, ChannelRoute, JSON wire serialization/parsing, reply/error/close message helpers), `stdlib/channel_server.march` (ChannelMailbox, JoinResult, ChannelConfig, do_join/do_leave, apply_result, handle_broadcast, shard helpers, select_shard), `stdlib/presence.march` (PresenceMeta/Entry/State, track_state/untrack_state/list_state, diff JSON helpers), `stdlib/channel_socket.march` (SocketConfig, ActiveChannels registry, topic routing, plug_for/ws_loop WebSocket adapter). 5 new stdlib test modules: `test_pubsub.march` (18 tests), `test_channel.march` (25 tests), `test_channel_server.march` (14 tests), `test_presence.march` (16 tests), `test_channel_socket.march` (12 tests). All passing.
- ✅ **Vault stdlib module** — `stdlib/vault.march` (ETS-like in-memory KV store; `new`, `set`, `set_ttl`, `get`, `drop`, `update`, `size`, `get_or`, `has`); `VVaultHandle` value variant + `vault_row`/`vault_table` types in `lib/eval/eval.ml`; 7 builtins (`vault_new`, `vault_set`, `vault_set_ttl`, `vault_get`, `vault_drop`, `vault_update`, `vault_size`); lazy TTL expiry checked on read; 10 tests in `vault stdlib` group all passing.
- ✅ **JIT REPL type_map fix — 14 pre-existing test failures resolved** — Root cause: `run_expr`/`run_decl`/`precompile_stdlib` in `lib/jit/repl_jit.ml` received empty `type_map` from tests, causing `ty_of_span` to return `TVar "_"` for all variable references during TIR lowering. With `TVar "_"`, `build_subst` in mono returned `[]` for polymorphic calls (e.g., `List.reverse` inside `range_step`), preventing specialization. The unspecialized function was absent from `tm_fns` → not in `top_level` → defun treated it as a free variable → spurious closure capture → invalid LLVM IR (load from non-existent alloca) → clang error. Fix: `run_expr`, `run_decl`, and `precompile_stdlib` now compute `type_map` internally via `March_typecheck.Typecheck.check_module m` (ignoring the passed `~type_map`), ensuring all expression spans have proper types before TIR lowering. Fixed 10 `repl_jit_regression` + 3 `repl_compiler_parity` + 1 `jit_eval_simple_expr` boolean-display failure = 14 tests. Also reverted debug code from `compile_fragment` that copied failing .ll files to `/tmp/march_debug/`.
- ✅ **REPL JIT parity — 5 remaining test failures resolved** — Three root-cause bugs fixed: (1) `ptr 0` in LLVM string equality call (`march_string_eq`) — occurs when one operand is an i64 literal (e.g. bool false as `"0"`); fix: coerce both operands to `ptr` via `coerce ctx` before formatting the call instruction in `llvm_emit.ml`. (2) `%List.length.addr` undefined — polymorphic REPL globals (free vars with `TVar "_"` type) caused `build_subst` in `mono.ml` to return `[]` for `EApp` calls, silently skipping worklist addition; the function was never emitted, defun converted it to `ECallPtr`, and LLVM emitted an invalid alloca load; fix: in the `EApp` branch of `rewrite_calls`, when `subst = []` add to worklist (matching the existing `ECallPtr` behavior). (3) Bool `v` displayed as `"1"` — when evaluating a REPL global with `TVar "_"` return type, the `Int64.to_string` path was used instead of the bool formatter; fix: added `global_tir_tys : (string, Tir.ty) Hashtbl.t` to `repl_jit` context, populated on each global store, and used in the `TVar _` display branch to look up the actual TIR type via body inspection. Also added `string_length` to `is_builtin_fn`, `mangle_extern` (→ `march_string_byte_length`), and `builtin_ret_ty` in `llvm_emit.ml` — it was registered in typecheck and defun but missing from LLVM emit, causing `@string_length` undefined errors.
- ✅ **`forge notebook` ergonomics (Livebook-class UX)** — `forge/tasks/notebook_server.march`: (1) Auto-print REPL semantics — each code cell wrapped in `pfn __cell_N__()`, last expression captured and printed if `to_string(__r__) != "()"`, giving Jupyter-style auto-output without explicit `println`. (2) Execution timing — client records `Date.now()` on `running` message and computes elapsed on `output`; displayed as `· 42ms` or `· 1.2s` in the cell gutter. (3) Shift+Enter muscle memory — runs current cell and advances focus to the next cell (or creates a new one if at the end), matching Jupyter workflow. (4) Inline HTML/SVG rendering — `renderOutput(text)` detects `<svg`, `<table`, `<!DOCTYPE`, `<html` prefix and renders as raw HTML in `.cell-out-html` div instead of escaped text; enables rich DataFrame tables and Plot SVG charts. (5) Styled `.cell-out-html` CSS — dark-theme table styling (purple column headers, row hover, monospace font); SVG auto-scaled to container width. (6) Bug fixes: `make_cell_fn` and `generate_server_runner` now strip trailing `\n` before splitting (prevents empty indented lines that cause parse errors); empty cell bodies emit `()` instead of empty function body (which is a parse error). `stdlib/dataframe.march`: `DataFrame.to_html(df)` — renders a DataFrame as a styled HTML table string; auto-detected by the notebook renderer and displayed inline; `pfn html_esc` helper for XSS-safe attribute/text encoding; caption shows row × column counts.
- ✅ **Uniform low-bit integer tagging (Phases 1 + 2)** — Fixed SIGSEGV when integers pass through polymorphic ADT fields (e.g. `List(Int)` with n ≥ 4096). Tag scheme: immediates stored as `(n << 1) | 1` (low bit 1); heap pointers remain even (always aligned). Changes: (1) `IS_HEAP_PTR` macro in `runtime/march_runtime.c` extended with `((uintptr_t)(p) & 1u) == 0` as first clause (short-circuits before page/sign guards on hot RC path). (2) `coerce ("i64", "ptr")` in `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` now emits `shl i64 %v, 1` + `or i64 %s, 1` + `inttoptr` to tag; `coerce ("ptr", "i64")` emits `ptrtoint` + `ashr i64 %i, 1` to untag (sign-preserving). (3) All three closure-wrapper emitters (top-level fn ref, AVar cross-module ref, REPL closure wrap) tag i64 return values before `inttoptr`. (4) `march_value_to_string` checks low bit first and calls `march_int_to_string((intptr_t)p >> 1)` for tagged immediates (Phase 2 fix, lands with Phase 1). Spec: `specs/plans/2026-04-16-uniform-integer-tagging.md`. Regression tests: `test_int_tag_coerce_ir` (shl+or+ashr appear in IR for `List(Int)` code), `test_int_tag_wrapper_ir` (wrapper emits tagged return). `test_march.exe` 1342 → 1358 (+2 new llvm_emit tests, +14 previously covered by tag-dependent paths now exercised).
- ✅ **Perceus RC crash fix: `TFn` ownership tracking + cross-branch EDecRC** — Fixed a `march_decrc` crash where a `.text-segment address` was being passed to the RC system during `Map.fold` with a `Bytes` accumulator. Two interlocking bugs in `lib/tir/perceus.ml`: (1) `needs_rc (TFn _)` was `false` — Perceus generated zero RC operations for function-typed parameters (e.g. `f` in `Map.node_fold`), causing no EIncRC when `f` is captured into the `go` closure and no EDecRC in the HEmpty arm where `f` is unused; the closure's code pointer slot was then misidentified as a Bytes payload pointer. Fix: changed to `needs_rc (TFn _) = true` with a guard comment explaining that post-defun any `AVar` with `TFn` type is a heap-allocated closure struct (never a raw code pointer — those are `ADefRef` and are never in AVar liveness; `llvm_ty (TFn _) = "ptr"` so RC ops emit correctly at the LLVM level). (2) ECase handler was discarding `live_before_br` for each branch — variables live in some branches but dead in others never got `EDecRC` in the dead branches (a second class of RC leak). Fix: collect `live_before_br` per branch, compute their union (`union_live_br`), and insert `EDecRC v` at the head of any branch where `v` ∈ `union_live_br` but `v` ∉ branch's `live_before_br` (excluding closure FVs, arm-bound vars, and vars live after the case). New `_var_ctx` global (`Tir.var StringMap.t ref`) maps names to their typed `Tir.var` records for emitting correctly-typed EDecRC ops. `_var_ctx` is seeded from function params in `insert_rc` and extended via ELet with save/restore semantics to avoid stale cross-branch interference. All tests pass.
- ✅ **JIT REPL `println(42)` segfault fix** — `println(42)` (and any prelude fn called with a non-string literal) caused SIGSEGV in JIT. Root cause: `lower_module`'s Pass 2 iterated only `m.mod_decls`, so top-level `DFn` declarations from `stdlib_context` (prelude fns like `println`, `str`, `map`) were never lowered into `fn_table`. With no `fn_table` entry, mono could not specialize `println$Int`; the LLVM emitter fell back to `mangle_extern` and emitted `call void @march_println(i64 42)` — a C runtime call with wrong arg type — causing SIGSEGV. Fix: added a pre-pass before Pass 2 in `lib/tir/lower.ml` that lowers `DFn` from `stdlib_context` (excluding `DMod`/`DImpl`, which are handled lazily). Also fixed four `EApp` sites in `lower.ml` that used `unknown_ty` instead of concrete `TFn` types for builtin operator references, and added `march_compare_*/march_hash_*` to `builtin_ret_ty` in `llvm_emit.ml`.
- ✅ **Generic `to_string` / polymorphic `println` via Show interface** — `stdlib/prelude.march`: `impl Show(List(a)) when Show(a)`, `impl Show(Option(a)) when Show(a)`, `impl Show(Result(a,e)) when Show(a), Show(e)` added; `println(x)` now delegates to `show(x)` making it polymorphic over all Show types; `str(x)` alias for `to_string`. `lib/eval/eval.ml`: `show_dispatch` helper routes `show` / `to_string` / `println` through `impl_tbl` keyed by `(interface_name, type_name)`; builtin constructors `Ok/Err/Some/None/Cons/Nil` pre-populated in `ctor_type_tbl` (in both `reset_scheduler_state` and `eval_module_env`) so `type_name_of_value` resolves `VCon("Err",…)` → `"Result"`; Show impl methods stored as plain `VClosure(env, params, body)` (not self-referential) so `show` inside an impl body calls the dispatcher, not itself. `lib/tir/lower.ml`: `Show$Unit.show` synthetic builtin added. 7 new tests in `test/test_march.ml` (`show List eval`, `show Option Some eval`, `show Option None eval`, `show Result Ok eval`, `show Result Err eval`, `show nested list eval`, `println polymorphic typecheck`). `test_march.exe` 1358 → 1365.
- ✅ **Record field auto-satisfy for single-method interfaces (Item 3)** — When discharging `CInterface(Iface, TRecord[...])` with no explicit `impl`, the typechecker now auto-satisfies if the interface has exactly one accessor method (`a -> T`) and the anonymous record has a matching field with the correct return type. `discharge_constraints` checks `env.interfaces`, inspects the single method's surface type, converts the return type with `surface_ty`, and uses `impl_matches_ty` for a pure structural comparison. Named types (`TCon`) never auto-satisfy — they require explicit `impl` declarations. 9 new tests in `record_auto_satisfy` group covering: matching field (ok), wrong type (error), missing field (error), multi-method interface (no auto), binary method (no auto), named type (no auto), named type + explicit impl (ok), when-constraint (ok), two record shapes (ok).
- ✅ **Capability Phase 1 — IO.Random/IO.Database in hierarchy + stdlib `needs` annotations (Item 1)** — Added `IO.Random` and `IO.Database` to `io_cap_hierarchy` in `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`. Added `needs` capability declarations to 8 stdlib modules: `stdlib/io.march` (`needs IO.Console`), `stdlib/file.march` (`needs IO.FileRead`, `needs IO.FileWrite`), `stdlib/dir.march` (`needs IO.FileRead`, `needs IO.FileWrite`), `stdlib/system.march` (`needs IO.Process`, `needs IO.Clock`), `stdlib/csv.march` (`needs IO.FileRead`), `stdlib/uuid.march` (`needs IO.Random`), `stdlib/random.march` (`needs IO.Random`), `stdlib/crypto.march` (`needs IO.Random`). These declarations make stdlib module capability requirements machine-checkable and self-documenting.
- ✅ **Capability Phase 2 — body-scan enforcement for builtin calls (Item 2)** — Added `calls_in_expr` AST walker (collects `(fn_name, span)` from EApp nodes), `builtin_cap_table` (~65-entry association list mapping runtime builtins to IO capabilities), and a body-scan pass inside `check_module_needs`. The pass walks each DFn clause body and DLet bind_expr, looks up each call in `builtin_cap_table`, deduplicates by cap name, and emits `Err.warning` (not error) for each implied capability not covered by a declared `needs`. Check 2 (unused `needs`) now also counts body-inferred calls as usage, preventing false "declared but not used" warnings. 14 new tests in `cap_body_enforce` group covering: happy path, missing console/fileread/filewrite/random/clock/process, warning-not-error, no-dup-warning, parent-cap umbrella, two missing caps, DLet body scanning, body satisfies declared needs, pure module silence.
- ✅ **Cap(IO.Spawn) — task-spawning capability** — `IO.Spawn` added as a new leaf under `IO` in `io_cap_hierarchy`. Five builtins mapped in `builtin_cap_table`: `task_spawn`, `task_spawn_link`, `task_spawn_steal`, `task_spawn_with_cancel`, `get_work_pool`. The body-scan pass (Phase 2) automatically enforces the new cap: modules that spawn tasks without `needs IO.Spawn` get a warning; `needs IO` (or any ancestor) satisfies it. 3 new tests in `cap_body_enforce` group: missing spawn warns, declared spawn suppresses warning, parent `IO` covers spawn.
- ✅ **Cap(IO.Mut) — shared mutable state capability** — `IO.Mut` added as a leaf under `IO`. 15 Vault builtins added to `builtin_cap_table` (all vault_* operations, including reads — touching shared mutable state is a side effect regardless of read/write direction). `stdlib/vault.march` annotated with `needs IO.Mut`. 3 new tests: missing warns, declared suppresses, parent IO umbrella.
- ✅ **Cap(IO.NetConnect.TLS) — encrypted transport capability** — `IO.NetConnect.TLS` added as a child of `IO.NetConnect`. 8 TLS builtins added (tls_client_ctx, tls_server_ctx, tls_connect, tls_accept, tls_read, tls_write, tls_negotiated_alpn, tls_peer_cn; cleanup ops tls_close/tls_ctx_free exempt). `stdlib/tls.march` annotated with `needs IO.NetConnect.TLS`. Declaring this cap without IO.NetConnect proves a module uses only encrypted connections. 3 new tests.
- ✅ **Cap(IO.Telemetry) — observability declaration-only capability** — `IO.Telemetry` added as a leaf under `IO`. Declaration-only (no builtins in body-scan table yet — telemetry module lives in the bastion repo): modules declare `needs IO.Telemetry` as a semantic annotation; transitive enforcement (Check 4) propagates it to importers. 1 new test. `docs/capabilities.md` updated with sections for all three new caps and a corrected hierarchy tree.
- [ ] **Cap(IO.NetListen) — body-scan enforcement for TCP bind/listen** — Already in `io_cap_hierarchy`; wire 3 builtins (`tcp_listen`, `tcp_accept`, `tcp_bind`) to `builtin_cap_table`, annotate `stdlib/http_server.march`, add 3 tests. Spec: `specs/capability-system-design.md §Future Capabilities §1`. **No blocker — immediately actionable.**
- [ ] **Cap(IO.WebSocket) — WebSocket session capability** — New leaf under `IO.NetConnect`. Wire `ws_connect`, `ws_send`, `ws_recv`, `ws_close`, `ws_upgrade`, `ws_ping` to `builtin_cap_table`; annotate `stdlib/websocket.march`. Confirm actual builtin names in `runtime/*.c` first. Spec: `specs/capability-system-design.md §Future Capabilities §5`.
- [ ] **Cap(IO.Crypto) — cryptographic operations capability** — New leaf under `IO` (sibling of IO.Random). Wire `crypto_sha256`, `crypto_sha512`, `crypto_hmac`, `crypto_hash_pw`, `crypto_verify_pw`, `crypto_secure_cmp`; update `stdlib/crypto.march` annotation. Spec: `specs/capability-system-design.md §Future Capabilities §6`.
- [ ] **Cap(IO.Timer) — scheduled callback capability** — New leaf under `IO`. Wire `timer_after`, `timer_interval`, `timer_cancel`, `sleep_ms`. Blocked on confirming these builtin names in the runtime. Spec: `specs/capability-system-design.md §Future Capabilities §4`.
- [ ] **Cap(IO.Database) — upgrade from declaration-only to enforced** — Currently declaration-only. Wire Depot builtins (`db_query`, `db_exec`, etc.) when Depot stdlib lands in the march compiler. Spec: `specs/capability-system-design.md §Future Capabilities §2`.
- [ ] **Cap(IO.IPC) — inter-process communication capability** — New leaf under `IO.Process`. Wire pipe/unix-socket/signal builtins when IPC runtime is written. Spec: `specs/capability-system-design.md §Future Capabilities §3`.
- ✅ **Cap(IO.Foreign) — FFI meta-capability** — New leaf under `IO` + sub-cap `IO.Foreign.Blocking` (child). Wired via `extern_cap_uses` in `check_module_needs` (Check 1c): any `DExtern` node implies `IO.Foreign`; a `DExtern` with any `ef_blocking = true` fn also implies `IO.Foreign.Blocking`. `IO.Foreign` subsumes `IO.Foreign.Blocking` (parent covers child). 4 compiler tests (300 total). All 9 `test/native/ffi_*.march` files annotated. `docs/capabilities.md` updated with IO.Foreign section and hierarchy tree.
- ✅ **Cross-module Boxed-vs-Niche `Option` repr mismatch resolved** — validated by removing forgepm's `force_copy` workaround (isolated worktree only) and running the full Playwright suite: 7/7 publish tests, 29/30 overall. See `specs/2026-06-30-mono-cross-module-option-repr-bug.md`.
- ✅ **TCO loop stack-overflow fixes (llvm_emit.ml)** — (1) `llvm.stacksave`/`llvm.stackrestore` around every TCO loop body, fixing unbounded per-iteration stack growth from `alloca`s inside the loop; (2) a second Perceus tail-call shape (`ESeq(EApp(self,args), dec_chain)`, arising from wildcarded constructor fields like `Cons(_, t)`) that was silently compiled as non-tail recursion. `Bytes.from_string`/`Bytes.length` now handle 200,000-element inputs; previously crashed above ~10,000. Full detail: `specs/2026-06-30-tco-loop-stack-overflow.md`.
- [ ] **Known limitation: nested (non-top-level) self-recursive closures never get TCO** — `fn go(...) do ... end` closures compile as an indirect `$apply` call, never a loop back-edge, regardless of the fixes above. Only top-level `pfn`/`fn` self-recursion is eligible for TCO. Not blocking (stdlib call sites were rewritten to top-level `pfn` helpers instead), but worth a compiler warning if a future change reintroduces the pattern in a hot path.
- ✅ **B5 — `EUpdate` on a type-erased record wrote past the allocation (llvm_emit.ml + runtime)** — when `get_record_fields` returns `[]` (record shape unknown at compile time — e.g. `record_from_list`/`record_put` results flowing through generic code), the old codegen still allocated a header-only cell (`emit_heap_alloc ctx 0 0`) and wrote every update at `field_index_for`'s `(0, TVar "_")` fallback offset, corrupting memory past the cell. Fixed with a new variadic runtime function `march_record_update_dyn` (`runtime/march_extras.c`, modeled on `march_record_field_dyn`): ONE call per update expression, one allocation, base fields copied, named fields overwritten, and a **runtime panic on any update name missing from the base shape** — the typechecker cannot validate names against an erased base (its TVar branch builds a partial record constraint from the update's own names), so `{ record_from_list([("a",1)]) with z: 99 }` typechecks; routing through `march_record_put` would have silently fabricated field `z`, and a per-field put-chain would have leaked N−1 intermediate cells (invisible to Perceus). Surface trigger confirmed reachable. 10 new checks in `test/imports/record_native/rn_entry.march` (compile-and-run fixture, now 28 checks) + 3 tests in `test/test_codegen.ml` (`erased_record_update_codegen`: IR asserts exactly one `march_record_update_dyn` call and zero chained `march_record_put` calls; compiled missing-field update panics with "no field" and exit 1; compiled 3-field update values match interpreter). Known divergence (documented): the interpreter's `ERecordUpdate` still silently appends missing update fields (eval.ml) — compiled fail-loud is the safer contract; aligning eval is a follow-up.

## Wave 1 — pipeline deep review P0 fixes (2026-07-01/02, `specs/analysis/2026-07-01-pipeline-deep-review.md`)

- ✅ **B3 — guard exhaustion returned literal `0` instead of panicking (lower.ml)** — commit `11925401`. Exhausted match guards now hoist the existing `nonexhaustive_panic` into both `compile_matrix_impl` fallbacks instead of fabricating a pointer-unsafe `LitInt 0`.
- ✅ **B4 — `PatRecord` and float-literal match arms silently dropped from match compilation (lower.ml, llvm_emit.ml)** — commit `e8a43b7b`. `pat_tag_and_subs` now handles both shapes; the swallowing `None -> ()` in the grouping loop fails loudly on any future unhandled kind instead of discarding the arm.
- ✅ **B9 — off-by-one in the `march_` runtime-extern prefix guard (llvm_emit.ml)** — commit `cd0770d9`. `String.sub n 0 7` (7 chars) vs the 6-char literal `"march_"` made the runtime-extern-as-first-class-value arm dead; fixed to match the three sibling `0 6` checks.
- ✅ **B10 — RC ops (`EIncRC`/`EDecRC`/`EFree`) skipped by name against top_fns/builtins with no shadow guard (llvm_emit.ml)** — commit `b98115da`. A local binding named `link`/`send`/etc. now gets correct RC ops; the five RC arms gained the `var_slot` shadow check `emit_atom` already applied twice.
- ✅ **B5 — `EUpdate` on a type-erased record wrote past the allocation (llvm_emit.ml + runtime)** — commit `0d1a829e`, see full entry above (Task 5).
- ✅ **B15 — raw newline inside a string literal desynced all subsequent diagnostic line numbers (lexer.mll)** — commit `f169070c`. `read_string`/`read_string_interp` now call `Lexing.new_line` on an embedded raw `\n`, matching the triple-quoted-string path.
- ✅ **FLOAT-filter — float-literal match arms failed to parse without a leading `|` (token_filter.ml)** — commit `6e376e4d`. Added `Parser.FLOAT` (and audited sibling literal tokens) to `is_pattern_start`.
- ✅ **B6 — pipe into a parenthesized `match` silently discarded the scrutinee (desugar.ml)** — commit `b579f848`. `x |> (match scrut do ... end)` now errors with a positioned diagnostic instead of matching on `x` and dropping `scrut`.
- ✅ **B14 — interleaved same-name fn clause groups silently shadowed the earlier group (parser.mly)** — commit `4cdc65de`. `group_fn_clauses` now rejects a fn name recurring in a non-adjacent group at the same level instead of leaving the first group as dead code.
- ✅ **B16 — `~H` sigil CSRF auto-injection referenced a free variable `conn` (desugar.ml)** — commit `f39a5c49`. Removed the unconditional CSRF-tag injection on `<form method=post/put/patch/delete>`, which broke every non-Bastion `~H` user (worse with bastion leaving the stdlib) — see `project_bastion_removal` context.
- ✅ **B7 — mutual-TCO back-edge dropped the Perceus dec-chain (llvm_emit.ml)** — commit `05d9e46b`. Added mutual-group twins of the self-TCO `ELet`/`ESeq` interception arms so the dec-chain emits before the back-edge instead of landing in a dead block (was an RC leak every loop iteration).
- ✅ **B8 — mutual-TCO loops had no reduction check, could starve the scheduler (llvm_emit.ml)** — commit `bc5b0e22`. `emit_reduction_check` now runs after the `mutual_loop` label, mirroring the self-TCO path.
- ✅ **B12 — `cur_type_defs` global left stale for every REPL/fragment emitter (llvm_emit.ml)** — commit `53c70a0e`, see full entry above (Task 8). Type defs are now a `ctx` field; the global is deleted.
- ✅ **B11 — REPL closure wrapper missed the uniform ptr-ABI fix (llvm_emit.ml)** — commit `b32d8569`, see full entry above (Task 9). The inline wrapper is gone; `emit_repl_fn_with_closure_slot` calls the canonical `clo_wrap_define`.

## Wave 2 — Task 1: interface-impl monomorphization fix (2026-07-02, `.superpowers/sdd/sortby-diagnosis.md`)

- ✅ **Historic "`List.sort_by` 100+ element compiled crash" formally closed — root cause was never sort_by.** Diagnosis (`.superpowers/sdd/sortby-diagnosis.md`, 95% confidence, evidence chain): compiled `println`/`show` of ANY generic container (`List`, `Option`, `Result`) whose `Show` goes through prelude's constrained impls (`impl Show(List(a)) when Show(a)`) was type-confused and had **never worked in compiled mode**, since the feature's introduction (`df3b1e44`, 2026-04-20). `lib/tir/mono.ml`'s three interface-impl-resolution enqueue sites (EApp direct-dispatch, EApp name-collision branch, ECallPtr twin) rewrote a call like `show(xs : List(Int))` to the mangled impl `Show$List.show`, but enqueued that impl with an **empty substitution** — so the impl body stayed generic, and its nested element-level `show(x : a)` call survived to `llvm_emit.ml` as an unresolved bare reference. `llvm_emit.ml`'s `unqualified_fns` dot-suffix fallback (built for legitimate cross-module unqualified refs like `base64_encode` → `Crypto.base64_encode`) then resolved the bare `show` to whatever `*.show`-suffixed impl was registered first — typically `Show$List.show` itself, applying the LIST impl to a raw element. Symptom varied by element type and impl-registration order (Int → SIGSEGV on a match-tag load against an erased-int treated as a heap pointer; String → non-exhaustive-match panic; Option → SIGSEGV/SIGBUS) — explaining the years of shifting "sort_by crash" symptom reports. sort_by itself is and was bit-for-bit correct compiled (1000-int checksum parity, 500-tuple stability parity, heap-capturing-comparator parity, all confirmed identical interpreter vs. compiled in the diagnosis). Fix (`lib/tir/mono.ml`): all three enqueue sites now compute a substitution from the resolved call's concrete argument types (`build_subst`, reusing the exact machinery the ordinary generic-fn EApp branch already used) and enqueue the impl specialized under it — mangling a further-specialized name (e.g. `Show$List.show` + `List(Int)` → `Show$List.show$List_Int`) when the substitution is non-empty, so the nested `show(x)` resolves through mono's normal rewrite-calls machinery to the concrete element impl. Recursion (`List(List(Int))` etc.) terminates via the existing worklist `done_set` dedup — verified explicitly: a 2-level and 3-level nested-list `println` both compile in ~2s with no runaway specialization loop. Defense-in-depth (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`): `unqualified_fns` population now EXCLUDES interface-impl-mangled names (detected as "contains `$` before the last `.`", e.g. `Show$List.show$List_Int` — ordinary qualified names like `Crypto.base64_encode` are unaffected since they never contain `$`), so a bare interface-method name recurring unresolved can never again be silently rebound to an arbitrary same-named impl; the ECallPtr catch-all fallback additionally `failwith`s by name (listing candidate impls) if it ever sees an unresolved bare name matching a registered impl's suffix, converting any future regression into a loud compile-time failure instead of a silent miscompile. Four RED/GREEN regression tests added in `test/test_codegen.ml` (`iface_impl_mono_codegen`): `println([1,2,3,4,5])` (pre-fix: SIGSEGV 139), `println(["a","b"])` (pre-fix: non-exhaustive-match panic, exit 1), `println([Some(42), None])` (pre-fix: SIGSEGV 139), `println([[1,2],[3]])` (pre-fix: SIGSEGV 139, also the mono-termination/recursion-guard check) — all assert compiled stdout == interpreter stdout AND exit 0. The original 150-element `List.sort_by` + `println(List.take(sorted, 10))` repro (`/tmp/sortby-kapitsa-baseline.march`) now produces byte-identical output in interpreter and compiled mode, exit 0 both — closing the saga end-to-end. **384 compiler / 227 eval / 341 codegen (+4 new, 337 pre-existing) / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march all pass, all exit 0.** RC benchmarks (compiled, `--opt 2`): `tree_transform` = `104857600`, `list_ops` = `333333666666`, `binary_trees` standard Benchmarks-Game output, `bench/mutual_recursion.march` (even/odd, 3-state machine, collatz) all correct — no regressions.

## Wave 2 — Task 3: W2.1 — LLVM IR validity gate (2026-07-02, `specs/plans/2026-07-02-wave2-testing-infra.md`)

- ✅ **Emitted LLVM IR is now verified for the whole native fixture corpus — new `test/test_ir_verify.ml`, wired into `run_codegen.exe`.** Previously nothing ever ran an LLVM verifier over emitted IR; an ill-typed emission (the `coerce` catch-all class, or any other emitter bug producing textually-plausible-but-type-incorrect LLVM assembly) only ever surfaced as an opaque `clang` parse/verify failure at `--compile` time, with no locatable `.ll:line:col` pointer at TEST time. **Tooling probe:** Xcode's Command Line Tools clang does NOT ship `opt`/`llvm-as` (`xcrun -f opt` fails) — the verifier had to come from Homebrew LLVM (`brew --prefix llvm`, not linked onto PATH to avoid shadowing Xcode's clang). `find_llvm_verifier_tool` (`test/test_helpers.ml`) probes, in order: `opt` on PATH, `opt` at the brew keg, `llvm-as` on PATH, `llvm-as` at the brew keg; `` `None `` is a legitimate, counted skip (`record_jit_skip`, same ledger as the W2.0 clang-absence skips) — never a silent pass. This machine has `opt` at `/opt/homebrew/opt/llvm/bin/opt` (Homebrew LLVM 22.1.1); the gate ran for real, not skipped. **RED:** a hand-crafted `.ll` with an `i64`/`double` type mismatch (`%r = add i64 %x, %f` where `%f` is `fadd double`) is asserted REJECTED by `verify_llvm_ir_file` (`opt -passes=verify -disable-output`, rc≠0 with a locatable `file.ll:line:col` error) — proven to actually matter by temporarily sabotaging the helper to always return `` `Ok `` and confirming the RED test then fails (restored clean afterward); a converse sanity test confirms well-typed hand-crafted IR is accepted, so the gate isn't just "reject everything." **Gate:** one aggregated Alcotest case (`test_native_corpus_ir_is_verifier_clean`) emits IR via `--emit-llvm` (bypasses the CAS artifact cache entirely, unlike plain `--compile`, so the gate always inspects freshly emitted text) for every `test/native/*.march` fixture and runs the verifier, collecting every failure before asserting (a full run names every bad fixture in one shot rather than stopping at the first). **Finding, disposition = scope exclusion, not a loud skip:** of the 43 `test/native/*.march` fixtures, the 6 `js_*` ones (`js_dom_available`, `js_extern_import`, `js_ffi_async`, `js_ffi_blocking_raises`, `js_ffi_cbacked`, `js_ffi_raises`) are compiled ONLY with `--target js` anywhere in `test/dune` (producing a `.mjs`; the JS backend never touches `Llvm_emit` at all — confirmed in `bin/main.ml`'s JS-target branch, which returns before the LLVM/clang path). Compiling them natively instead (no `--target js`, what a naive "gate everything in test/native/" would do) DOES produce a `.ll` that fails verification — the emitted `declare` for a JS-side extern keeps its raw JS-style name verbatim (e.g. `@node:path_dirname`, `@./foo.mjs_bar`), and `:`/`.`/`/` are invalid characters in an LLVM identifier — but this is a probe artifact, not a real bug: no existing test ever exercises these 6 fixtures as native IR, only as `.mjs`. `list_native_fixtures` (`test/test_ir_verify.ml`) therefore excludes any `js_`-prefixed fixture, mirroring actual `test/dune` usage rather than inventing a loud skip for a scenario the corpus never puts them in. **GREEN on the real corpus:** all 37 gated (non-`js_`) fixtures emit verifier-clean LLVM IR as of 2026-07-02 — zero emitter bugs found, no `specs/todos.md` entries needed for this task. **Design: new file, not a growth of `test_codegen.ml`** (already ~6400 lines) — a distinct corpus-scanning concern (walk a directory, shell out to an external tool, aggregate a failure list) vs. `test_codegen.ml`'s single-source-snippet IR assertions; wired into the EXISTING `run_codegen.exe` runner per the task brief (`Test_codegen.codegen_suites @ Test_ir_verify.suites`), not a 6th dune test executable, so the mandated five-runner invocation recipe is unchanged. **Review hardening (same commit):** (1) the `js_` exclusion is loud, not silent — an `at_exit` note names every excluded fixture in the terminal (same mechanism as the W2.0 skip ledger), plus a misclassification invariant: the gate FAILS if `test/dune` ever contains a `native_<fixture>` compile rule for an excluded `js_` fixture (`assert_excluded_are_js_target_only`). (2) A real temp-dir leak found and fixed: even `--emit-llvm` drops a `.march/cas/vc/` verdict-cache tree in its CWD, so the original flat remove+`rmdir` cleanup silently ENOTEMPTY'd on every fixture (~240 leaked dirs observed); replaced with `rm_rf_temp_dir` (`test/test_helpers.ml`) — recursive, lstat-based (symlinks removed as links, never followed), and SELF-CONFINING: refuses `""`/`"/"` outright and refuses (`Alcotest.failf`, deleting nothing) any existing path that is not a strict realpath-resolved descendant of the realpath-resolved system temp root (realpath on both sides neutralizes the macOS `/var`→`/private/var` `$TMPDIR` spelling mismatch); the unconfined walker is local to the function so it cannot be called unguarded. Confinement unit test (refuses `""`, `"/"`, and an existing outside-temp-root dir, with a sentinel file asserted to survive) proven load-bearing by sabotage (guard arm removed → test fails exit 1; restored byte-identical). **384 compiler / 227 eval / 346 codegen (+4 new: RED-rejects, valid-IR sanity, delete-confinement, corpus gate) / 790 stdlib (789 pass + 1 pre-existing documented skip from W2.0) / 53 stdlib_march all pass, all exit 0.**

## Wave 2 — Task 4: W2.2 — TIR golden-snapshot infrastructure (2026-07-02, `specs/plans/2026-07-02-wave2-testing-infra.md`)

- ✅ **TIR golden-snapshot tests: `test/test_snapshots.ml` + new `run_snapshots.exe` runner, 14-program corpus, post-lower and post-Perceus `.expected` goldens.** No TIR-level golden test existed before this task — every lowering/mono/defun/Perceus regression was only ever caught (or missed) indirectly via a program's runtime output changing. See the detailed writeup in `specs/progress.md`'s "Wave 2 Task 4" entry for the full design (printer choice, prelude-noise filtering, fresh-name-counter determinism, corpus list, RED/GREEN proof, 3x determinism check including a cold-`$HOME` rebuilt-binary run). **Human audit finding, filed as a new P0 above (not fixed — `lib/tir/perceus.ml`/`borrow.ml` were read-only for this task):** reading the `scrutinee_borrowed_conservatism` corpus program's post-Perceus dump surfaced a genuine double-`dec_rc` of the same scrutinee variable across sibling match arms, reproducible standalone as a compiled-only `RC underflow — aborting` (exit 134) crash — a real double-free, not just the documented "leak-not-crash" conservatism the corpus program was written to pin. Also noted (non-bug): the FBIP dead-binding-reuse corpus program never actually emits an `EReuse` in this pipeline slice — a join-point-closure allocation Lower always interposes before the reuse-eligible `EAlloc` defeats `try_fbip_sink`'s `ELet`-only sink logic, silently falling back to Free+Alloc (correct, just not reused) for essentially any ordinary match-and-reconstruct arm. **384 compiler / 227 eval / 346 codegen / 790 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshot (new 6th runner: 14 lower + 14 perceus + 1 determinism canary) all pass, all exit 0.**

## Wave 2 — final-review follow-up: guard-liveness tests + skip-ledger wording (2026-07-02)

- ✅ **Positive-control (liveness) tests for `fail_if_unresolved_iface_method` (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`) — the Wave 2 Task 1 defense-in-depth guard's `failwith` path is now exercised.** On a healthy compiler the guard never fires (mono.ml resolves every interface-method call), so nothing tested it: if `ctx.top_fns` naming or the guard's `$`-before-last-dot detection predicate drifted, the guard would rot silently. Three new tests in `test/test_codegen.ml` (`iface_impl_mono_codegen` suite) hand-build the exact regression signature as a raw `Tir.tir_module` — a bare `describe` call alongside a registered mangled impl `Pretty$Int.describe` — precisely BECAUSE the real frontend can no longer produce that state (that unreachability is what Task 1 fixed). Approach chosen: full `Llvm_emit.emit_module` over a synthetic-ctx unit test of the helper alone, so the `is_known_fn` gating at BOTH consumer call sites (the general EApp path and the ECallPtr no-var-slot catch-all) stays covered too; both positive tests assert the raised `Failure` names the unresolved symbol AND the candidate impl, and a negative control (registered `Pretty$Int.render`, non-matching suffix) pins the predicate against false positives by asserting emission succeeds and falls through to the pre-existing forward-declare.
- ✅ **Skip-ledger truthfulness nits (behaviorally trivial, all in `test/`):** (1) the W2.0 canary `test_setup_jit_runtime_gate_is_live`'s clang-absent path claimed in a comment to be a "counted skip" but never called `record_jit_skip` (it short-circuits before `setup_jit_runtime`) — now actually counted; (2) the `at_exit` ledger banner in `test/test_helpers.ml` said "clang-gated … (clang not found on PATH)" but the same ledger also carries the IR gate's LLVM-verifier-absence skips (`test_ir_verify.ml`) — header generalized to "[tool-skip ledger] … (required external tool not found)", each reason line names its tool; (3) four stale "skip: clang or runtime not available" comments in `test/test_codegen.ml` (plus the sibling block banner in `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml` and the `setup_jit_runtime` doc block) still described the pre-W2.0 contract — reworded: only clang absence skips (counted); runtime-source/link problems fail loudly. Verified the reworded banner + newly-counted canary render correctly under a simulated clang-absent run (`PATH=/bin`). **384 compiler / 230 eval / 349 codegen (+3 new) / 791 stdlib (790 pass + 1 documented mono-limit skip) / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshot — all six runners exit 0.**

## Wave 3 — Task 1: `Tir_names` — single home for cross-pass name contracts (2026-07-03, `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk1-refactors.md`)

- ✅ **New `lib/tir/tir_names.ml` centralizes every cross-pass magic-string/name-predicate contract flagged by the Wave 1 deep review (§1 root-cause 2, §7.1) as duplicated-by-convention across `lower.ml`/`defun.ml`/`borrow.ml`/`perceus.ml`/`llvm_emit.ml`/`js_emit.ml`/`dce.ml`.** Pure behavior-preserving move — every converted call site was verified byte-identical (diffed old-vs-new logic before converting; see `.superpowers/sdd/w3-task-1-report.md` for the full inventory table). Surface: `tuple_tag`/`is_tuple_tag` (`"$TupleN"`), `fv_field`/`is_fv_field`/`fv_field_index` (`"$fvN"`, shared by defun.ml's 1-based closure fields and lower.ml's 0-based tuple-destructure fields), `clo_struct_name`/`is_clo_struct` + `apply_fn_name`/`is_apply_fn` (closure struct + apply-wrapper naming), `clo_param_name` (`"$clo"`), `is_try_call` (`__try_call`/`__try_call_val`), `is_iface_mangled`/`iface_mangle` (the `$`-before-last-`.` predicate from Wave 2 Task 1), `default_arg_mangle`/`parse_default_arg` (`"base$N"`), actor codegen suffixes + the C-runtime-coupled `$d_`/`$e_`/`$f_` field-sort prefixes, bool-tag producer spellings (`synthetic_true_tag`/`synthetic_false_tag`/`bool_lit_tag` — the llvm_emit.ml decoder's capitalize-tolerant matching stays as-is per the plan), test/setup synthetic fn names, and the `"march_"` C-runtime-extern-prefix check.
  - **`is_apply_fn` diff verdict (the plan's flagged critical subtlety): BYTE-IDENTICAL, not diverged.** The perceus.ml and llvm_emit.ml copies used the exact same substring-scan algorithm and `"$apply$"` marker — unified into one predicate with no per-caller behavior split needed (contrast with the still-open Task 2 `needs_rc`/`borrow_eligible` divergence, which IS real and must NOT be unified). The four `"march_"`-prefix checks in llvm_emit.ml and the two `is_iface_mangled` copies were likewise byte-identical.
  - **Scope boundary:** `desugar.ml`'s default-arg-mangle producer was NOT converted — `march_tir` already depends on `march_desugar` transitively (lower.ml calls `March_desugar.Desugar.desugar_module`), so the reverse edge would cycle the build; only the lower.ml consumer (same library) was converted. Two same-file `lower.ml` `"$Tuple"`/`"$Record"`/`"$Unknown"` interface-impl-dispatch type-name-key literals (a different, non-duplicated convention) were left as-is — outside the plan's suggested surface.
  - **Proof of behavior preservation:** all four `specs/benchmarks.md` benchmarks compiled with `--compile --opt 2` produce byte-identical LLVM IR before/after (diffed against a clean build of the exact base commit in a sibling worktree). Zero TIR snapshot churn (29/29 `run_snapshots` green, no `.expected` diffs). New `"tir_names"` unit-test suite in `test/test_codegen.ml` (12 tests) covers every exported name/predicate including the `"List.map$Int"` negative case for `is_iface_mangled` and the `$d_`/`$e_`/`$f_` field-sort invariant. **384 compiler / 230 eval / 362 codegen (+12 new) / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots — all six runners exit 0.**

## Wave 3 — Task 2: `Rc_types` — canonical `needs_rc` + `borrow_eligible` with documented divergence (2026-07-03, `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk1-refactors.md`)

- ✅ **New `lib/tir/rc_types.ml` is the single canonical home for the two RC-relevance predicates that were previously two independently-maintained copies both named `needs_rc`** (perceus.ml's and borrow.ml's, the latter tagged "duplicated to avoid a cyclic module dependency"). `Rc_types.needs_rc` (Perceus's RC-op-emission question) and `Rc_types.borrow_eligible` (Borrow's inference-eligibility question) are byte-identical moves of the copies they replace; perceus.ml keeps a local `let needs_rc = Rc_types.needs_rc` alias (all ~30 use sites and the external `test_eval.ml` references unchanged — minimal diff ahead of Task 4's env-threading churn), borrow.ml's copy is deleted and its use sites call `Rc_types.borrow_eligible` directly.
  - **Divergence contract — WIDER than the plan documented, investigated and confirmed intentional before proceeding.** The plan (via the analysis doc) named one divergent pair: `TFn _`/bare `TVar _` (needs_rc true / borrow_eligible false — closure-FV ownership fix lineage `a705cc95`, `d2cf09e`, `fd520110`, `78e31ff7`). Fresh constructor-by-constructor diffing found the real divergence set is **four** constructor patterns: additionally `TTuple _`/`TRecord _` disagree in the OPPOSITE direction (needs_rc false / borrow_eligible true). Task initially reported BLOCKED per its stop rule; git-history investigation resolved it — the TTuple/TRecord fork was deliberately introduced by `0b52510d` (record-liveness multi-call RC underflow: borrow needs record/tuple params eligible so field-only readers infer cfg:borrowed) and independently re-confirmed by `390dff00` #4 (Toml `get_str` pair-list corruption: Perceus reconciles at the field level via `_borrowed_field_vars` because the aggregate itself is never RC-freed). The controller confirmed the analysis doc simply understated the contract (it predates full awareness of `0b52510d`). `rc_types.ml`'s module doc now records the full truth table over all 11 `Tir.ty` constructors, each divergent pair's WHY, its fix-history commits, and what breaks in each direction if either side is changed (Map.fold crash / Gate.cast UAF class vs the record-liveness underflow and Toml corruption class).
  - **Pinning test:** new `"rc_types"` suite in `test/test_codegen.ml` (2 tests): a 20-row table-driven truth-table test over representative types (including `TCon("Atom",[])` vs `TCon("Atom",[TInt])`, `TVar "_"` vs bare `TVar`, empty vs non-empty tuple/record) asserting BOTH predicates' answers row-by-row, plus an exactness test asserting the divergence set is precisely {`TFn _`, bare `TVar _`, `TTuple _`, `TRecord _`} and nothing else.
  - **Proof of behavior preservation:** all four `specs/benchmarks.md` benchmarks (`--emit-llvm --opt 2`) produce byte-identical LLVM IR vs the same clean-base-commit baseline Task 1 diffed against (0 diff lines each; transitively identical to the Task 1 commit since Task 1 proved base == Task 1). Compiled binaries produce the documented expected outputs. Zero TIR snapshot churn (29/29 `run_snapshots` green, post-perceus snapshots included, no `.expected` diffs). **384 compiler / 230 eval / 364 codegen (+2 new) / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots — all six runners exit 0.**

## Wave 3 — Task 3: `fn_kind` role flags on `Tir.fn_def` — retire name-sniffing (2026-07-03, `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk1-refactors.md`)

- ✅ **Added `Tir.fn_kind` (`FnNormal | FnLambda | FnJoinPoint | FnApply | FnFused`) as a mandatory field on `Tir.fn_def`, set honestly at all 21 synthesis sites in `lib/tir/*.ml`** (lower.ml ×15 covering parsed user/stdlib fns, `ELam`/`ELetFn` lambda-creation sites, the `compile_matrix` hoisted-fallback join point, actor handler/dispatch/spawn glue, module-level-`let`-as-zero-arg-fn, builtin Eq/Ord/Show/Hash iface shims, and `__march_test_N__`/`__march_setup__`/`__march_setup_all__`; defun.ml ×1 for the closure apply wrapper; fusion.ml ×3 for the map/filter/fold loop-fusion helpers). ADAPTED the plan's suggested kind list: dropped `FnTryThunk` (no synthesis site exists — `__try_call`/`__try_call_val` are typecheck/eval builtins, never lowered to a TIR `fn_def`; the thunk passed to them is an ordinary `FnLambda`), added `FnFused` (fusion.ml's generated loop helpers — a real synthesis site the plan's list missed; no consumer currently sniffs these names, so the kind exists purely for honest labeling, not yet load-bearing).
  - **Consumers converted with transitional flag-vs-sniff asserts (mandatory this chunk, removed in Chunk 2):** all three `Tir_names.is_apply_fn` call sites — `perceus.ml`'s post-call EDecRC/closure-consumes-`$clo` gating (via a new `_fn_kinds` module-level lookup table, mirroring the existing `_borrow_map`/`_type_defs` convention), `llvm_emit.ml`'s `emit_fn` (direct — the `fn_def` is already in scope), and `llvm_emit.ml`'s general `EApp` case (via a new `ctx.top_fn_kind` Hashtbl populated once from the top-level module's `tm_fns` at `emit_module`'s entry; deliberately NOT populated at the ~10 scattered REPL/JIT incremental pseudo-module registration sites — those callees have no table entry and the assert is skipped there, not treated as a mismatch). Zero assert firings across the full six-runner suite (1851 tests) — every synthesis site agreed with the pre-existing name-sniffing logic it replaces.
  - **Two named consumers left unconverted, by design, documented in place:** `borrow.ml`'s `is_clo_struct` checks a `Tir.ty` (closure-struct TCon name from an EAlloc), not a `fn_def` — there is no `fn_kind` to assert against without re-deriving the lambda's `uid` back out of the type name, which would be a new fragile re-derivation, not a fix. `borrow.ml`'s `is_try_call` checks a call-target name against the hardcoded `__try_call`/`__try_call_val` builtin names — since those are never TIR `fn_def`s, a `fn_kind` lookup by that name would always miss; this is builtin call-target dispatch, not synthesis-role classification (same shape as the `"task_spawn_steal"` check elsewhere).
  - **Printer caveat honored:** `pp.ml`'s `string_of_fn_def` and `tir.ml`'s `show_fn_def` write specific fields by name (not exhaustive record patterns) — neither needed an edit, neither prints `fn_kind`; snapshot output stayed byte-identical (0 `.expected` diffs).
  - **Critical finding, flagged not fixed:** `lib/cas/serialize.ml`'s `write_fn_def`/`write_letrec_fn`/`write_fn_sig` (feeds `impl_hash`/`sig_hash` — cross-binary RPC admission + the CAS artifact cache key) likewise write fields by name and so did NOT start hashing `fn_kind` — verified explicitly and documented with an in-code warning that `fn_kind` must NEVER be added there (it would silently invalidate every cached artifact and change RPC admission the day it was hashed — `fn_kind` is a same-binary compiler-internal role tag, not part of a function's externally observable identity).
  - **Test-file mechanical fallout:** adding a mandatory record field forced every `Tir.fn_def` literal across the repo to set it (`bench/bench_opt.ml`, `test/test_cas.ml`, `test/test_codegen.ml`, `test/test_compiler.ml`, `test/test_eval.ml`, `test/test_helpers.ml`, `test/test_hot_reload.ml`) or the build would not compile — OCaml has no default field values. This unavoidably included **`test/test_properties.ml`** (on the plan's "do not touch" list); applied the plan's own `bin/`-exception precedent ("except if a moved module forces a reference update — flag it") since there is no way to make one field mandatory-everywhere-except-one-file in OCaml.
  - **Proof of behavior preservation:** zero TIR snapshot churn (29/29 `run_snapshots` green). Same-machine A/B against a detached-HEAD `git worktree` built from `0cd6b627`: all four `specs/benchmarks.md` benchmarks produce identical outputs with timings within noise (tree_transform ~8.0s both; list_ops/binary_trees/mutual_recursion sub-second, both sides matching to within measurement noise). **384 compiler / 230 eval / 364 codegen / 791 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots — all six runners exit 0 (1851 tests total).**

## Wave 3 — Task 4: Perceus env-record threading — retire module-level mutable state (2026-07-03, `specs/plans/2026-07-03-wave3-chunk1-refactors.md`)

- ✅ **`lib/tir/perceus.ml`'s nine module-level mutable refs replaced by one immutable `env` record threaded as an explicit parameter through `insert_rc_expr` and every helper it calls** (`incrc_for`/`decrc_for`/`wrap_incrcs`/`find_inc_vars`/`scrutinee_shares_payload_storage`). In-place refactor only — no file split (that's Task 5). Ref inventory and classification (full table in `.superpowers/sdd/w3-task-4-report.md`):
  - **Module-scoped → env fields, set once per `perceus` run, read-only for the whole traversal:** `_borrow_map`, `_type_defs`, `_fn_kinds`, `_extern_names`.
  - **Function-scoped → env fields, set once per `insert_rc` call, constant across that function's traversal (including nested `ELetRec` closures, which reuse the ambient env exactly as the old refs were never reset for them):** `_current_fn_name`, `_closure_fvs`, `_actor_sent`.
  - **Subtree-scoped → env fields, updated via `{ env with field = ... }` on descent into `ELet` bindings / `ECase` branches** (the functional equivalent of the old hand-rolled `let prev = !r in r := x; ...; r := prev` dance — the "restore" is now implicit because the caller's own `env` binding is never mutated): `_borrowed_field_vars`, `_var_ctx`.
